A B O U T BHAKTIVEDÁNTA VIDYÁPÍTHA
O n 2 1 M a y 1969, H i s D i v i n e G r a c e A . C . B h a k t i v e d a n t a Stvami Prabhupáda a r r i v e d at
N e w V r i n d a b a n , W e s t V i r g i n i a , U . S . A , on his first visit to the m o u n t a i n dsratna. T h i s
dsrama      w a s the     first f a r m c o m m u n i t y of the          International Society            for    Krsna
Consciousness i n the W e s t . Srila Prabhupáda had numerous ideas and desires on h o w
to develop this c o m m u n i t y as a replica of Vrndávana i n I n d i a . H a v i n g left Vrndávana
in 1965 to spread the glories of L o r d K r s n a , Srila Prabhupáda n o w set f o r t h to manifest
Vrndávana - and the spirit of l o v i n g devotional service - i n all parts of the w o r l d .
"1 am so glad to learn that one gentleman is going to open an ashram in the
West Virginia, and he has secured a big tract ofland, 320 acres, and I wish
that this tract of land may be turnéd into New Vrindaban.                          You have New
York, New England, and so many "New" duplicates ofEuropean                                  countries
in the USA; why not import New Vrindaban in your                            country?"
(Letter to Hayagriva,          17 March       1968)
Srila Prabhupáda shared this v i s i o n for N e w V r i n d a b a n w i t h his disciples on various
occasions. I n his letters to H a y a g r i v a , Kirtanánanda and a f e w others between                           1968
and 1973, Prabhupáda emphasized several times on the various vitai aspects of f a r m
communities, namely -               C o w protection,         Sustainable A g r i c u l t u r e (producing o w n
food), Simple Village L i f e (Plain l i v i n g , H i g h t h i n k i n g - saving time for spiritual
advancement), a Placc of Pilgrimage (a replica of Vrndávana, w i t h all major temples
and kundas), a Placc of H i g h e r learning (Rüpánuga Vidyápitha and T h e i s t i c school)
and ultimately a placc for people to develop their love for K r s n a .
Focusing on the education aspect, Srila Prabhupáda w r o t e to A n i r u d d h a on 4 F e b r u a r y
1969, about his desire for a well-established system of education specifically dedicated
to sástric study or the study of the V a i s n a v a literatures. "...we                   can start a theological
school also, along with a boys primary school. Our theological school will regularly
teach our published            books,     such as Bhagavad-gita              As It Is,         Srimad-Bhagavatam,
Teachings of Lord Caitanya,               Nectar of Devotion,           Brahma Samhita, and Krishna.                  We
wish to hold examinations and award titles such as Bhakti-sastri,                              Bhakti-vaibhava,             and
Bhaktivedanta.             The whole program will continue for seven                       years..."
Desiring to implement this vision of Srila Prabhupáda, H i s Holiness Rádhánátha
S w a m i M a h á r á j a directed I S K C O N C h o w p a t t y to begin a f a r m community calied
G o v a r d h a n E c o V i l l a g e ( G E V ) , focusing o n the above-mentioned vitai aspects. Since
its inception i n 2 0 0 3 , G E V has made steady progress i n the areas of c o w protection
and organic f a r m i n g . I n 2 0 0 9 , G E V began its full-scale focus on the development of a
retreat center w h i c h is successfuUy r u n n i n g n o w . I n recent years, G o v a r d h a n E c o
Village replicated Vrndávana forest w i t h its seven m a i n temples, G o v a r d h a n a H i l l and
Y a m u n a R i v e r and gained local as w e l l as international popularity. T h e educational
aspects at G E V include - the activities of L N H O School for under-privileged r u r a l
children and the r u r a l outreach programs conducted i n over 30 villages around G E V .
I n 2012, Bhaktivedánta Vidyápitha w a s established at G E V as a n addition to I S K C O N
C h o w p a t t y ' s training p r o g r a m f o r the n e w l y joined m o n k s .

B h a k t i v e d á n t a V i d y á p í t h a is a t w o - y e a r , f u l l time study course at G o v a r d h a n E c o
Village. I t covers the systematic study of Srimad-Bhagavatam                                         and Sri          Caitanya-
caritámrta       translated and commented by Srila Prabhupáda. I n addition, commentaries
by acaryas like Srila Visvanátha C a k r a v a r t i T h á k u r a are incorporated i n the syllabus
to e n r i c h the understanding of the students. T h e course is based on a " 5 S " model to
help provide a wholesome, a l l - r o u n d development scheme for a sincere practitioner of
bhakti and the principles of t r u t h , t r a d i t i o n and t r a n s f o r m a t i o n to delineate the core
values behind the Bhaktivedánta Vidyápitha's vision and mission.
T R U T H . TRADITION . TRANSFORMATION
O u r philosophy and practice of it is governed by an important fact - w e cannot invent
G o d , a religion and the practices that support it. I t must come f r o m the actual source
itseif. T h u s , w e f o l l o w a t r i a d to q u a l i f y w h a t w e speak and practice - sastra - guru -
sadhu: one has to v e r i f y a l l spiritual matters according to the instructions of the
scriptures (sastra),              the spiritual master (guru) and the saintly persons (sadhu).                             This
I      Bhágavata Nul..lhn.^^^     ^ ^ ^ ^
ancient V e d i c measure ensures that instructions a n d practice should not differ at any
point f r o m the above-mentioned three a n d should a l w a y s reconcile. T h u s , w e are sure
that w e are f o l l o w i n g a genuine path. B y f o l l o w i n g such a hona fide path one's
character w i l l be transformed like that of a sadhu, a saintly person. T r u t h , T r a d i t i o n
and T r a n s f o r m a t i o n are symboiic equivalents for sastra, guru a n d sadhu respectively.
VISION
Inspiring and inculcating Vaisnava qualities and a mood of selfless service
in the follotvers of the International Society for Krsna Consciousness, by
creating a strong sástric foundation for their spiritual lives.
MISSION
Assisting the devotees in understanding the glorious contribution of Srila
Prabhupada's books, by systematically studying them and contemplating
practical ways for application of their teachings through an effective
educational framework as envisaged by the Gaudiya Vaisnava              parampard.
T H E "SS" M O D E L
T h e Svádhyáya aspect, involves five elements:
a.   Sravanam ( H e a r i n g ) - D a i l y classes are conducted progressing verse by verse
along w i t h discussions on the purports. Special seminars covering important
topics f o r character development such as leadership, V a i s n a v a dealings, etc. are
organized once a m o n t h .
b.   Mananam (Recollection) - D a i l y closed book tests are conducted based on the
previous day's coverage of the syllabus to ensure the students' regular study. I n
addition, a w e e k l y r e v i e w is organized to recap the w e e k ' s study.
c.   N i d i d h y á s a n a m (Contemplation) - Vidyápitha studies also include a channel
to express one's gathered learning i n the f o r m of open book essays and
assignments. Students are required to sift through the canto looking for
c o m m o n themes, or elaborating o n a notable phrase based on w h a t they have
studied a n d learnt.
d.   Pathanam (Recitation) - N o V e d i c institution is complete w i t h o u t recitation
of scriptural verses. A t Bhaktivedánta Vidyápitha, students daily recite select
verses f r o m the on-going section of the Bhagavatam.
e.   Kirtanam (Speaking) - T h e students are also trained i n communication skills
and i m p r o m p t u speech a n d thought, by asking them to present on various
topics or themes at regular intervals.
Sádhana involves the standard spiritual practices i n I S K C O N such as the m o r n i n g
program, w h i c h consists of attending mangala-árati,           cbanting japa and attending the
guru-püjá   a n d temple's m o r n i n g Srimad-Bhagavatam        class.
Sevá consists of v o l u n t a r y w o r k i n various departments at G E V , such as serving cows,
agricultural exposure, maintenance or facilitating the visiting guests.
Sanga is developed by encouraging students to study i n groups and w o r k i n teams for
projects. T h i s fosters team w o r k a n d strengthens this element.
Sadacara aspect involves the overall practice of the other four elements, grooming the
student's interpersonal skills, a n d encouraging one's dealings based on respect,
gratitude a n d interdependence f o r m u t u a l g r o w t h and sustenance. Special classes on
sadacara are also conducted f o r the students.
Bhaktivedánta Vidyápitha has been a dream come true for many devotees w h o desire to
study the Bhaktivedánta Purports. I n sight of this, a n online Bhagavatam                  course
(vidyapitha.in) has been made available, where devotees c a n study at their convenience.

A B O U T BHÁGAVATA SUBODHINÍ
w
T h e Srimad-Bhagavatam         is the ripened f r u i t of the desire tree of the V e d i c scriptures.
T h i s transcendental literature is nondifferent f r o m L o r d K r s n a , w h o has returned i n
the f o r m of this book (sri-krsna parivartitd)      to shed light on the misdirected civilization
of K a l i - y u g a . I t w a s compiled by the literary incarnation of G o d h e a d , Srila Vyásadeva
in the m a t u r i t y of his knowledge. T h e study of Bhdgavatam,             done i n line w i t h the
explanations of hona fide dcdryas i n the V a i s n a v a parampard, gives the sincere reader
an unparalleied understanding of the Absolute T r u t h , w h i c h is not possible by one's
o w n speculative efforts. I n fact it is L o r d K r s n a H i m s e l f w h o speaks through the
disciplic succession to enlighten the serious students of the              Bhdgavatam.
T h e Bhdgavatam has gained its prominence amongst modern philosophers, theologians
and   historians     due   to   the    unprecedented       efforts    of   H i s Divine Grace A . C .
Bhaktivedanta S w a m i Prabhupáda, the íounder-dcdrya               of the International Society for
K r s n a Consciousness     ( I S K C O N ) . H e personally undertook the herculean task of
translating and providing an E n g l i s h commentary for this voluminous masterpiece,
w h i c h w a s originally rendered i n the Sanskrit language. H e unveiled to the E n g l i s h -
speaking w o r l d the secrets of V e d i c culture that focused on perfecting the humán f o r m
of life.
H a v i n g hegun his society i n the B o w e r y , a r u n - d o w n s u h u r h of N e w Y o r k C i t y ,
Bhaktivedanta S w a m i delivered daily classes on the Bhdgavatam,                  w h e r e most of his
audiences were the heatniks of the counter culture - fondly k n o w n as the hippies. T h e y
took to the illuminating radiance of the Bhdgavatam and w h i l e hasking i n its splendour,
the hippies hegan to t u r n a n e w leaf. Society witnessed their change i n character,
appearance and behaviour. H a v i n g once been a t h o r n i n the social body of the N e w
Y o r k e r s , the hippies, n o w became fuU-fledged contributing members of society. T h e i r
incredihle transformation became the first proof of the Bhdgavatam's immense potency
- something unseen h y the study of any other literature.
About Bhagavata Subodhini
BHAGAVATAM COMPARED TO THE LORD'S B O D Y
T h e Srímad-Bhagavatam        is a classic that is able to c h u r n out endless lessons on life,
personal inspirations, philosophical knowledge and most importantly the meaning of
life. Its glories are limitless a n d it presents the tatest knowledge about the identity,
nature and character of the Supreme C r e a t o r , the Supreme L o r d Sri K r s n a . T h e Srimad-
Bhagavatam      has   twelve     cantos,   which      represent the    different    limhs    of   the
transcendental body of the Supreme L o r d .
pddau yadiyau prathama-dvitiyau              trtiya-türyau kathitau      yad-ürü
nábhis tathá pancama eva sastho            bhujdntarath dor-yugalam         tathdnyau
kanthas tu rdjan navamo yadiyo            mukhdravindam       dasamah     praphullam
ekddaso yasya laldta-pattam            siro 'pi tu dvddasa eva bhdti

"The First and Second Cantos of the Bhagavatam are Sri Krsna's lotus feet. The
Canto is H i s chest. The Seventh and Eighth Cantos are His arms. The Ninth
Canto is H i s throat. The Tenth Canto is H i s beautiful lotus-face. The Eleventh
Canto is His forehead. The Twelfth Canto is His head. I bow down to that Lord,
the ocean of mercy whose colour is like that of a tamdla tree and who appears
in this world for the welfare of all. I worship H i m as the bridge for crossing the
unfathomable ocean of matériái existence. The Bhdgavatam has appeared as H i s
very Self." [Padma Purdna]
SRÍLA P R A B H U P A D A ' S EMPHASIS O N S Y S T E M A T I C S T U D Y
Srila Prabhupáda compared Bhdgavatam               study w i t h taking darsana of the Deity of
L o r d K r s n a , w h i c h is done gradually f r o m H i s lotus feet to the lotus face. H e
emphasizes a step-hy-step study of the Bhdgavatam                   i n his preface to       Srimad-
Bhdgavatam:     "Srimad-Bhdgavatam         begins ívith the definition ofthe ultimate source. It
is a bona fide commentary       on the Veddnta-sütra      by the same author, Srila      Vydsadeva,
and gradually it develops into nine cantos up to the highest state of God                realization.
The only qualification one needs to study this great book of transcendental                knowledge
is to proceed step by step cautiously and not jump forward                 haphazardly as with an
ordinary book. It should be gone through chapter by chapter, one after another.                   The
reading matter is so arranged with its originál Sanskrit text, its English           transliteration,

T H E ÁCÁRYAS' UNPARALLELED CONTRIBUTION
. T h e V e d i c E n c y c l o p e d i a , Srimad-Bhdgavatam    is like a n ocean. Its expansive noumenal
and phenomenal suhject matters w i t h deep spiritual and philosophical insights, make
it a text for hoth researchers and relishers alike. Srila Prabhupáda mentions i n his
purport to the first verse of Bhdgavatam:              "...there is no doubt about the authority ofthe
Bhdgavatam. Within the past five hundred years, many erudite scholars and dcdryas like
Jiva Gosvami, Sandtana Gosvdmi, Visvandtha Cakravarti,                    Vallabhacarya, and many other
distinguished scholars even after the time of Lord Caitanya made elaborate commentaries
on the Bhdgavatam. And the serious student would do well to attempt to go through them
to better relish the transcendental messages."
M o s t verses of the Bhdgavatam are given descriptive commentaries h y various G a u d i y a
V a i s n a v a dcdryas.    Amongst them is the esteemed               commentator        Srila    Visvanátha
Cakravarti       Thákura.         H i s explanations     are    devotional, relishing, logical, highly
regarded i n the           scholarly circles of Bhdgavatam             and are often         quoted      i n the
Bhaktivedánta purports. H e is w e l l - k n o w n for giving connections between verses and
chapters, presenting the conversations a n d prayers i n an interesting dialogue manner
and giving positive interpretations for the hlasphemous speech of demons i n their
various     encounters       with     the   Lord   and H i s devotees.       U n s u r p r i s i n g l y therefore,
C a k r a v a r t i Thákura's commentaries have been used for m a n y of the supplementary
links and notes provided i n Bhdgavata Subodhini for enhanced c l a r i t y .
B H Á G A V A T A S U B O D H I N Í aims at helping the reader to relish studying or reading
through the Srimad-Bhdgavatam.                L v e n regular readers of the Bhdgavatam might f i n d
themselves sometimes lost i n the multitudes of conversations and f l a s h h a c k s , struggling
to   keep t r a c k of      the    various personalities        and episodes     or    understanding           the
complicated technical aspects of creation, u n i v e r s a l f o r m , time, etc. Because of this,
readers tend to read selective sections or pastimes f r o m the Bhdgavatam rather than
endeavouring for a systematic approach. H a v i n g t a k e n into consideration a l l these
academic anomalies that inhihit one f r o m f u l l y delighting i n the splendour of the
Bhdgavatam,         the Bhaktivedánta         Vidyápitha designed a systematic f r a m e w o r k to
present the Bhdgavatam i n a lucid manner - Bhdgavata                     Subodhini.
Bhágavata Subodhim simplifies the apparent complexity in
understanding the Bhagavatam, by uncovering and integrating the
explanations of Sríla Prabhupáda and the previous ácáryas, thus
catering for the taste and the needs of avid readers, scholars and even
newcomers to the Srimad-Bhágavatam.
E v e n one w h o hasn't heard of the Bhdgavatam, c a n read through Bhdgavata                                   Subodhini
and develop a n interest in the originál manuscript. Nevertheless, this book is not meant
to be a replacement for a detailed study of the Bhdgavatam itseif, but a complement
for   devotees,        students      and      curious readers.           It   is therefore         advised that           one
simultaneously refer to the Bhdgavatam                         w h i l e going through this book and study
Bhdgavatam w i t h the assistance of Bhdgavata Subodhini. W e hope that this attempt to
sérve the V a i s n a v a c o m m u n i t y w i l l be pleasing to a l l V a i s n a v a s . T h e f o l l o w i n g is a brief
description of the various features of Bhdgavata                         Subodhim.
OVERVIEW
T h i s is the m a i n section of Bhdgavata Subodhini and gives a n o v e r v i e w of the F i r s t and
Second C a n t o s , w h i l e the other sections sérve as a u x i l i a r y contents that enhance one's
appreciation of the suhject matter. T h e f o l l o w i n g are the various features in this
section:
•     C a n t o A t a G l a n c é : O v e r v i e w of the entire canto, presented i n a f l o w c h a r t .
•     C h a p t e r O v e r v i e w c h a r t : Provides a b i r d ' s eye v i e w of the major sections and
subsections of a chapter.
•     S e c t i o n : A group of verses that f a l i under the same heading.
•     S u b s e c t i o n : A sub-group of verses w i t h i n a section.
•     V e r s e s y n o p s i s : A brief s u m m a r y of a verse that highlights its import (a condensed
version of its translation by Srila Prabhupáda and Srila Visvanátha C a k r a v a r t i
T h á k u r a ) . A l l the verses are duly numbered and important k e y w o r d s of the
Sanskrit verse are given i n brackets.
•     L i n k : T h e connection between t w o verses, sections or subsections, w h i c h is often
taken f r o m Srila Visvanátha C a k r a v a r t i T h á k ú r a ' s Sdrdrtha-darsini                 commentary.
•     S a n s k r i t V e r s e : A most vitai verse of the chapter presented i n the f l o w , w h i c h is
worth memorizing.
•     T a b l e : A list of items under a section.
•     F l o w C h a r t : C i v e s a bird's eye v i e w of a sequence of events or the f l o w of a set
of prayers.
•     N o t e : A vitai comment or explanation on a verse or section, m a i n l y related to the
f l o w of the chapter, t a k e n f r o m the purports of Srila Prabhupáda and/or Srila
Visvanátha C a k r a v a r t i T h á k ü r a for enhancing the understanding of those verses
or   sections,      their context          and philosophical depth.                  [However, when               Srila
Visvanátha C a k r a v a r t i T h á k ü r a gives a unique and different perspective other
t h a n the one given by Srila Prabhupáda, his name is mentioned i n that note.]

SPECIAL V E R S E S
. T h i s section contains selected verses f r o m a canto that c a r r y a deep a n d e x t r a o r d i n a r y
significance i n terms of philosophy (siddhánta)               and emotion (bhdva), w h i c h w o u l d

accessing them. W e ' v e provided a list of a f e w citations by the various                        dcdryas
(wherever possible), focusing o n the numerous w a y s one c a n understand a single verse.
These different angles of vision, i n relishing the verses of the Bhdgavatam, are the glory
of the V a i s n a v a dcdryas w h o i n this w a y e x h i b i t their astounding realizations and
scholarly flare. T h e list of citations is not exhaustive and surely a l l verses of the
Bhdgavatam         are equally important. Nevertheless, f r o m these f e w pearls of special
verses, one can get a glimpse of the vast treasures hidden i n the ocean of                               the

THEMATIC COMPILATIONS
T h i s section consists of various prominent themes that are present i n a specific canto
of the Srimad-Bhdgavatam.           E a c h compilation contains a comprehensive list of verses
on a particular theme. T h e objective of this section is to m a k e the reader appreciate the
cross-sectional prominence of a specific theme revealed i n various chapters of a canto,
i n addition to inspiring one to study the Bhdgavatam                     f r o m various angles. These
compilations are based on the analysis a n d research of m a n y devotees as part of their
study i n the Bhaktivedánta Vidyápitha course.
APPENDIX

section. U n l i k e the T h e m a t i c C o m p i l a t i o n s , these materials are more direct i n nature
w i t h little or no analysis, such as, names of L o r d K r s n a and analogies. A q u i c k o v e r v i e w
of the m a i n characters that appear throughout the canto(s) are also presented. T h i s
section aims at providing a n interesting, q u i c k , yet extensive reference guide.

glories of the name, fame, forms, pastimes, etc., of the unlimited Supreme Lord is a
different creation, full of transcendental words directed toward bringing about a
revolution in the impious lives of this world's misdirected civilization. Such
transcendental literatures, even though imperfectly composed, are heard, sung and

T h e f i r s t t w o cantos of the Bhdgavatam are considered the Supreme L o r d ' s lotus feet
and f o r m a perfect prelude to the rest of the Bhdgavatam by introducing the reader to
its key philosophical concepts and prominent characters. These cantos also establish
bhakti-yoga as the topmost spiritual path and L o r d K r s n a as the Supreme Personality
of Godhead. W o n d e r f u l prayers h y devotees and the L o r d ' s reciprocation w i t h them,
the h i r t h of Pariksit M a h á r á j a and his activities, a n d the history of Bhdgavatam                      are
w o n d e r f u l l y described. F u r t h e r , there are descriptions of the process of creation, the
virdt-rüpa,    the catuh-sloki Bhdgavatam a n d f i n a l l y the ten topics of the                      Bhdgavatam.
T h e T h i r d a n d the F o u r t h C a n t o s correspond to the Supreme L o r d ' s thighs and pre-
dominantly discuss the topics of sárga and visarga through the conversation of V i d u r a
and M a i t r e y a . T h e T h i r d C a n t o also describes time, embryology, f a m i l y life, Sánkhya,
bhakti-yoga, astdnga-yoga, jndna-yoga, karma, culture, the spiritual w o r l d , the matériái
w o r l d and hell. W e further read the pastimes of L o r d V a r á b a , the description of
V a i k u n t h a l o k a , the household life of K a r d a m a M u n i a n d Devahüti, and the teachings
of L o r d K a p i l a i n this canto. T h e F o u r t h C a n t o is very r i c h i n its content. I t unveils the
stories of admired personalities like L o r d Siva, Suniti, D h r u v a , Nárada, A h g a , P r t h u and
the Pracetás, w h o all showed e x t r a o r d i n a r y examples of devotional service. One can
also learn about the unfavourable attitudes i n bhakti f r o m the mistakes of characters
like D a k s a , S u r u c i , V e n a , I n d r a and Prácinabarhi.                   T h e positive   transformation
undergone by m a n y of these personalities, m a i n l y due the intervention of devotees, add
to the glory of bhakti. L u r t h e r m o r e , the prayers of pure devotees like D h r u v a , P r t h u and
the Pracetás disclose the foremost aspiration of a devotee - to hear krsna-kathd i n the
association of devotees. B y rejecting heavenly enjoyment and impersonal liberation,
w h i c h are the results of the karma and jhdna paths respectively, such prayers once again
establish the superiority of bhakti-yoga. I n the T h i r d and F o u r t h Cantos, M a i t r e y a
described the f a m i l y lines of Sváyambhuva M a n u ' s descendants: Devahüti ( 3 . 2 I - 4 . I ) ,
Aküti (4.1), Prasüti (4.1-7) and Uttánapáda                      (4.8-4.31).
T h e F i f t h C a n t o corresponds to the lotus navel of the Supreme L o r d . H e r e , Sukadeva
Cosvámi continues on the topic of visarga a n d describes the dynasty of P r i y a v r a t a
(5.1-15), the other son of M a n u . T h e latter half of this canto (5.16-26) discusses
sthánam,     the positioning of various planetary systems w i t h i n the structure of the
universe for the inhabitation of various living entities.
The dynasty of Priyavrata
P r i y a v r a t a exemplified the spirit of sacrificing one's personal desire to be obedient to
the orders of superiors. A l t h o u g h he w a n t e d to be i n the renounced order, upon L o r d
B r a h m a ' s request, he took charge of his father's k i n g d o m w i t h o u t any grudges. H e
ruled i n a n exceptional w a y , dividing Bhü-mandala into seven islands and seven oceans,
appointing his sons their rulers, and f i n a l l y retiring. H e showed h o w devotees can
achieve great things, because they are surrendered to the a l l - p o w e r f u l L o r d . (5.1)
A g n í d h r a , although a son of a pure devotee, w a s b o r n w h e n P r i y a v r a t a w a s lusty, and
thus developed a desire to enter P i t r l o k a . Agnídhra later m a r r i e d Pürvacitti, a celestial
damsel and begot nine sons headed by Nábhi. A f t e r her departure, Agnídhra, whose
lust w a s unsatisfied, attained the same planet as her, after death (5.2).
Nábhi desired a son as good as the Supreme L o r d . A l t h o u g h this is considered a
matériái desire, the Supreme L o r d as R s a b h a actually appeared as his son, and Nábhi
gradually attained perfection through vdtsalya prema for H i m . T h e prayers of the
priests of Nábhi reveal the L o r d ' s m e r c i f u l attitűdé t o w a r d s H i s devotees and the
devotees' aspiration to a l w a y s remember the L o r d (5.3).
Lord R s a b h a d e v a ' s unparalleled qualities made even I n d r a envious. H i s life as a
brahmacdri, grhastha and ruler w e r e e x e m p l a r y and H i s progeny of one hundred sons
showed that people are categorized according to their qualities and w o r k , and not by
birth (5.4). T b e L o r d ' s p o w e r f u l teachings on topics like transmigration, liberation and
bhakti f o r m one of the most vitai sections of the Bhdgavatam (5.5). H i s activities as an
avadhüta    and rejection of mystic p o w e r s , give great lessons on the nature of the m i n d .
H i s disappearance i n the forest fire is meant to teach yogis h o w to give up their bodies
(5.6).
Bharata, the eldest son of L o r d R s a b h a , w a s a topmost devotee. H e ruled his citizens
w i t h affection a n d performed sacrifices w i t h exemplary consciousness, m a k i n g the
oblations a pure offering to the various limbs of L o r d Vásudeva. H i s retirement to
Pulahás'rama showed his detachment                  and his w o r s h i p of the L o r d , even up to
controlling his symptoms of ecstasy, showed his dedication i n sddhana. B h a r a t a , due
to his misplaced compassion for a deer, got distracted f r o m his spiritual practices, and
thus had to enter the body of a deer. R e a l compassion is to uplift one f r o m matériái
!
Bhágavata Subodhini
consciousness to spiritual consciousness, and cannot be directed t o w a r d s a n animál.
T h i s cautions a l l the practitioners of spiritual life that distractions c a n come at any
stage and that one needs to be extremely cautions on this path. B u t on another levél,
Bharata's becoming a deer w a s the L o r d ' s plan to intensify his devotion. T h e proof is
that he remembered his past life even as a deer and attained a hráhmana                            b i r t h in his
next life as J a d a B h a r a t a (5.7-8).
J a d a B h a r a t a , though internally conscious of the Supreme L o r d , behaved externally
like a dull m a n . People mistreated h i m , and once, a h a n d of dacoits even tried to
sacrifice h i m to the goddess Káli. B u t she protected h i m , being unable to tolerate such
a vaisnava-aparadha           ( 5 . 9 ) . A n o t h e r time, he w a s f o r c e f u l l y engaged to c a r r y the
palanquin of a k i n g . T h i s k i n g , Rahügana, dealt very h a r s h l y w i t h J a d a B h a r a t a but
later appreciated his w i s e w o r d s on the nature of the m i n d , transmigration, liberation
and devotional service. Rahugana's regret for offending J a d a B h a r a t a cautions one of
the gravity of vaisnava-aparadha.             J a d a Bharata's allegorical description of the "forest
of matériái enjoyment" graphically depicts the entanglement of the conditioned soul i n
this matériái existence (5.10-14).
Sukadeva Gosvámi then described the further descendants of P r i y a v r a t a ' s dynasty,
including prominent kings like S u m a t i , G a y a a n d P r a t i h a (5.15).
The geography of the universe
H e a r i n g about the seven islands (dvipas) created by the chariot wheels of P r i y a v r a t a
(in 5.1), Pariksit inquired about their dimensions and characteristics. Sukadeva
Gosvámi then described the structure of Bhü-mandala beginning w i t h Jambüdvipa, its
nine varsas (tracts of land) and its various m o u n t a i n ranges.
I n the middle of Ilávrta-varsa stands the magnificent golden m o u n t a i n S u m e r u , on top
of w h i c h are the abodes of Brahmá and the eight Lokapálas. T h e divine R i v e r Ganges
descends f r o m the top of S u m e r u and divides into f o u r paths, f l o w i n g i n four
directions, to meet the salt w a t e r ocean surrounding Jambüdvipa (5.16-17). T h e prayers
offered by the residents of the nine varsas of Jambüdvipa to their respective deities give
w o n d e r f u l insights into the glories of the Supreme L o r d , the dependence of devotees
on H i m , and the process of bhakti (5.17-19). A f t e r describing the details of Jambüdvipa,
Sukadeva Gosvámi continued to describe Bhü-mandala's remaining s i x dvipas,                                   the
oceans separating them, their varsas, rulers, inhabitants, rivers, m o u n t a i n s , dimensions
and other characteristics. T h e n the "golden l a n d " and the Lokáloka m o u n t a i n rangé
that surrounds these seven dvipas are described ( 5 . 2 0 ) .
About The Fiftb and Sixth Cantos
A f t e r completing the borizontal structure of the universe, Sukadeva Gosvamí described
its verticai structure, i n c l u d i n g the movements of the s u n , the orbits of the planets and
the altitudes at w h i c h v a r i o u s planetary systems are located (e.g. Ráhu, the s u n , the
polestar) (5.21-23). T h e n the l o w e r planetary systems ( A t a l a , V i t a l a , Sutala, T a l a t a l a ,
M a h á t a l a , Rasátala and Pátála), the position and glories of L o r d A n a n t a w h o holds the
universe are described (5.24-25). A n d the sufferings of the sinful living entities i n
various types of bellish planets are described i n 5.26. T h i s part of the F i f t h C a n t o
elaborately presents the details of the universe, hearing and explaining w h i c h , one's
f a i t h and devotion w i l l gradually increase a n d one w i l l understand the topics of the
Supreme L o r d , w h i c h are d i f f i c u l t to understand.
T h e S i x t h C a n t o of the Bhdgavatam presents several interesting episodes centered on
the theme of posana,          or protection offered by the Supreme L o r d to H i s devotees,
although they sometimes transgress the l a w s of dharma. H e a r i n g about the hellish
planets in 5.26, compassionate Pariksit inquired f r o m Sukadeva Gosvámi h o w the living
entities m a y be saved f r o m entering such hellish conditions. Sukadeva Gosvámi, to test
his disciple, first presented karma-kdnda                and jndna-kdnda          as solutions, but seeing
Pariksit not satisfied, presented bhakti-yoga as the ultimate protection f r o m a l l sins. T o
substantiate this, he told the story of Ajámila.
Ajámila
T h e instructive episode of Ajámila comprehensively presents the glories of the H o l y
N a m e through the talks of the Visnudütas and Y a m a r á j a . Ajámila m a r r i e d a prostitute
and committed m a n y s i n f u l activities, but because he chanted " N á r á y a n a " (the name
of the L o r d and the name of Ajámila's son) at the time of death, the Visnudütas
immediately arrived there and saved h i m f r o m the ropes of the Yamadütas. T h u s he
was given a second chance to perfect his life. H i s subsequent regrets and resolutions
teach h o w to be determined i n devotional service. T h e L o r d ' s protection for one w h o
chants H i s name even unconsciously, or indicating someone else, is unparalleled. T h e
L o r d delivers them, to speak nothing of those w h o chant H i s name attentively and have
virtuous behaviour. Y a m a r á j a f i n a l l y instructed the Yamadütas not to approach
devotees, for they are protected by the L o r d (6.1-3).
Daksa - Nárada
U p o n Paríksit's request Sukadeva Gosvámi resumed the topic of visarga. H e described
the austerities of D a k s a and the H a r h s a - g u h y a prayers that he offered to the L o r d .
D a k s a begot 11,000 sons, a l l of w h o m w e r e made into renunciants by Nárada. T h e
furious D a k s a blasphemed and cursed N á r a d a , w h o patiently w a i t e d for his anger to
subside so that he could preach bhakti to h i m . Y e t D a k s a ' s anger didn't w a n e . I n fact
Daksa's offences were a continuation of his vaisnava-aparadha                      to L o r d Siva ( i n C a n t o
.   F o u r ) . D a k s a later begot 60 daughters whose progeny populated this universe i n the
s i x t h manvantara.   A d i t i , a daughter of D a k s a m a r r i e d K a s y a p a and begot Tvastá w h o
married Racaná, the daughter of the D a i t y a s . T h e i r son Visvarüpa w a s accepted as
guru by the demigods. (6.4-6)
Indra - Visvarüpa
I n d r a , püffed up at his matériái opulences, once failed to respect his guru Brhaspati
w h e n he entered the heavenly assembly. Pride is so dangerous that as a result the
demigods were defeated by the inferior demons. F o r d Brahmá chastised them and
advised them to accept Visvarüpa as their guru.                       Visvarüpa instructed I n d r a the
Náráyana-kavaca by w h i c h the demigods regained their opulence (6.7-8).
Vrtrasura - Indra
Visvarüpa secretly offers oblations to the demons. Being insecure, I n d r a beheaded h i m ,
although he w a s a brdhmana and his guru, w h o helped h i m regain his k i n g d o m . Tvastá
then performed a sacrifice to get a son w h o could k i l l I n d r a . T h u s Vrtrásura appeared
f o r m the s a c r i f i c i a l fire. T h e demigods offered knowledgeable prayers to the L o r d
seeking protection f r o m Vrtrásura. U p o n the L o r d ' s order, they approach D a d h i c i w h o
selflessly sacrificed his body for the m a k i n g of the thunderbolt w e a p o n (vajra)                   with
w h i c h I n d r a w a s to fight Vrtrásura (6.9-10).
T h e narration of the battle between I n d r a and Vrtrásura depicts the glories of Vrtrásura
in various w a y s . H i s courage and prowess w a s evident i n his fight w i t h the demigods and
I n d r a . H i s f a i t h i n the w o r d s of the L o r d , that he w o u l d be killed by the thunderbolt, is
revealed i n his hold instructions to I n d r a to not be morose but to fight. H i s dutiful nature
and detachment is understood f r o m his fighting w i t h his mouth even w h e n both his arms
were cut. H i s exemplary prayers show his exalted devotion to L o r d Sankarsana and his
glorious aspiration to become a servant of the servant of the L o r d (6.11-12). I n d r a then
had to suffer the sinful reaction for k i l l i n g Vrtrásura. O n l y after 1000 years w a s he
reinstated i n his position as the K i n g of heaven (6.13).
Citraketu - Nárada - Aiigirá
Surprised at the devotional qualities of Vrtrásura, w h o w a s after all a démon, Pariksit
w a n t e d to k n o w more. Sukadeva Gosvámi therefore narrated Vrtrásura's previous life
as K i n g C i t r a k e t u . C i t r a k e t u could not heget a son and w a s therefore i n great anxiety.
Aiigirá R s i performed a sacrifice for h i m by w h i c h he f i n a l l y obtained a son through
About The Fifth and Sixth Cantos
his principal queen, K r t a d y u t i . T h u s C i t r a k e t u neglected the other queens. But the
other queens became envious and poisoned the cbild (6.14). Seeing the appropriate time,
the sages Nárada and Angirá arrived there and enlightened C i t r a k e t u . These talks
wonderfully delineate the insubstantiality of matériái relationships i n this w o r l d .
C i t r a k e t u received a mantra f r o m Nárada by w h i c h he attained the darsana of L o r d
Sankarsana i n one week. H e offered heartfelt prayers that glorify                      Bhágavata-dharma
(6.15-16).
Another time, C i t r a k e t u h a d laughed at L o r d Siva for sitting i n an assembly of sages
w i t h Párvati on his lap. H i s c r i t i c i s m w a s out of concern that a c o m m o n m a n might
misunderstand Siva. Y e t a tinge of pride made h i m boldly criticize Siva. M o t h e r Párvati
then chastised and cursed C i t r a k e t u to become a démon. C i t r a k e t u then h u m b l y bowed
to Párvati, accepted the curse, explained the temporary nature of dualities i n this w o r l d ,
and sought forgiveness. L a t e r , Siva explained to Párvati the exalted nature of devotees
w h o c a n see any situation w i t h equanimity, focusing only on devotional service. T h a t
exalted C i t r a k e t u later became Vrtrásura w i t h the L o r d ' s blessing that he w o u l d r e t u r n
back to C o d h e a d just w i t h i n one life (6.17).
Dítí - Indra
Sukadeva Cosvámi resumed the description of the descendants of the daughters of
D a k s a f r o m 6.6. D i t i ' s sons were killed by L o r d V i s n u to help I n d r a . T h u s she served
K a s y a p a w i t h a desire to have a son w h o w o u l d k i l l I n d r a . K a s y a p a lamented upon
discovering her desire and instructed her i n the w o r s h i p of L o r d V i s n u (tbe puthsavana
v o w ) . I n d r a began serving her only to f i n d f a u l t i n her execution of the v o w . W h e n she
made a mistake, he entered her w o m b and cut her embryo into 4 9 pieces. T h o s e pieces
became tbe M a r u t s . Devotional service brought about a t r a n s f o r m a t i o n in the hearts
of both D i t i and I n d r a w h o desired h a r m for one another earlier. T h e y both gave up
their envy and I n d r a accepted the M a r u t s as his brothers and f o l l o w e r s .
I n this w a y , the S i x t h C a n t o describes several illuminating activities of the devotees of
the L o r d , w b o are protected by H i m despite their faults. T h i s volume of                 Bhágavata
Subodhim presents the contents of the L i f t h and S i x t h C a n t o s i n a simplified manner,
highlighting the most c r u c i a i elements           and giving connections            between various
episodes. T h e geography of the universe i n the L i f t h C a n t o has been carefuiiy presented
w i t h diagrams and tables to assist the reader's understanding. I t should be noted
however that these figures are not e x a c t l y to scale and somé elements are positioned i n
a p p r o x i m a t i o n to their actual position, since not a l l the details are a l w a y s given i n the
Bhdgavatam.

6. A b s o r p t i o n : P r i y a v r a t a sought the lotus feet of N a r a d a M u n i and thus attained

F a t h e r ' s request: H i s father Sváyambhuva M a n u then asked h i m to take charge of
r u l i n g the w o r l d for he possessed a l l the good qualities for r u l i n g .
R e l u c t a n c e : A l t h o u g h such a n order could not be refused, P r i y a v r a t a d i d not welcome
it. Being absorbed i n bhakti-yoga and engaging a l l his senses i n the L o r d ' s service, he
considered that it m a y divert h i m f r o m devotional service.
Note: Uttánapáda's descendants up to the Pracetás had ruled the kingdom of the earth.
But now there was no king, for their son Daksa (in his second hirth) took to performing
austerities. Sváyambhuva Manu, seeing no alternative, wanted to hring Priyavrata back
from the forest and make him the king. Thus Priyavrata and his descendants ruled until
the fifth manvantara. A t the beginning of the sixth manvantara, Daksa gave up his
austerities and became the king.

10. B r a h m a ' s c o m p a s s i o n a t e s m i l e : N á r a d a , M a n u and P r i y a v r a t a presented offerings
and praised h i m , according to V e d i c etiquette, i n highly qualified language. T h e n L o r d
Brahmá, smiling and looking at P r i y a v r a t a w i t h compassion, spoke.
Note A : Brahma's compassion: " 1 have interrupted his bhakti, jnana and vairágya,
but even in household life, may his bhakti increase!"
Note B : Brahma's smile: " T o refuse the kingdom is your stubbornness. I will make
you rule the kingdom. That is my stubbornness. I will see whose stubbornness will
prevail." (A joking competition between grandson and grandfather.)

29. P o w e r : P r i y a v r a t a ruled the universe for eleven arbuda (11 x 100,000,000) years
and conquered his enemies h y the mere sound of his h o w d r a w n hy t w o strong arms.
E n j o y m e n t : H e greatly loved his w i f e B a r h i s m a t i . B y her feminine behaviour of
dressing herself, w a l k i n g , s m i l i n g , and glancing about, she increased his joy. T h u s he
appeared lost i n the feminine conduct of his w i f e , hut actually he w a s a great soul.
30. P r i y a v r a t a ' s c h a r i o t f o l l o w i n g the s u n : P r i y a v r a t a once became dissatisfied w i t h
movements of the sun-god a r o u n d M o u n t S u m e r u , as he lit up half the surface of the
earth w h i l e the other half remained d a r k . P r i y a v r a t a foUowed the orbit of the sun-god
on a brilliant chariot and thus fulfilled his desire of m a k i n g night into day. H e could
p e r f o r m such w o n d e r f u l activities hy dint of his w o r s h i p of the L o r d .
Note: By the power of yoga, Priyavrata had two bodies, one as the king and one as the
sun. Priyavrata followed the sun, not to compete with the sun, but to give happiness to
the inhabitants of earth. Because of this arrangement, in months like Jyesthya (hot
months) the sun made by Priyavrata was cooler than the moon, and in cool months like
Márgasirsa (cold months), the sun made by Priyavrata was warmer than the other sun
at dawn and dusk.

created seven islands and seven oceans on Bhü-mandala. H e gave these islands to his
sons to rule. (Refer to the tahle i n 5.20 o n p. 125)

1 - 3 : QUALITIES OF L O R D RSABHADEVA                         t^^^^^^^m
1. Q u a l i t i e s : L o r d Rsabhadeva distinctly manifested (abhivyajyamdna)                   the symptoms
of the Supreme L o r d (bhagavat-laksanam)                     w h e n H e w a s born. D a y by day H i s
qualities, p o w e r s , detachment, sense control and equal vision increased. Due to this,
everyone w a n t e d H i m to rule the earth.
2. N á b h i n a m e d H i m R s a b h a , the best of humán beings, seeing H i s good qualities: a
well-built body w i t h a l l the symptoms of the G o d h e a d , prowess, strength, beauty,
name, fame, influence and enthusiasm.
3. I n d r a ' s e n v y : Becoming envious of R s a b h a d e v a , p o w e r f u l I n d r a stopped the r a i n i n
Bhárata-varsa. U n d e r s t a nding this, R s a b h a d e v a , the master of yoga profusely poured
water upon Ajanábha, H i s o w n place, through yogamdyd.
Note: Bhárata-varsa was formerly known as Ajanábha-varsa because it was ruled by
King Nábhi and his son, Rsabhadeva. After Bharata Mahárája's rule, it became
celebrated as Bhárata-varsa.
ÁBHI'S VÁTSALYA-RASA AND
4. N á b h i ' s a f f e c t i o n f o r h i s s o n : N á b h i w a s a l w a y s blissful, having attained his desire
for a perfect son, w h o m he considered as the Supreme L o r d and addressed i n a faltering
voice. T h i s transcendental illusion w a s brought about by yogamdyd                              [mdyd-vilasita-
matih). T h u s he affectionately raised the L o r d w h o accepted the w a y s of an ordinary
humán         {grhita-naraloka-sadharmam).
5. N á b h i e n t h r o n e d R s a b h a d e v a because: (/) he w a s popular amongst the citizens and
ministers, (ii) he w o u l d give protection to the people i n terms of the V e d i c religious
system (samaya-setu-raksdydm).               T o do this he entrusted H i m into the hands of learned
brdhmanas.
N á b h i a t t a i n e d V a i k u n t h a : T h e n N á b h i and M e r u d e v i w e n t to Badarikásrama. T h e r e
the k i n g w o r s h i p p e d N a r a - N á r á y a n a i n f u l l samddhi, and i n due course of time w a s
elevated to V a i k u n t h a     (tan-mahimdnam).
6-7: Verses by old sages to glorify Nábhi

Note A : Mahatmas are of two types - impersonalists (worshippers of Brahman) and
devotees (worshippers of Bhagaván).
•     A n impersonalist sees no differences (sama-cittdh) and is peaceful (prasdntd).
•     A devotee has a sincere heart (sama-cittdh) and his intelligence is fixed in the
Lord (prasdntd).
Both are devoid of anger (vimanyavah), are friendly to all (suhrdah) and do not see
faults in others (sddhavah).

becoming an associate of the Lord, by either serving an impersonalist or a devotee
respectively.

4. Sense gratification leads to perpetuai transmigration
W h e n a person considers sense gratification the
nunam pramattah kurute vikarma
aim of life, he certainly becomes mad and
yad indriya-pritaya        áprnoti
engages in all kinds of sinful activities.
1 do not consider such actions befitting, for they
na sadhu manye yata átmano 'yam                      lead to another temporary body, w h i c h gives
asann api klesada ása dehah                    suffering to the jiva although he actually has no
connection w i t h it.
L i n k : If sin leads to bondage then shouldn't                one do pious         activities?
Is. Both sin and piety lead to bondage. Therefore inquire into the self.
One is defeated and subjected to miseries arising
parábhavas tavad abodha-játo
from ignorance.
as long as one does not inquire about the
yávan na jijnásata          átma-tattvam
spiritual values of life.
A s long as fruitive activities continue (pious or
yávat kriyás tavad idam mano vai
sinful) the mind remains absorbed in action and
karmdtmakath yena sarira-bandhah
the bondage to repeated bodies results.
Note: Therefore even pious actions lead to sathsára and suffering. But all karmas, be
they pious or sinful, can be destroyed by jhána ( B G 4.37).

1 - 5 : NATURE O F THE M I N D
L i n k : Hearing that Rsabhadeva did not welcome mystic powers, Pariksit            inquires.
1. Why did Rsabhadeva neglect mystic powers? F o r atmaramas, w h o have attained
knowledge by practice of bhakti-yoga and w h o have burned up the seeds of karma, the
powers of mystic yoga arise automatically and do not cause distress.
Sukadeva Gosvamí describes the nature of the mind
2. Never trust the mind: Y o u have spoken correctly, but advanced souls do not put
faith i n the m i n d just as a hunter does not put f a i t h i n his captured animals.
Note: The mind takes on a new condition at every moment. Just as a cunning man
behaves nicely, but then robs and kills a trusting man, the mind shows itseif as pure
and fixed in hearing and meditation, but given an opportunity, it suddenly becomes
absorbed in sinful objects and takes away discriminating knowledge.
3. The restless mind can cheat at any moment
na kurydt karhicit sakhyam
Never make friends w i t h the unsteady m i n d .
manasi hy anavasthite
because by trusting it even L o r d Siva was agitated
yad-visrambhac cirac cirnam
(by Mohinl-mürti) and the long-standing austerity of
caskanda tapa aisvaram
Saubhari M u n i was quickly destroyed.
4. The mind Is like an unchaste wife
nityarh daddti kdmasya          T h e mind gives facility to enemies like lust, anger and
cchidrarh tam anu ye 'rayah        greed. W h e n given a chance
yoginah krta-maitrasya          by a yogi w h o trusts it.
it is just like an unchaste wife whose paramours can
patyur jayeva purhscali
come back to k i l l the husband.
5. Mind -     tfi^^^^au^^^nTeriem^r
kamo manyur mado lobhah              Lust, anger, pride, greed, lamentation, illusion,
soka-moha-bhayddayah               fear, etc.

karma-bandhas ca yan-mulah
constitute bondage to karma.
svíkurydt ko nu tad budhah           W h a t learned man w o u l d put faith in the mind?
Note: Just as the paramours of an unchaste wife will kill her husband, the anarthas
caused by the restless mind will kill the yogi (entangling him in karma).

13. Bhárata-varsa is the most pious amongst all lands on this earth, because its inhabitants are
accustomed ro glorifying the auspicious activities of Lord's incarnations, such as Rsabha.

who executed dharma for destroying the results of karma.
15. W h i c h yogi can compare with Rsabhadeva and follow His example, even with his mind?
He rejected all yogic perfections, which other yogis hanker to attain.
16. Phala-sruti: Rsabhadeva is the master of all Vedic knowledge, humán beings, demigods,
cows and brdhmanas. Hearing and describing His activities -
i.      vanquish sinful activities [samasta duscarita     abhiharanam)
ii.     create complete auspiciousness (parama mahd mangala)
ni.     make one attain pure bhakti (vdsudeve eka-antatah bhaktih)

bhakti for relief of the sufferings of samsdra.           T h e y enjoy supreme bliss, and reject even
liberation, w h i c h comes of its o w n a c c o r d . J u s t by being servants of the L o r d , they
have attained everything desirable.

2. Five sons: J u s t as the falsé ego creates the five subtle sense objects, B h a r a t a begot
five sons i n his w i f e - S u m a t i , Rástrabhrta, Sudarsana, A v a r a n a and Dhümraketu.
3. B h á r a t a - v a r s a : F o r m e r l y this place w a s k n o w n as Ajanábha-varsa, but since
Bharata's reign, it has become k n o w n as Bhárata-varsa.
4. P e r f e c t r u l e : H e w a s a learned k i n g like his father and grandfather. Engaging i n his
o w n duties, he also engaged the citizens i n their duties w i t h great affection.
5. S a c r i f i c e s : B h a r a t a w o r s h i p p e d the Supreme L o r d , w h o is the f o r m of sacrifices
(yajna-kratu-rüpam),         by f a i t h f u l l y p e r f o r m i n g various sacrifices - agni-hotra,   darsa.
Bhágavata     Subodh^grí|:^;|)|^^^
pürnamasa,      cdturmasya, pasu-yajha and soma-yajha - either completely or partially,
and according to the regulations of              cdturhotra.
L i n k : One should continue Vedic rituals or prescribed duties until one - (i) becomes
detached from sense gratification              and    (ii) develops faith for hearing and               chanting
(11.20.9). Then how could Bharata,             who was qualified for pure devotion,                having deep
faith in the Lord's topics and no matériái desires, perform sacrifices meant for                         karmis?
6. C o n s c i o u s n e s s i n s a c r i f i c e s : B h a r a t a w a s free of all matériái contamination, such
as attachment, lust and greed, because he considered the -
•    oblations offered to different demigods by the priests into the s a c r i f i c i a l fire as
actually offerings unto the different limbs of L o r d Vásudeva.
•    r e s u l t of the sacrifices, namely dharma, as a n offering to L o r d Vásudeva, the -
o    ultimate performer of all actions              (sdksdt-kartari)
o    recipient of a l l sacrifices      (yajha-puruse)
o    controller of all demigods w h o are revealed through V e d i c mantras.
Note: Bharata was not the enjoyer of the sacrifice - H e did not have any desires for
the results or benedictions from the demigods. He performed all the yajnas for the
satisfaction of Krsna who is the enjoyer of sacrifice (yajna-bhug).
Bharata was not the performer of the sacrifice - He did it through representatives
like the adhvaryu priest, and he did not have faith in those actions. Lord Vásudeva is
the independent doer and antarydmi, inspiring everyone to act. He is the performer of
the sacrifice (yajna-krt) and the sacrifice itseif (yajha).
Bharata's bhakti was pure - Worshipping each demigod independently destroys the
ananya or exclusive aspect of bhakti, but worshipping them as limbs of Lord does not.
His bhakti was not covered by karma as he constantly engaged in hearing, etc.
7. T h u s B h a r a t a ' s b h a k t i i n c r e a s e d day by day unto Vásudeva, w h o manifests as the
Paramátmá as w e l l as the impersonal B r a h m a n . T h e L o r d ' s body is decorated w i t h the
Srivatsa, the K a u s t u b h a jewel and a g a r l a n d , and H e holds a conch, disc, club and
lotus. Devotees like Nárada a l w a y s t h i n k of H i m w i t h i n their hearts.
8. B h a r a t a r e t i r e s : A f t e r ruling for 10,000,000 years, he retired f r o m f a m i l y life and
divided the w e a l t h he had received f r o m his forefathers among his sons and w e n t to
Pulahásrama. T h e sdlagrdma-silds are obtainable there.
9. A t P u l a h á s r a m a , L o r d H a r i is visible even n o w i n the f o r m desired by the devotee
(icchd-rüpena),      out of transcendental affection for H i s devotee.

10. W i t h its sálagráma-silás the Gandaki (Cakra-nadi), the best of rivers, purifies all

11. Worship: There he lived alone and worshipped the Lord with varions flowers and

•   his heart melted.
•   he gradnally lost all attachment for regnlative dnties.

-7: BHARATA BRINGS AN INFANT D E E R T O H I S A S R A M A
1 . Bharata on the bank of G a n d a k i : One day, after finishing his morning duties,
Maharaja Bharata sat down on the bank of the River Gandaki and began chanting his
mantra, beginning with orhkdra.
2-4. A thirsty doe came there. While she was drinking water, she heard the frightful
roar of a lion and became very afraid and suddenly leaped across the river though she
had not yet quenched her thirst.
5-6. T h e doe's miscarriage and death: The doe was pregnant and when she jumped
out of fear, her baby feli from her womb iuto the flowiug waters. Being separated from
its flock and distressed by its miscarriage, the black doe feli down in a cave and died.
7. Bharata's compassion: The great King Bharata, seeing the small fawn floating down
the river, felt great compassion and lifted it from the waves. Knowing it to be
motherless, he brought it to his dsrama.
Note: Our compassion should raise others from the material platform to the spiritual.
Otherwise it cau spoil our spiritual advaucemeut.
8-15:   BHARATA'S ATTACHMENT T O THE DEER
8. Neglects sádhana: Bharata began to raise, maintain, protect and caress the deer as
his own child. I n this way, he gave up his rules and regulations, and gradually neglected
to worship the Lord. After a few days he forgót everything about his spiritual
advancement.
9-10: Philosophy of misplaced compassion
9a. Thoughts about the deer: It is helpless and alone, having lost its relatives by the
force of time. It has taken my shelter, with fuU faith and dependence.
9h-10: Thoughts about himself: " I should not be envious and neglect the deer, but
selflessly raise, protect, gratify and fondle it. Renounced persons, who are cultured and
saintly, should neglect their important interests to protect the surrendered."
Note: Bharata forgót that it was impossible for him to elevate a deer [auimal] to
spiritual cousciousuess.
11-12. Attachment and affection: Due to attachment [anusangah] for the deer, he lay
down, walked about, bathed and even ate with it. Thus his heart became bound to the
deer in affection [sneha). Fearing that ferocious animals might kill the deer, Bharata
I         Bhágavata Subodhiní
took it with him whenever he went to the forest to collect kusa grass, flowers, wood,
leaves, fruits, roots and water.
13. Attraction: I n the forest he would carry the deer on his shoulders, sometimes keep
it on his lap or, when sleeping, on his chest. Thus he felt great bliss in petting it.
14. Distraction: While he was worshipping the Lord, he would at intervals get up and
see where the deer was. Seeing it, he would be satisfied, and bless it saying, " M y dear
calf, may you be happy in all respects."
15. Agitation, anxiety and lamentation in separation: Sometimes not seeing the deer,
his mind would be very agitated, like a miser who has lost his wealth. When the deer
was gone, he would be filled with anxiety and would lament in separation. Thus he
would become illusioned and speak as follows:
1 6 - 2 5 : B H A R A T A ' S L A M E N T A T I O N IN S E P A R A T I O N   Wt^^^m
16. " I am negligent but the deer is noble: Like a cunning hunter, my mind is always
filled with cheating propensities and cruelty. Perhaps this deer, having faith in me, will
not consider my offence, and like a gentle person return out of its kindness.
17-18. Shall I see it again? Alas, is it possible that 1 shall again see this animál protected
by the Lord? Shall 1 again see him wandering in the garden eating soft grass? Perhaps
the deer has been eaten by a wolf, a pack of boars, or a tiger.
19. Not yet returned: Sunrise is the beginning of all auspicious things, but it is not so
for me. The sun is setting now, yet the poor animál who trusted me has not returned.
2 0 - 2 5 : Symptoms of Moha - Illusion
2 0 . Prince: When will this prince among deer return and drive away my grief by his
pleasant personal activities? 1 certainly must have no pious assets, otherwise the deer
would have returned by now. (This is moha, illusion)
21. Feigned meditation: Seeing me feigning meditation with closed eyes, it would
circumambulate me out of anger in love (prema-samrambhena) and fearfully touch
me with its soft horns, which felt like drops of water.
22. Son of a saint: When I chastised the deer by pushing it away for contaminating
the sacrificial ingredients, it would immediately become fearful and sít down
motionlessly, just like the son of a saintly person.
L i n k : After speaking like a madman, Bharata goes outside and sees the footprints of the
deer on the ground.
5.8 The Character of Bharata Maharaja         65
23. T h e deer's footprints are small, beautiful, auspicious and soft. They must -
•   indicate the superiority of the earth's austerities over mine.
•   show the path to regain my lost wealth.
•   have made this land suitable for executing sacrifices.
L i n k : Seeing the dark marks on the rising moon that resembled a deer, he said -
24. Has the compassionate moon given the deer (mrga-balakam) shelter, knowing
that it has strayed from home and has become motherless (mrta-mdtaram), to protect
it from the fearful attacks of a lion (mrga-pati-bhayát)}
25. Forest fire of separation: The deer was like my submissive son and now my heart,
which is like the land lily, burns with the feverish flames of the forest fire of separation.
Seeing me so distressed, the moon is certainly splashing its cool, shining nectar upon
me like a friend.
Note: According to Áyur-veda, splashing gargled water on one who has a high fever
will subside the fever.
26-31:    B H A R A T A T A K E S B I R T H AS D E E R IN N E X T L I F E
26. Bharata feli down due to past k a r m a : I n this way Bharata was overwhelmed by
an uncontrollable desire which manifested in the form of the deer. H o w but by the
fruitive results to his past deeds (svárabdha-karmand)   could he have fallen down from
mystic yoga, austerity and worship of the Lord, after renouncing his family to pursue
spiritual life?
Insurmountable death, which is compared to a venomous snake entering a mouse
hole, approached him.
Note A : Two types of prárabdha-karma -
•   Auspicious prárabdha-karma - occurs by the will of the Lord to increase the
devotee's longing for devotion to the Lord and to intensify his bhakti. This
occurs in devotees at the stage of bháva.
•   Inauspicious prdrabdha-karma - consists of the results of actions from
previous lives which manifest later as absorption in material life.
Note B: Why Bharata fel! down? Bharata purposefully became over-addicted to the
deer and neglected his spiritual advancement. To immediately rectify his mistake, he
was given punishment (a deer body) for a short time. This was just to increase his desire
for mature devotional service. The proof is -
I     Bhágavata Subodhiní
•   Bharata remembered his past even in the deer's body
•   He was anxious to get out of his deer body
•   It intensified his bhakti
•   He received a brahmana body in his next life
27. A t the time of death, seeing that the deer was lamenting by his side like his own
son and absorbed in thinking of it, Bharata left his body. Thus he acquired the body of
a deer in his next life, as if due to his past karma. However, he did not forget the
incidents of his previous life.
28-29. Lamentation: Due to his rigid devotional service in his past life, he could
understand the cause of his deer birth and thus lamented, "What misfortune! I gave
up my sons, wife and home, became self-controlled and engaged constantly in hearing,
thinking, chanting, worshipping and remembering Lord Vásudeva. But out of
foolishness, I became attached to a young deer and feli down."
30. Detachment: By constant repentance he became completely detached and left his
mother deer at Kálanjara Mountain, his birthplace. H e again went to the forest of
Sálagráma and to the dsrama of Pulastya and Pulaha, the place of the Lord.
31. H e gave up his deer body: He was now very careful not to fali victim to bad
association. Without disclosing his past to anyone, he remained there eating dry leaves,
grass and bushes. Taking Paramátmá as his companion (dtma-sahacarah), he waited
for death. Bathing in that boly place, he finally gave up that body.

L i n k : Bharata, the son of Lord Rsabha was born in a rich ksatriya family, but due to
his wilful negligence of spiritual duties and excessive attachment       to an   insignificant
deer, he did not attain success in one life and was obliged to take birth as deer. But due
to his strong position as a devotee, he was gifted with remembrance of his past life and
became repentant, always thinking of Krsna. Thus be was born in a good brahmana
family.

1-3: JADA BHARATA'S BIRTH A N D A V O I D A N C E O F B A D A S S O C I A T I O N
1-2. Jada Bharata's father: There was a brahmana in the line of Angira, who was
endowed with self-control, austerity, Vedic study, renunciation, satisfaction, tolerance,
gentieness, knowledge, nonenviousness and satisfaction in self-realization.
H i s progeny:
•    Brahmana + T ' wife        9 sons as qualified as himself
•    Brahmana + 2""^ wife        twins: a female and a male (Bharata Maharaja)
After giving up the body of a deer, Bharata took birth as the son of this brahmana.
3. Bharata's internál thinking: Bharata remembered his previous lives by the Lord's
mercy. He was afraid and cautious of the association of his nondevotee relatives and
friends (sva-jana-sangát).  He feared obstructions to his progress       (pratighátam
ásankamánah)    and always thought of the Lord's lotus feet and chanted H i s glories,
which save one from the bondage of fruitive action (karma-bandha vidhvarhsana).
Bharata's external behaviour: So that others would not talk to him he manifested
himself to the public as a madman - dull, blind and deaf.
4. H i s father's affection: The brahmana was always affectionate to his son, and seeing
him unfit for the grhastha-ásrama,     performed his sarhskáras only up to the end of
brahmacári life. Although his son was unwilling, he instructed him in cleanliness and
dcamana, thinking that the son should be taught by the father.
Note: Jada Bharata was completely absorbed in devotional service within, therefore it
was not necessary for him to execute the regnlative principles of fruitive activity.
5. Bharata acted like a fool before his father. He performed actions in reverse order,
to stop his father from instructing further. Still his father tried to teach him the Gayatri
mantra during the spring and summer, but was unsuccessful.
6. He taught brahmacári activities - cleanliness (sauca), Vedic study (adhyayana),
vows [vrata), rules (niyama), service to guru and fire (sacrifice) - to the unwilling
Bharata, but failed in all his attempts.
7. T h e bráhmana's death: Without fulfilling his desire, the attached and forgetful
brdhmana met with inevitahle death, that appeared by the influence of vigilant time.
Thereafter, his younger wife, entrusting her twin children to the elder wife, voluntarily
died with her husband and departed for Patiloka.

1 . Jada Bharata forced to carry the palanquin: King Rahügana, the ruler of Sindhu
and Sauvira was on his way to Kapilásrama. His palanquin carriers (who were engaged
forcibly) needed an extra beatet. They saw the great devotee [mahdnubdvah) Jada
Bharata, who was young and strong. Thus they forcibly engaged him to carry the
palanquin, although he was not fit for such a work.
2. E r r a t i c movements: While waiking, Jada Bharata, being compassionate to the ants,
checked every three feet, not to step on them. Due to this the palanquin was shaking
and the agitated king asked the reason.
3-4. Expianation: The palanquin carriers became afraid of punishment and spoke,
"We are not negligent and are discharging your orders properiy. But this new man does
not waik swiftiy and therefore we are unable to carry the palanquin with h i m . "
5-6. Rahügana's criticism and jada Bharata's sílence -
Rahügana was knowiedgeabie from serving eideriy sages [updsita vrddhah). Yet his
mind was covered by the mode of passión and he became angry due to his innate nature
as a king. Thus he spoke angriiy and sarcasticaiiy as follows:
•   6a: Y o u must be fatigued from carrying the palanquin alone, for so long and
for so far.
•   6b: Y o u must be oid and unheaithy, without strong iimbs.
•   6c: Are your feiiow carriers not cooperating with you?
Jada Bharata's response: His spirituai power was covered Üke fire covered by ashes.
He was unaffected, because he had no bodily conception of " I and mine", having
realized his spirituai identity. Without saying anything, he continued carrying the
palanquin.
7. When Rahügana noticed his palanquin still shaking, he spoke angriiy -
•    7a: Are you dead despite the life within your body?
•    7b: Don't you know that I am your master? (And you're disregarding me).
•    7c: Y o u are mad. For this disobedience I shaii punish you (so that you wiii
come to your senses).
8. Rahügana was - under the bodily conception of being a king (nara-deva-
abhimdnam), infiuenced by passión and ignorance, mad (madena), and considering
himself learned (pandita-mdninam). Unaware of the conduct of great yogis, he
chastised Jada Bharata.
Jada Bharata was - a powerfui devotee (bhagavdn), the dear abode of the Lord
[bhagavat-pnya-niketam),   seif-realized (brahma-bhüta),   free from pride [vigata-
smayah) and a friend of aii living entities (sarva-bhüta-suhrt-dtmd). Therefore he
smiied and spoke as follows.

9a. I am a soul detached from the body: " Y o u r words are actuaiiy true, not sarcastic.
The load, path of travel and destination beiong to the body and not to me, for I am a
spirit soul. Being detached from the body, I am not fatigued carrying the palanquin."
L i n k 6b   9b-10: Reply to the comment that he were not strong and stout.
9b-10. Bodily designations do not apply to the soul: "Fatness, thinness, bodily and
mentái distress, thirst, hunger, fear, disagreement, desires for material happiness, oid
age, sieep, attachment for material possessions, anger, lamentation, illusion and bodily
identification are aii transformations of the material covering of the spirit soul. These
things affect a person absorbed in bodily conception, but I am free from aii such
conceptions."

L i n k : After hearing Rahügana's   questions in 5.10, Jada Bharata replies as follows.
1 . T h e wise don't speak on material subjects: Though inexperienced (akovida), you
speak like an experienced man (kovida-vddavdddn). A n experienced person discerns
the truth [tattva avamarsena) and doesn't speak about mundane social behaviours
(vyavahdram) like the relationship between master and servant.
Note: In 5.10.22 Rahügana gave the example of a pot heated by fire in which rice is
being cooked in milk. In this example there is a connection between the pot and the
fire. But the liberated átma is a spiritual partiele and does not touch the material senses.
Fatigue of the body does not mean fatigue of the átma. Only a conditioned jiva, covered
by the material body, experiences fatigue due to fatigue in the body.
2 . Even veda-vádis generally don't discuss tattva. People interested in material
activities are inclined toward material sacrifices expounded in the Yedas. For such
people pure discussions on the Absoiute Truth generally do not arise.
3. T h e Vedas are insufficient: For one who realizes that material happiness is
insignificant like a dream, the Vedas, though an excellent source, become insufficient
means for understanding the highest truth.
L i n k : W/ty do people engage in material happiness despite being enlightened by devotees?
4-8:   T H E M I N D IS T H E C A U S E O F M A T E R I A L   ENTANGLEME
4. T h e mind expands through the senses. The mind, contaminated by the three
modes, is like an uncontrolled elephant (nirankusam). It expands pious and impious
activities through the knowledge senses (cetobhir) and action senses (dkütibhir). It
keeps one in the material world to suffer or enjoy.

7. Samsára is caused by the mind. The mind makes the jiva wander through different
species of life in different conditions. Thus the mind is the cause of bodily appearance,
bondage and liberation.

9-11: FUNCTIONS OF THE MIND

11. Unlimited transformations of the mind: The physical elements [dravya), nature
[svabhdva), culture [dsaya), destiny [karma) and time [kdla) are all material causes.
They agitate the functions of the mind which transform into hundreds, thousands and
millions. They become unlimited, not by themselves or by their combined strength, but
by the power of the Paramátmá [ksetrajha).
Note: Both Paramátmá and jiva are ksetrajna. 5.11.11 and 13 mention Paramátmá.
5.11.6 describes the conditioned jiva, and 5.11.12 describes the liberated jiva.
1 2 - 1 4 : A L I B E R A T E D S O U L IS A B S O R B E D IN T H E S U P R E M E L O R D
12. T h e liberated jiva (ksetrajna) can see the transformations of the mind, which are:
• made of mdyd
• existing since time immemorial
• sometimes manifest (in their wakeful and dreaming states)
• sometimes disappear (during deep sleep or trance).

14         A l i pervading: Just as the air enters and controls all moving and stationary

1 5 - 1 7 : DELIVERANCE O F C O N D I T I O N E D SOULS F R O M ILLUSION
15. H o w long does the jiva wander i n samsára? Until he: (i) becomes free of material
contamination (ii) conquers the six enemies (sat-sapatnah) and {iii) becomes seif-
realized through spiritual knowledge.
16. T h e mind is the sowing ground of all tribulations in the material world. It is the
covering on the soul (dtma-lingam) and the cause of lamentation, illusion, disease,
attachment, greed and enmity. As long as it has possessiveness over sense objects it
must accept the miserable conditions of samsdra.
17. Conquering the mind: The uncontrolled mind is the greatest enemy of the living
entity. Given a chance, it will become powerfui and cover the constitutional position
of the jiva. Conquer this mind by:
i.        Neglect {upeksayd)
ii.        Attentiveness (apramattah)
iii.        The weapon of service to the lotus feet of guru and of the Lord (guror hares carana
updsana astrah).
Note: The easy weapon to conquer the mind is neglect. One should disobey the orders
of the mind expertly, abide by the orders of the guru and engage in the Lord's service.

1 . " I offer my respects unto you. Y o u have an eternal form like the Lord's {kdrana-
vigrahdya) and you remove all contradiction in the sdstras            [svarüpa-tucchikrta-
vigrahdyá). Disguised as a dvija-bandhu, you are hiding your transcendental position
{nigüdha-nitydnubhavdya).
2. M y condition:
• Body is filled with dirty things (kudeha)
• Vision has been bitten by the serpent of pride                    {mdndhi-vidasta-drsteh)
Y o u r words are:
•      Cooling waters (hima-ambhah) for one scorched by the beat
•      Proper medicine (agadam) for one suffering from fever
•      Nectar (amrtam)
Note: In case the example of medicine were unsatisfactory (medicine does not always
cure) another example is given: nectar.
3. M y mind is inquisitive {kautühala-cetasa) so piease explain this adhydtma-yoga in
a simpler way so that I may understand. I shall ask my other doubts later.
4. M y mind is bewildered. Y o u said that the results of actions seen in this world
{drsyamdnam kriyd-phalam), such as feeling fatigued, are simply based on convention
{vyavahdra-mülam)  and do not constitute the highest truth."
5-6. Transformations of earth: The material body is made of earth in the form of feet,
ankles, calves, knees, thighs, torso, throat and bead. The palanquin carriers, the
palanquin and the king inside it are also transformations of earth.
Y o u r illusion: Blinded by the illusion of faise identity you think yourself the king of
Sauvira.
Note: A man is not fatigued in carrying the ornaments of a young girl, nor are parents
fatigued in carrying their young son. Therefore happiness and distress are experienced
in relation to one's particular identity.
L i n k : Rahügana        thought himself a dharmic ruler (see 5.10.23), though acting without
knowledge.
mmM^(lMiMúmiMMfm&MmiMM
7. Y o u r rule is adharmic. Y o u forcibly engage these innocent palanquin carriers
without payment who are suffering. This proves that you are cruel and unkind. But
out of faise prestige you think yourself the protector of the citizens.
8-9: The íllusory nature of the conventionai world
8. T h e earth is the cause of all bodies. Different bodies and capacities are simply
transformations of the earth that exist in name only. They arise from the earth and
disappear into the earth. I n other words, we are but dust and will remain but dust.
9. T h e earth is made up of atoms w h i c h are also impermanent. Although the earth
(universe) may temporarily appear to be the truth, it ultimately has no real existence.
Note: The earth is the cause of various objects in this world, but the Supreme Lord is
the originál cause of all causes and the source of the earth. The cosmic manifestation
is a mixture of His energies, namely matter and spirit.
10. T h i s universe is created by material nature. It is characterized by substance,
nature, mind, time and karma. It has divisions of fine and very fine, big and very big,
cause and effect, conscious and unconscious objects. However its distinctions exist in
name only and are imaginary.
L i n k : If the universe is not truth, then what is?
11. Nondual knowledge is devoid of material contamination {visuddham) and gives
liberation. It is the all-pervading Brahman, Paramátmá and Bhagaván. Learned
scholars describe it as the Supreme Person, Vásudeva.
L i n k : How is that Vásudeva        attained?

1 4 - 1 6 : J A D A B H A R A T A R E V E A L S HIS H I S T O R Y
14. Previous life as Bharata: Previously, I was a king named Bharata, detached from
all material activities, both seen and heard (vimukta-drsta-sruta-sanga-bandhah).  I was
fully engaged in the Lord's service, but was thwarted in my goal [háta arthah) due to
affection for a deer. Thus I became a deer in the next life {mrgo 'bhavarh mrga-sangdd).
15. Previous life as deer: Due to my past service to Lord Krsna {krsnarcana-prabhava),
I could remember my past life even in the deer body. Fearing material association, I
wandered about unnoticed.

20. Instructions to Rahugana: Rahugana, you are also a victim of the external energy
(like the merchant described in 5.13.1-19). Y o u should -
i. become an equal friend to all living entities                      {krta-bhüta-maitrah)
ii. give up your kingly position and the rod of punishment [sannyasta-dandah)
iii.    give up your attraction for sense objects                 (asaj-jitátmd)
iv. take up the sword of knowledge sharpened by devotional service (hari-sevayd
sitath jndndsim).
Thus you will cross material existence.

24-25: jada Bharata and Rahügana go their separate ways
24. Mercy of Jada Bharata: Jada Bharata was a paramahamsa. Therefore, although
Rahügana insulted him, he mercifuiiy instructed Rahügana on the science of the soul.
He forgót the insult because Rahügana pitifully begged pardon at his lotus feet. Then
he began to wander all over the earth.
25. Rahügana understood the constitutional position of the soul from Jada Bharata
and thus gave up his bodily conception. Such is the result of taking shelter of the servant
of the servant of the Lord    (bhagavat-dsrita-dsrita-anubhdvah).
L i n k : Pariksit requests Sukadeva Gosvdmi to give the direct meaning of the allegory.
26. "Piease explain: Y o u have described the position of the conditioned soul, who is
compared to a merchant in the forest. Intelligent men can understand its purport but
inexperienced cannot. Therefore I request you to give its direct meaning."
Note: Sukadeva Gosvámi explained the allegory in 5.14.1-42, which is given in the table
beginning on p. 86.
42. Like Garuda: Flies cannot follow the path of Garuda. Similarly great kings cannot
follow the path of Bharata, even mentally.
43. Renunciation: Bharata was fond of the Lord. I n the prime of his life he gave up
his wife, children, friends, and empire as if they were stool, although such things are
very difficult to give up.

1-3: Pariksit Maharaja inquires about the details ot Jamhudvipa
