11-29: Description ot llavrta-varsa and Mount Sumeru
L i n k : Pariksit Maharaja heard in 5.1.31 about the islands and oceans created on Bhü-
mandala by the impressions of Priyavrata's                 chariot. He now inquires about their
dimensions.
1 . Bhü-mandala's expanse: Y o u have spoken about Bhü-mandala, which extends as
far as the sun shines, and as far as the moon and stars can be seen.
2. Oceans and Islands: The rolling wheels of Priyavrata's chariot created seven ditches
in Bhü-mandala which became seven oceans, dividing it into seven islands. Now 1 desire
to know the measurements and qualities of those oceans and islands.
L i n k : Pariksit now speaks ofthe result of such an inquiry.
3. Result of knowing the Lord's maya: O gurul When the mind is fixed upon the
Supreme Lord's external feature - the gross universal form made of the modes
[bhagavato gunamaye sthüla-rüpa) - then it is brought to the platform of goodness, in
which one can understand Lord Vásudeva who is beyond the modes. Therefore piease
describe vividly that gross form, the structure of the universe.
Note: Here Pariksit asks the question not for himself, but at the request of bhakti-
misra-yogis present at the gathering, who desired to concentrate their minds. Paríksit's
way of attaining the Lord, on the other hand, is by drinking with his ears the honey of
topics of the Lord flowing from Sukadeva Gosvámi's lotus mouth.
5.16 A Description of Jambüdvípa
4 - 1 0 : J A M B Ü D V Í P A , ITS V A R S A S , A N D M O U N T S U M E R U
4a. N o one can possibly understand the end of the Lord's material energy {bhagavato
máyá-guna-vibhüteh),   which is a transformation of the three modes, even in a lifetime
as long as that of Brahma (vibudhdyusdpi).
4b. Y e t , I w i l l try to describe the principal regions (prddhdnyena), such as Bhüloka
(Bhü-mandala), with their names, forms, measurements and characteristics.
5. Bhü-mandala resembles a lotus flower, and its seven islands resemble seven circular
coverings of a lotus. The middle island, known as Jambüdvípa, is round like a lotus
leaf and measures 0.1 M Y in length and breadth.
6. I n Jambüdvípa, there are nine varsas (divisions of land), each with a length of 9
K Y (Ilavrta, Bhadrásva and Ketumála varsas are exceptions) and are separated by eight
mountain ranges that mark their bonndaries.
Figure 16.7: Sumeru Mountain
Bhágavata Suhodhini   ^
7a. Ilávrta-varsa is situated in the middle of Jamhudvipa. It has Sumeru Mountain,
which is made of gold, as its pericarp.
7b. Sumeru (or M e r u ) Mountain: Its height is the same as the width of Jambüdvípa
(0.1 M Y ) . Of that 16 K Y are within the earth and 84 K Y are above the earth. Its width
is 32 K Y at its summit and 16 K Y at its base.
JAMBÜDVÍPA-i
(AH dimensions are in KY)
Figure 16.8-10: Top view of   Jambüdvipa
8a. North of Ilavrta: Three mountain ranges named Nila, Sveta and Srhgavan mark
the borders of Ramyaka, Hiranmaya and K u r u varsas.
9a. South of Ilavrta: Three mountain ranges named Nisadha, Hemaküta and
Himalaya mark the bonndaries of H a r i , Kimpurusa and Bharata varsas.
V !»^5f | i       1       5*       liS.                     ^ ^ Description of J.iiniiücKi|w        ^ ^ ^ H
8-9b. Dimensions of the ahove mountain ranges: Width = 2 K Y ; Height = 10 K Y ;
Length = extends on the east and west to the salt ocean (going northwards from Mt.
Nila, the length of each mountain is 10% shorter than that of the previous mountain).
10. East and West of Ilavrta: T w o mountain ranges named Mályaván and
Gandhamádana mark the houndaries of Ketumála and Bhadrásva varsas with Ilavrta-
varsa respectively. The width and height of these mountains are 2 K Y and 10 K Y .
Lengthwise they extend from Níla Mountain in the north to Nisadha in the south.
Note A: Two varsas on the north (Kuru) and south (Bhárata) are bow-shaped. Four
other varsas (Kimpurusa, Hari, Ramyaka and Hiranmaya) are long and Ilavrta is
square. Bhadrásva and Ketumála are also shaped like bows, bordered by Nila and
Nisadha mountain ranges running horizontally and entering the sea.
Note B: Ilávrta-varsa is 9 K Y in all four directions from Mt. Sumeru, which has a base
of width 16 K Y . Thus, Ilávrta, Bhadrás'va and Ketumála are 34 K Y (9 K Y + 16 K Y + 9
KY) in width (measuring from north to south).
L i n k : After describing the divisions of Jambüdvipa,      each varsa is explained in detail.
1 - 2 9 : DESCRIPTION O F ILÁVRTA-VARSA A N D M O U N T SUMERU
Note: All the details of this section are summarized in the tahle at the end and are
depicted in Figures 16.11-29.
11. Four Mountains around Sumeru: Mandara, Merumandara, Suparsva and
Kumuda mountains are situated on its four sides like its helts. The length and height
of these mountains are 10 K Y .
12. Trees on the mountains: There is a mangó tree, a rose-apple tree, a kádamba tree
and a hányan tree on the summits of these four mountains respectively, standing like
flagstaffs. Their widths and heights are 0.1 K Y and 1.1 K Y , and their branches spread
to a radius of 1.1 K Y .
13. Lakes on the mountains: There are four huge lakes on these four mountains, fiiled
with water tasting like milk, honey, sugarcane juice and pure water respectively.
Siddhas, Caranas and Gandharvas, naturally endowed with mystic yogic perfections,
enjoy in those four lakes.
14-15. Gardens on the mountains: There are also four celestial gardens named
Nandana, Caitraratha, Vaihhrájaka and Sarvatohhadra. The hest of the demigods en-
joy in those gardens with their wives, while their glories are sung hy lesser demigods.
16-17. T h e mangó tree (Devacüta) on M t . Mandara: When the huge nectarean
mangoes fali from the top, they hreak, and the sweet, fragrant juice, mixing with other
scents, flows out as the River Arunoda to the eastern side of Ilávrta-varsa.

18. Effect of drinking from the Arunoda River: When Bhavaní's maidservants, the
pious Yaksa women, drink the water of the Arunoda River, their hodies become
fragrant, and it perfumes the entire atmosphere for 10 Y around.
19. The jambü tree on Mt. Merumandara: The elephant-sized fruits of the jambü
tree, fali and hreak and their juice hecomes the Jambü River. It flows for 10 K Y on the
Southern side of Ilávrta-varsa, flooding the entire land.
20-21. Jámbü-nada: The mud from the hanks of the Jambü River, moistened hy
flowing juice and dried hy air and sunshine, transforms into gold called jdmbü-nada.
The heavenly denizens use this to make ornaments to decorate their youthful wives.

22-23. T h e Mahákadamba tree on M t . Suparsva: From its hoUows flow five rivers
of honey (each 12 meters wide), that spread all over Ilávrta-varsa on the western side,

mouths of those who drink that honey perfumes the land for 100 Y around.
24. T h e Satavalsa banyán tree on M t . Kumuda: From the roots of this one hundred
branched tree flow many rivers on the northern side of Ilávrta-varsa. These rivers henefit
the people Iiving there with ample supplies of milk, yogurt, honey, ghee, molasses, food
grains, clothes, hedding, sitting places and ornaments. Thus they are very happy.
25. Happy humán society because of rivers: They do not experience wrinkles on their
hodies, grey hair, fatigue, perspiration, had odour, afflictions of old age, disease or
untimely death, and they do not suffer from chilly cold or scorching heat, nor do their
hodies lose their luster. They all live very happily, without anxieties, until death.

26. The Kesarácala Mountains: There are 20 mountains beautifully arranged around
the foot of Mt. Sumeru like the filaments around the whorl of a lotus flower. Their
names are Kuranga, Kurara, Kusumbha, Vaikahka, Triküta, Sisira, Patanga, Rucaka,
Nisadha, Sinivása, Kapila, Sahkha, Vaidürya, Járudhi, Haihsa, Rsahha, Nága,
Kálanjara and Nárada.
Note: These mountains are called the Kesarácala Mountains in 5.17.6
27. Another eight mountains surround Mt. Sumeru. East: Jathara and Devaküta,
West: Pavana and Páriyátra, South: Kailása and Karavira, and North: Trisrhga and
Makara. Their dimensions are L x W x H : 18 K Y x 2 K Y x 2 K Y . M t . Sumeru is made of
solid gold and shines as hrilliantly as fire, surrounded hy these eight mountains.
Note: Both mountains put together extend for 18 K Y in length, that is, each mountain
is 9 K Y in length and 2 K Y in height and width.
28-29: The townships of Brahma and the Lokapálas on top of Sumeru
28. Brahmá's township on top of Sumeru is named BrahmapurI, Sátakaumhhi or
Manovati, and is made entirely of gold. It lies is in the center of Sumeru's summit and
extends for many yojanas on its four sides.
29. T h e townships of the eight Lokapálas (governors of the directions) surround
BrahmapurI. They are similar to Brahmapuri hut are one fourth in size. The Lokapálas
starting from east are: Indra, Agni, Yama, Nirrti, Varuna, Váyu, Kuvera, and Siva.
Their cities are named: Amaravati, Tejovati, Sarhyamaní, Krsnáhganá, Sraddhávati,
GandhavatI, Mahodayá, and Yasovatl.

H a . Bhárata-varsa is understood to he the field of fruitive activities {karma-ksetram).
11b-13: The eight heavenly varsas (Bhauma-svarga)
11b. For pious people the other eight var sas are known as "heaven on earth" (bhauma-
svarga) and are meant for highly elevated pious persons who enjoy the remainder of
their pious acts (punya-sesa) after leaving the heavenly planets.
Note: There are three types of heavenly places: celestial (divya-svarga), earthly
(bhauma-svarga), and subterranean (bila-svarga).
12. Long and youthful life: I n these eight varsas the humán beings are like demigods
and live for ten thousand years. They have hodies sturdy as thunderholts and strong as
ten thousand elephants. I n youth they enjoy sexual unión for a long time and the
women conceive only once in the last year of their lives. Their happiness is like that of
Tretá-yuga.
Note: Their happiness is compared to Tretá-yuga for in this age people enjoyed sense
pleasure without tribulations. In Satya-yuga everyone was fixed in meditation and in
Dvápara and Kali suffering hecame more prominent.
13a. Ambience: These varsas are füled with many gardens with flowers and fruits,
hermitages, great mountains, lakes of clear water with swans, ducks, water chickens,
cranes and humhlehees.
13b. Enjoyment: The chief demigods there are always attended hy their respective
servants and enjoy life with their wives, who smile playfully and look upon them with
lusty desires.

14. The Lord's expansions: T o show mercy to H i s devotees, in each of these nine
varsas the Supreme Lord Náráyana expands Himseif in various forms and remains
near His devotees to accept their service.

in Ilávrta-varsa (in the southern direction of Sumeru). The mountain Kailása is the
abode of Lord Siva as indicated in the verse.

20. T h e Unagitated L o r d : For persons with impure vision, the Lord's eyes appear
intoxicated, angry and fearful. The Nagapatnis were agitated upon touching His lotus
feet and could not proceed in their worship out of shyness, hut the Lord remained
unagitated.
21. T h e Unlimited L o r d : The Lord is the cause of creation, maintenance and
destruction, though He actually has nothing to do with them [tribhir         vihinam).
Therefore He is called unlimited (anantam). As Sesa He holds all the universes on His
hoods, hut each universe appears no heavier than a mustard seed to H i m .
22-23. T h e Greatest L o r d : Supreme Lord  Lord Brahma       I (Siva)    demigods,
five elements and senses. Therefore I worship H i m under whose control we are all

who ties the knot of karma [karma-parvanim). Without being favoured hy H i m , one
cannot get out of that mdyd (nistdrana-yoga or bhakti-yoga). I offer respects to the
Lord, who is the cause of creation and annihilation (vilayodaydtmane)
Note: Srila Visvanátha Cakravarti Thákura gives another meaning for
vilayodaydtmane: The Lord who is the destroyer of mdyd for the devotee and the
creator of mdyd for the nondevotee.

15. I n Ketumála-varsa the Lord in the form of Kámadeva resides with Laksmi. He
stays there to please Prajápati Sarhvatsara whose sons and daughters are the controlling
deities of the days and nights respectively. Lhey number 36,000 - one for each day and
night in the lifetime of a humán being. A t the end of each year the his daughters become
agitated by the Lord's kdla-cakra and their fetuses are aborted.
Note: The fetuses refer to time with its minutes and seconds - O R - to the results of the
prdrabdha-karma of the inhabitants enjoying in that land. Because the nights act as the
cause of enjoyment of karmas they are called fetuses of the daughters. At the end of
gross time in the form of a year, with the end of enjoyment, they are aborted.
16. Kámadeva pleases L a k s m i (Ramá): By His gracious movements, beautiful face,
mild smile, movement of His eyebrows and playful glances. Lord Kámadeva pleases
Ramá and enjoys His transcendental senses (ramdrh ramayann indriydni ramayate).

31. Universal form vs. actual form: This universe, manifested by Your external energy
with countiess varieties of forms (bahu-rüpa-rüpitam),   is not Your real form. I offer
respects to Y o u whose actual form cannot be perceived by mentái specuiation

form. M y respects unto Y o u as Kapila, who have analyzed the cosmic manifestation

matériái world. His suffering in separation from H i s wife and banishing Sitá and
Laksmana are to teach how dutiful a king should be [dharma) and also indicate His
prema for H i s devotees.

13. T h e perfection of yoga (yoga-naipunam) means to place the mind at Your lotus
feet with devotion, giving up bodily Identification at the time of death.
14. Fear of death disturbs people who are very attached to bodily comforts {kdma-

wives, children and wealth. If a learned person [vidvdn) also fears death, his endeavour
of studying the sdstras is simply a waste of time (srama eva kevalam).
15. Please bestow bhakti-yoga. Infected by Your mdyd, we are attached to this
miserable body (ku-kalevara-arpitdm) and the conception of me and mine {aham-
mamatdm) which is very difficult to give up (sudurbhiddm). Therefore please give us
the power to fix our restless minds upon Y o u . That process is the only way out.

16. Mountains: Malaya, Mafigala-prastha, Maináka, Triküta, Rsabha, Kütaka, Koliaka, Sahya,

and Kámagiri. Besides these, there are many other hills, with many large and small rivers
flowing from their slopes.

and Visvá. The inhabitants of Bhárata-varsa are purified hy rememhering, touching and
chanting the names of these rivers.
19. Birth, activities and destination of the inhabitants: People take birth in Bhárata-varsa
according to their past karmas in sattva, raja, or tamo-gunas and have heavenly, humán or
hellish destinations according to their actions in these modes. If one is properly engaged in the
Lord's service (hy a guru) according to varndsrama-dharma, one achieves liheration.
L i n k : When does liberation occur and what is its nature?
20. T h e association of devotees leads to liberation. By such association (purusa-
prasangah) one can cut the knot of ignorance (avidyd-granthi-randhana) and render
service to Lord Vásudeva, who is: transcendental, free from matériái attachment
(andtmye), beyond the mind and words (anirukte) and independent (anilayane). That
bhakti-yoga is the real path of liberation.

Supreme Lord. Because He is complete. He offers them the benedictions they desire.

them from their desires by His special mercy.

2 9 - 3 1 : ISLANDS A R O U N D JAMBÜDVÍPA
29-30: Eight smaller islands surround Jambüdvipa: Svarnaprastha, Candrasukla,
Avartana, Ramanaka, Mandara-harina, Páficajanya, Simhala and Lanka. When the sons of
Mahárája Sagara were searching all over the world for their lost horse they created eight islands
hy digging the earth.

1-33: Characteristics of the six islands of Bhu-mandala

L i n k : After describing the varsas of Jambüdvipa      in detail, Sukadeva Gosvami begins to
explain the var sas of the remaining six dvipas.
1. N o w I shall describe the remaining six islands of the Bhu-mandala and their
dimensions, characteristics and forms beginning with the island of Plaksa.
Note: The common characteristics of all these six dvipas art listed below. The further
characteristics unique to each of these dvipas are mentioned in the tahles helow. All
these details are hased on 5.20.1-33.
Common aspects of the six islands (dvipas)
Structure: Jambüdvipa is surrounded by an ocean of salt water. The width of this
ocean (0.1 M Y ) is the same as the diameter of Jambüdvipa (0.1 M Y ) . Similarly, this
salt water ocean is surrounded by the next dvipa (Plaksa), which has a width double
that of the salt water ocean (0.2 M Y ) . A l l the six dvipas and their surrounding oceans
form disc-like structures surrounding the previous one. Each successive dvipa
surrounds its previous ocean and is twice the width of the previous ocean. The ocean
surrounding the dvipa is of the same width as the dvipa.
A special tree, piant, or mountain characterizes each dvipa. By this item each island
derives its name.
Varsas and rulers: The rulers of all these dvipas are the sons of Priyavrata Mahárája.
There are seven varsas in each of the first five dvipas and only two in the last dvipa
(Puskaradvipa). They are named according to the names of the sons of the rulers.
Handing over the rule to their respective sons, the sons of Priyavrata retired for full
engagement in devotional service.
Figure 20.1-33-A: The mountain ranges and rivers of Piaksadvipa and Sálmalidvipa
Mountains and rivers: Each of the first five dvipas has seven mountains and seven
rivers, with beautifully decorated hermitages, enormous lakes with flowers and birds.
In the last dvipa there is one mountain and two rivers. One can be free from matériái
contamination by touching or bathing in those rivers.
Inhabitants: There are four castes of people Iiving in those dvipas. The residents of the
first five dvipas, beginning from Piaksadvipa, all experience extraordinary Iongevity,
sensory prowess, physicai and mentái strength, intelligence and hravery.
Deities: The inhabitants there worship the Supreme Lord in H i s aspect as a demigod
(Sun, Moon, Agni, Varuna, Váyu and Brahma).

30. Manasottara Mountain is in the middle of Puskaradvipa and acts as the boundary
between the inner and outer parts of that island.
• Its width and height are 10 K Y .
• On that mountain, in four directions, are the residential quarters of demigods.
o East - Devadháni, the abode of Indra
o South - Samyamaní, the abode of Yamaraja
o West - Nimlocani, the abode of Varuna
o North - Vibhávari, the abode of the moon
• The wheel of the chariot of the sun-god, goes around Mt. Sumeru in one year,
and travels on top of Manasottara Mountain. Its northern {uttarayana - six
montbs) and southern {daksinayana - six months) courses are called the day
and night of the demigods.
• The distance from the center of M t . Sumeru to its middle point is 15.75 M Y .
L i n k : Sukadeva Gosvami now describes the remaining land of Bhü-mandala               beyond
the seven dvipas.
: LOKÁLOKA     MouNTAl^PI^|^^
34. Lokáloka Mountain surrounds the area beyond the ocean of sweet water and
divides the countries that are full of sunlight from those not üt by the sun.
35. T h e Golden Land is a tract of land between the ocean of sweet water and Lokáloka
Mountain. It is as broad as the area between the middle of Mt. Sumeru and the middle
of Manasottara Mountain (15.75 M Y ) . Its surface is like a mirror and any object
dropped there cannot be seen again. Therefore all Iiving entities have abandoned this
place.
36. Position: It stands between the land inhabited by Iiving entities {loka) and the
uninhabited land {aloka) and separates them. Thus it is celebrated as Lokáloka.
37. Height: It extends up to the end of the three worlds (Bhürloka, Bhuvarloka, and
Svarloka^) in height and in all eight directions. Thus by the Lord's supreme will, it
functions as the outer bordér of the three worlds. The rays of the sun, stars and other
luminaries spread throughout the three worlds, but cannot spread beyond the
boundaries of Lokáloka.
Note: Because it extends as high as Dhruvaloka, it defines the limit of the three worlds.
Dhruvaloka is 3.9 M Y above Bhü-mandala and above Svargaloka hut helow Maharloka.
^ Bhürloka refers to Bhü-mandala, Bhuvarloka refers to the antariksa, and Svarloka refers to the
heavenly abodes.
5.20 Studying the Structure of the Universe
38. Radius: The diameter of the universe is 500 M Y and the distance from Mt. Sumeru
to the end of Lokáloka is one fourth of this (125 M Y ) .
Note A: The width of the ring shaped uninhabited land (aloka) is also 125 M Y .
Note B: The outer shore of the sweet water ocean lies 25.35 M Y from the center of
Mt. Sumeru. Beyond that comes the Golden Land, extending for 15.75 M Y , and then
Lokáloka Mountain, extending for 82.2 M Y . Therefore, the totál distance from the
middle of Mt. Sumeru to the far end of Lokáloka is 123.3 M Y , or approximately one
quarter of 500 M Y , the diameter of the universe.
39. The four Gaja-patis: Lord Brahmá, the supreme spirituai master of the entire uni-
verse, established the four elephants - Rsabha, Puskaracüda, Vámana and Aparájitá -
in the four directions. They are responsible for supporting the universe.
Note: The diameter of Bhü-mandala is 493.2 M Y (4 x 123.3 MY), with all its oceans,
islands and mountains. Thus, there is space of 3.4 M Y separating it from the universal
shell. This allows Sesa to hold up the earth, with the Gaja-patis steadying it.
Link: How do these elephants support the universe?
40-41. The Lord's presence: The Supreme Lord, surrounded by His associates such as
Visvaksena, and exhibiting all His opulences, remains on Lokáloka Mountain until the
end of the kalpa (Brahmá's day). By His presence he increases the powers of those
elephants, who are protectors of the planets and His own expansions. He does this to
henefit all people and to maintain the worldly affairs created by His yoga-máyd.
4 2 : ALOKA-VARSÁ BEYOND LOKALOKAMOUNTAII
42. Aloka-varsa: The width of the land outside the Lokáloka mountain rangé (Aloka
varsa) is same as the distance from Meru to the far edge of Lokáloka Mountain
(123.3MY). Beyond Aloka-varsa is the destination of those who aspire for liberation
from this world, which is beyond the control of the modes and completely pure.
Note A : Lord Krsna took Arjuna through this place to bring back the sons of the
bráhmana in 10.89.47-50.
Note B: From this verse one can calculate the diameter of Bhü-mandala (4 x 123.3 M Y
= 493.2 M Y ) .

4 3 - 4 6 : GLORIES O F THE S U N
43. T h e sun is i n the middle of the universe: The sun is situated (vertically) in the
area between Bhürloka and Bhuvarloka (the antariksa or outer space). The distance
between the sun and tbe circumference of the universe is 250 M Y (vertically above and
below).
44. Different names of the sun-god:
• Vairája - The totál matériái body for all Iiving entities
• Mártanda - " W h o entered the dull egg of the universe."
• Hiranyagarbha - He received his matériái body from Hiranyagarbha (Lord
Brahmá).
45. T h e sun allows divisions to be seen among the sky, the higher planets, the middle
planets and the lower planets. By it one can know which are the places for enjoyment,
liberation and heh.
46. Glories of the sun: Because of tbe sun's presence all Iiving entities can see, and
therefore he is called drg-isvara. Thus he is the eye and life of all Iiving beings, of
devatás, animals, humans, reptiles and plants.
Router denotes the radius of the circle förmed by the outer end of that particular land, measured
from the center of Mt. Sumeru, which is in the middle of Jambüdvipa.

Note: As indicated by Srila Vádirája Tirtha in his book Bhügola Varnanam, "the seven
islands with their surrounding oceans are not fiat, but they take a terraced type of
arrangement. This terraced type of Bhü-mandala will help us reconcile the descriptions
of Srimad-Bhágavatam about the sun's movements in uttarayana (northern course) and
daksinayana (southern course)."
1 - 6 : T H E SUN'S M O V E M E N T U N D E R T H E SUPREME L O R D ' S C O N T R O L
1. Thus I have described Bhü-mandala with its dimensions and generál characteristics.
Figure 21.2: The lower and upper halves of the universe
2. Antariksa: A bean seed has two parts and one can understand the measurements of
the upper part by seeing its lower part. Similarly, the dimensions of Bhü-mandala, the
surface of the lower half of the universe (which consists of the Garbhodaka Ocean and
5.21 The Movements of the Sun
the lower planetary systems), indicate the dimension of the upper half of the universe.
The sky between the lower and upper halves (earthly sphere and heavenly spheres) is
called the antariksa or outer space, measuring 0.2 M Y in height.
3a. The sun is situated in the middle of the antariksa. It is the hottest of all planets and
by its radiation it heats up and lights the three worlds.
Note: Situated in the middle of the antariksa means it is vertically situated ahove
Puskaradvipa (at a height of 0.1 M Y ) . Horizontally, the sun's chariot travels at a
distance of 15.75 M Y from the center of Mt. Sumeru.
Note: There are twelve star signs in the zodiac helt or the kála-cakra (time-wheel). The
time taken hy the sun to move over half the zodiac helt is called an ayana. For more
details on the zodiac refer to 22.7 (p. 137) and the Note B on p. 136.
3b. Uttarayana and Daksinayana: Uttaráyaria is the sun's movement from Capricorn
to Gemini, during which it moves slowly, making the days longer and the nights
shorter. Daksinayana is the sun's movement from Cancer to Sagittarius, during which
it moves quickly, making the days shorter and the nights longer.
3c. Equinoxes: The sun moves at médium speed through Aries and Libra, making the
days and nights equal.
4. Movement of the sun through Mesa to Tula: I n Mesa (Aries) and Tula (Libra) the
durations of day and night are equal. I n Vrsabha (Taurus) and Mithuna (Gemini) the
days become longer and then gradually decrease by half an hour each month, until day
and night are again equal (in Libra).
5. Movement of the sun from Tula to Mesa: From Vrscika (Scorpio) the days become
shorter until Makara (Capricorn), and then gradually increase month after month,
until day and night are again equal (in Aries).
6. Until the sun travels to the south (daksinayana) the days grow longer, and until it
travels to the north (uttarayana) the nights grow longer.
7. The sun's path on Manasottara Mountain: The sun travels over all sides of
Manasottara Mountain in a circle with a circumference of 95.1 M Y . On top of
Manasottara Mountain are the residences of four demigods in the four directions:
• East: City of Indra - Devadháni
• South: City of Yamaraja - Sarhyamaní
• West: City of Varuna - Nimlocani
• North: City of Soma - Vibhávari
B         Bhagavata Subodhinl     f^C^lMiMlMlS^lMi^^
Sunrise, midday, sunset and midnight occur in all these places in succession, thus
engaging and disengaging people in work.
Note: Sunrise, sunset, midday and midnight in the different places: When the sun is
east of Mt. Sumeru, then those who reside south of Sumeru perceive sunrise, those west
midnight, those east midday, and those north sunset. When it shines at noon, it is
midnight at the city diametrically opposite.
8-9a: Clockwise and anticlockwise movements: The people on Mt. Sumeru
experience midday always. Although the sun moves anticlockwise, relative to the
constellations, with Mt. Sumeru on its left, it also moves clockwise being influenced by
the daksinávarta  wind.
8-9b. Days and Nights: The sun rises in one city while it sets at the city diametrically
opposite. When it shines at noon, it is midnight at tbe city diametrically opposite.
10. Speed of the sun: When the sun travels from Devadháni to Samyamani, it travels
23.775 M Y (one quarter of its totál orbit of 95.1 M Y ) in fifteen ghatikds or six hours
(one ghatiká is 24 minutes).
H a . T h e sun travels through the demigod resideuces from Sarhyamani           Nimlocani
—> Vibhávari        Devadháni. [Refer to Figure 20 on p. 129)
11b. T h e moon, stars and planets similarly rise and set in this way.
12-16: Description of the sun-god's chariot
12. Speed of the sun's chariot: Thus the chariot of the sun-god, worshipped by the
Gáyatri mantra (om bhür bhuvah svah tat savitur...), travels through the above four
residences at a speed of 3,400,800 Y in a muhürta (48 minutes).
13a. T h e time-wheel: The chariot of the sun-god travels on a wheel known as
sarhvatsara. This wheel has twelve spokes (twelve months), six rims (six seasons), and
three hubs (three cátur-másya periods).
Note: This is further described in 5.22.2. Refer to Figure 22.7 (p. 137) to see the
pictorial representation of the time-wheel.
13b. Chariot wheel and axle: Tbe chariot of the sun-god has only one wheel. One side
of its axle rests upon the summit of Mt. Sumeru, and the other rests upon Manasottara
Mountain. Affixed to the outer end of the axle, tbe wheel continuously rotates on
Manasottara Mountain like the wheel of an oil-pressing machine.
14. A second axle is attached to Dhruvaloka. Its length is 3.94 M Y , or one fourth of
the first axle. Its upper end is attached to Dhruvaloka (by a rope of wind) and its lower
end is bound to the first axle exactly opposite to tbe salt water ocean.
&'^ir'Ö7ÍÍ'''^lír'ÖHÍ^ C^irirtgltíj^                   5.21 The Movements of the Sun
Figure 21.13-14: Axles of the sun's chariot
15. T h e carriage of the sun-god's chariot is 3.6 M Y long and 0.9 M Y wide. The
chariot's horses, which are named after Gayatrí and other Vedic meters, are harnessed
by Arunadeva to a yoke that is also 0.9 M Y wide.
16. A r u n a watches the sun-god: Although Arunadeva sits in front of the sun-god and
is engaged in driving the chariot and controlling the horses, he looks backward towards
the sun-god.
17. 60,000 sages named Válikhilyas, each the size of a thumb, stay in front of the sun-
god and offer him eloquent prayers of glorification.
18. Other sages, Gandharvas, Apsaras, Nágas, Yaksas, Raksasas and demigods, form
into groups (2, 7, or 14) and, assuming different names each month, continuously
perform rituals to worship Süryadeva, who has many names.
19. Speed of the sun-god's chariot: I n his orbit through Bhü-mandala, the sun-god tra-
verses a distance of 95.1 M Y at the speed of 2 K Y and two krosas (0.5 Y ) in a moment.

L i n k : In 5.21.8, Sukadeva Gosvami mentioned that the sun travels in a                 clockwise
direction but also appears to travel anticlockwise through the zodiac. Pariksit now asks
for a    clarification.
1 -7: M O V E M E N T S A N D GLORIES O F T H E S U N
1. Paríksit's question about clockwise and anticlockwise movement: " Y o u have
affirmed that the sun travels around both Dhruvaloka and M t . Sumeru keeping them
on its right. Yet at the same time the sun, facing the signs of the zodiac, keeps Sumeru
and Dhruvaloka on its left. H o w can we reasonably accept this?"
Sukadeva Gosvámí's reply
2. Potter's wheel: Just as the motion of ants on a potter's wheel (which are moving in
the opposite direction to the rotation of the wheel) is different from that of tbe wheel,
the planets and the sun move separately from the zodiac, and thus shift through
different constellations and zodiac signs (in the opposite direction).
Note A : There is no contradiction since there are two separate movements: the
movement of the time-wheel and the individual movement of the planets.
Note B: The sun moves from east to west in the sky from our point of view. But in relation
to the zodiac and other stars, the sun appears to move from Aries to Taurus, and to other
signs in the opposite direction. Thus the sun, moon and other luminaries move in a
clockwise direction on daily hasis, but w.r.t. the time-wheel they move in a counter-
clockwise direction and seem to be entering different zodiac signs at different times.
3. The Lord as the sun-god arranged the seasons: Lord Náráyana, the originál cause
of the cosmic manifestation, responding to the prayers of the sages, divided Himseif
5.22 The Orbits of the Planets
into twelve parts and created six seasons with quaíities like heat and cold, for the
enjoyment of the Iiving entities according to their karma.
Note: The Lord, famous as time, divided Himseif into twelve forms and six seasons for
purifying the activities of the people. This means by manifesting Himseif as the time-
wheel (kála-cakra-gatah), the Lord regulates and purifies our activities.
4. Worshipping the Lord in varnasrama: By following varndsrama-dharma and
worshipping the Lord as the sun, with faith, by yoga, and by regulated activities, people
can easily attain the highest goal.
5. The months according to the sun: Tbe sun, the soul of all worlds, passes through
the twelve signs of the zodiac and takes on twelve different names. One month is two
fortnights by lunar calculation, or the time for the sun to cover 2.25 constellations and
is a day and a night for the Pitrs. T w o months constitutes a season.
6. Ayana: The time to move over half the zodiac beit is called one ayana.
•••••         Bhagavata Subodhini       3Í''ÍÍ*2*    li^^Ő* ií^^^^
'3y3',/k*''*33,y**-^'-,-^^'^33,/*é>-^z,/**^
7. Five names of the year: The time taken by the sun to pass completely through tbe
antariksa, the earthly pláne, and the heavenly planes, with slow, quick and médium
speeds, is called samvatsara, parivatsara, iddvatsa, anuvatsara, and vatsara.
Note: The twelve zodiac signs, such as Simha and Tula, get their names based on the
form manifested by a group of stars representing that sign. For example if a group of
stars are arranged in the shape of a lion, then that sign is called as Leo or Sirhha-rási.
8 - 1 0 : M O V E M E N T S A N D GLORIES O F THE M O O N
8. The moon's position and movement: The moon, situated 0.1 M Y above the rays
of the sunshine, travels at a speed faster than that of the sun (w.r.t. zodiac). It covers
the sun's year in two fortnights, its month in two and a half days and its fortnight in
one day.
9. W a x i n g and waning of the moon: The moon creates day for the demigods during
its waxing period, and creates day for the Pitrs during its waning period. The moon
that supports the life of all beings passes through one constellation in thirty muhürtas
(one day).
10. Glories of the moon: Because the moon is full of all potentialities, it represents the
influence of the Supreme Lord. It is called:
• Manomaya - The predominating deity of everyone's mind
• Annamaya - Wbo gives potency to all herbs and plants
• Amrtamaya - The source of life for all Iiving entities.
• Sarvamaya - He pleases everyone: the demigods, Pitrs, humán beings, animals,
birds, reptiles, trees, plants and all other Iiving entities.
1 1 - 1 7 : DESCRIPTION O F THE STARS A B O V E THE M O O N
11. The 28 constellations, headed by Abhijit, lie 0.3 M Y above the moon. Inspired by
the Lord, they move clockwise around Mt. Sumeru on the time-wbeel.
Note: By the will of the Supreme Lord, they are fixed on the time-wheel. They do not
have a different motion, but move clockwise along with the time-wheel {kalayane).
12. Venus (favourable): Venus (Usaná or Sukra) lies 0.2 M Y above the constellations.
It moves like the sun, sometimes behind and sometimes in front of the sun. It is
considered favourable for all, by causing rain and nullifies tbe influence of planets that
are obstacles to rainfall.
1                    *»\^3'~rkf
5.22 The Orbits of the Planets
13. Mercury (auspicious): Mercury (Budha, the son of the moon), lies 0.2 M Y above
Venus, and moves in a similar fashion. Generally it is auspicious, but when it does not
move along with the sun, it produces a fearful situation with drought, cyclones, and
continual clouds.
14. Mars (inauspicious): Mars (Angaraka) lies 0.2 M Y above Mercury. If not
travelling retrograde, it passes through one of the twelve zodiac signs every three
fortnights. Generally it is inauspicious and indicates grief.
15. Jupiter (favourable): Situated above Mars by 0.2 M Y , Jupiter (Brhaspati) moves
through each sign in one parivatsara, if not travelling retrograde. It is generally very
favourable to tbe brdhmanas.
16. Saturn (troublesome): Above Jupiter by 0.2 M Y is Saturn (Sani), which travels
slowly through one zodiac sign in thirty months. I n this way it travels through all
twelve signs in thirty anuvatsaras. It is generally very troublesome for all people.
17. T h e Sapta-rsis circumambulate D h r u v a l o k a : Situated above Saturn by 1.11 M Y
are the seven sages, who are always thinking of the well-being [sam anubhdvayantah)
of the inhabitants of the universe. They circumambulate Dhruvaloka (the polestar,
situated 1.3 M Y above the Sapta-rsis), the supreme abode of Lord Visnu.

1 - 3 ; GLORIES O F D H R U V A L O K A ( P O L E S T Ü J
1. Ahove the Sapta-rsis is Dhruvaloka: 1.3 M Y above the planet of the seven sages is
the abode of Lord Visnu, where Dhruva remains until the end of the kalpa. Circum-
ambulating him are Agni, Indra, Prajápati, Kasyapa and Dharma, with respect.
2. The polestar is the pivot of all luminaries: This star, established by the Lord, is
the central pivot of all the stars and planets. The unsleeping, invisible, most powerful
time factor causes these luminaries to revolve around the polestar without cessation.
3a. How do the planets and stars rotate around the polestar? Just as buUs yoked
together and tied to a central post to thresh rice, tread around that pivot without
deviating from their proper positions, so all the planets and luminaries, fixed in the
zodiac, revolve around the polestar in their respective orbits (higher and lower), till the
end of the kalpa, propelled by wind.
3b. How do they remain in orbit? Just as clouds or eagles, controlled by the wind and
pushed by karma, move about in the sky, so the planets, supported by matter and the
Lord, and moving by their past karmas, do not fali to tbe ground.
4. Sisumára (dolphin): Somé describe this zodiac beit, the great machine, consisting of
the stars and planets, to have the form of a dolphin (Sis'umara), used for worshipping
Vásudeva in meditation.

8-9:     M A N T R A T O W O R S H I P SISUMÁRA A N D T H E RESULT
8. Mantra to worship Sisumára: The body of Sisumára should be considered the external
form of Lord Visnu, composed of all devatás (sarva-devatámayam rüpam). Morning, noon
and evening, one should silently observe this form and worship Him with this mantra: " M y
obeisances unto You, who have assumed the form of time, the resting place of all the
planets, the master of all demigods. I meditate upon Y o u . "
9. Result of virát-rüpa worship: One who chants this mantra three times a day to
worship the Supreme Person and rememher this form of the Lord, which is the resting place
of all the demigods (ádhidaivikam) and all the stars and planets  (graharksatárámayam),
will surely be freed from all sinful reactions born of time.

1. Ráhu: 10 K Y below the sun is the planet Ráhu, which moves like one of the stars.
The presiding deity of that planet, Rábu, the son of Sirhhiká, although the most
abominable of all asuras, has achieved that position by the Lord's grace.
2. Ráhu covers the sunshine and moonshine: The sun globe, the moon and the Ráhu
planet extend for 10 K Y , 20 K Y and 30 K Y respectively. Ráhu, being inimical towards
the sun and the moon attacks them on the full and dark moon days.
3. Eclipse: Hearing of the attack of Ráhu, the Supreme Lord, engages His Sudarsana
cakra, to protect the sun and the moon. The intense heat of its effulgence, when it is
rotating for forty-eight minutes (one muhürta), is unbearable to Ráhu, and he therefore
flees in fear of it. People say that this is an eclipse.
4. Siddhaloka, Cáranaloka and Vidyádharaloka are situated 10 K Y below Ráhu.
5. Places of Yaksas, Ráksasas, etc: Beneath Vidyádhara-loka, Cáranaloka and
Siddhaloka, in the sky called antariksa, there are places of enjoyment for the Yaksas,
Ráksasas, Pisácas, ghosts and so on. It extends in the downward direction as far as the
wind blows and the clouds float in the sky.
6. Limits of Bhü-mandala: Bhü-mandala (earth) is 100 Y below the abodes of the
Yaksas and Ráksasas. Its upper limits extend as high as swans, hawks, eagles and simi-
lar large birds can fly. (This describes tbe upper limit of Bhürloka.)
Bhagavata Subodhini
7 - 1 5 : O P U L E N C E O F BILA-SVARGA (PLANETS B E L O W T H E E A R T H )   ^
7. Below the surface of Bhü-mandala, are the seven lower planets situated within the
hollow cavities of the earth, known as Atala, Vitala, Sutala, Talatala, Mahátala,
Rasátala and Pátála. Each has a width, length and height of 10 K Y .
8. Opulence of Bila-svarga: I n the subterranean heavens, known as Bila-svarga, there
are beautiful houses, gardens and places of sense enjoyment, more opulent than those
in the higher planets. Most of the residents of Bila-svarga, known as Daityas, Dánavas
and Nágas, live as householders. Their wives, children, friends and society are all fully
engaged in illusory, matériái happiness (mdyd-vinodd), with desires more unimpeded
than in heaven.
9. Magnificent constructions of Maya Dánava: Maya Dánava, an expert artist and
architect, has constructed many brilliant cities there, with wonderful houses, walls,
gates, assembly houses, temples, yards, and resting places. The houses for the leaders
are constructed with tbe most valuable jewels, and they are always crowded witb
Nágas and Asuras, as well as many pigeons, parrots and similar birds.
10. Further description of Bila-svarga:
• The parks and gardens surpass the beauty of those in the heavenly planets. The
trees, embraced by creepers, bent with the burden of twigs with fruits and flowers,
attract anyone and make bis mind fully blossom in the pleasure of sense
gratification.
• The lakes and reservoirs with clear water are agitated by jumping fish and are
decorated with many flowers such as lilies, kuvalayas, kahldras and blue and red
lotuses. Pairs of cakravdkas and other water birds nest in the lakes and always enjoy,
making sweet, pleasing vibrations that give sensual pleasure.
11. No sunshine and no fear of time: Since there is no sunshine there, time is not
divided into days and nights, and consequently fear produced by time does not exist.
12. The gems on the heads of the great serpents drive away all darkness.
13. Great health: By taking elixirs and juices made from divine herbs, the residents do
not experience anxiety, disease, grey hair, wrinkles (invalidity), discoloration of the
body, foul perspiration, or fatigue (lack of energy or enthusiasm) due to old age.
14-15. Fear of nothing except the Sudarsana: Living comfortably, they do not fear
death from anything but the Lord's effulgence called the Sudarsana cakra. The
pregnant wives of the demons all have miscarriages due to fear of its effulgence.
5.24 The Subterranean Heavenly Planets
1 6 - 1 7 ; A T A L A A N D V I T A L A PLANETS   ^ ^ ^ ^ ^ ^ ^ ^ ^ ^
16. Atala:
•    Bala, the son of Maya Dánava, resides here. He created ninety-six kinds of
mystic power, which somé so-called yogts and svámis use to cheat people.
•    Three kinds of women were created simply by his yawning:
o   The Svairinis - marry men from their own group
o   The Káminís - marry men from any group
o   The Puihscalis - change husbands one after another.
•    Enjoyment by the women: When a man enters their houses, these women
make him fit with hataka (an intoxicating beverage), and enjoy with him.
•     Intoxication: Drinking hátaka, the man, thinking he has the strength of ten
thousand elephants, as if blind with intoxication, thinks himseif God.
17. Vitala:
•    L o r d Siva, the master of gold mines (hdtakesvarah), lives here with his wife,
personal associates, the ghosts and similar living entities.
•    Hátaki river: Siva engages in sex with Bhaváni to produce progeny, and from
the mixture of their vitai fluid the river named Hátakí is generated.
•   Hátaka gold: When Agni, ablaze by the wind, drinks this river and then sizzles
and spits it out, it produces gold called Hátaka.
•   The demons who live on that planet with their wives decorate themselves with
various ornaments made from that gold.
1 8 - 2 7 : S U T A L A PLANET - GLORIES O F BALI M A H Á R Á J A             H
18. Sutala:
•   Bali Mahárája, the most pious king and son of Virocana, resides there even now.
•   For Indra's sake. Lord Vámana tricked Bali and took all the three worlds.
•   Being pleased with him, the Lord reinstated him on Sutala and made him richer
than the opulent King Indra.
•   Fven now, Bali worships the most worshipable Lord with a fearless heart.
19. Bali's real opulence: Mere attainment of matériái opulence on Sutala is not the
real result of Bali's giving land to the Lord, who is the source of life for all living
entities, and the Supersoui. The real result of this act, which was executed with great
Bhagavata Subodhini
faith and devotion, is the awakening of pure love for tbe Lord, which continues under
all circumstances.
L i n k : What to speak of offering the three ivorlds! Even námábhdsa     destroys the bondage
of karma, tuhich cannot be removed by pious acts.
20. Power of chanting the holy name once: Even while starving, f alling or stumbling,
if one chants the Lord's holy name even once (sakrn ndmdbhigrnan), he is immediately
freed from the reactions of karma. T o achieve that same freedom, others face many
difficulties in the practice of mystic yoga and so on.
21. T h e L o r d gives Himseif to H i s devotees (such as Nárada Muni) and gives
realization of Paramátmá to the dtmdrdmas (such as the four Kumáras).
Note: The Lord became Bali Mahárája's doorkeeper not because of his giving
everything to the Lord but because of his exalted position as a lover of the Lord.
22. Matériái opulence is not mercy: The Lord did not award His mercy to Bali
Mahárája by giving him matériái happiness and opulence, for these rob one of loving
remembrance (dtmdnusmrti-mosanam) of the Lord.
23. Bali bound and thrown into a cave: Finding no other means, the Lord adopted
the trick of begging and took away everything from Bali. The Lord bound him with
Varuna's ropes and locked him in a cave. At that time, Bali spoke as follows:
24-26: Bali Mahárája's reply imbued with humility
24. " I n d r a is ignorant of his real self-interest. Instead of begging the Lord for
transcendental loving service, he engaged H i m in asking me for alms to gain the three
worlds for his sense gratification. What value are the tbree worlds, that last only for a
manvantara, whicb is but a tiny fraction of endless time?
25. But our grandfather Prahláda understood his self-interest. He accepted only ser-
vice to the Lord and not the property of his father, which the Lord offered him when
He killed his father. He did not even accept the spirituai world, the place of no fear.
L i n k : Out of humility, Bali feels that he cannot follow   Prahldda.
26. Attached men can't follow Prahláda: What materially contaminated person like
me, who lacks the Lord's mercy, desires to follow the supreme path of the great devotee
Prahláda?"
27. Vámana kicked Rávana: I shall explain the glories of Bali Mahárája later. The
Lord Himseif stands at Bali's door with a club in hand. When Rávana came to gain
victory over Bali, Vámana kicked him a distance of 10,000 yojanas with His big toe.

28. Talatala: Maya Dánava, the king of the Dánava demons, lord of three cities and master
of magicians, resides here. Lord Siva (tripurdri) once set fire to his three cities, but later he
regained his kingdom by Lord Siva's grace. Protected by Lord Siva, he falsely thinks that
he need not fear the Sudarsana cakra.
29. Mahátala: On this planet live very angry snakes with many hoods. They are
descendants of Kadrü and headed by Kuhaka, Taksaka, Káliya and Susena. Bewildered by
the association of wives, children, friends and relatives, they enjoy themselves but
constantly live in fear of Garuda.
30. Rasátala: The demoniac sons of Diti and Danu, called Panis, Niváta-kavacas, Káleyas
and Hiranya-puravasis reside here. They are all enemies of the demigods, and they reside
in holes like snakes. From birth they are powerful, cruel and proud, but are always defeated
by the Sudarsana cakra, who rules all the planetary systems. When a female messenger
(dog) of Indra named Saramá chanted a particular curse, these serpentine demons became
afraid of Indra.
31. Pátála or Nágaloka: Many demoniac serpents, the masters of Nágaloka, such as
Sankha, Kulika, Mahásankha, Sveta, Dhananjaya, Dhrtarástra, Sankhacüda, Kambala,
Asvatara and Devadatta reside here. The chief among them is Vásuki. They are all very
angry, have many, many hoods - five, seven, ten, a hundred or a thousand. The valuable
gems on these hoods illuminate the caves of Pátála.

1 - 3 : A B O D E A N D ACTIVITIES O F L O R D A N A N T A
1. L o r d Ananta, or Saiikarsaria, is an expansion of the Supreme Lord, residing 30 K Y
below Pátála. He is called:
• Tamási - because He is the predominating deity of the mode of ignorance and
the supervisor of destruction, though always in the transcendental position.
• Sankarsana - because he is the predominating deity of falsé ego and draws
together (sankarsana) the enjoyer and the object of enjoyment, characterized by
the conception of " I . "
2. T h e universe as a mustard seed: This great Bhü-mandala (universe) is situated on
one of Lord Ananta's thousands of hoods, and appears just like a white mustard seed.
3. Rúdra appears from Ananta: When Lord Ananta desires to destroy the entire
creation, from between his two moving eyebrows appears the three-eyed angry Rúdra,
with eleven expansions, carrying a trident.
5.25 The Glories of Lord Ananta
jjEMirY     OF LORD ANANTADEVA
4. Transparent toenaiis: When the unalloyed devotees and the leaders of the snakes
offer their obeisances to Lord Sankarsana, they become very joyful upon seeing their
own beautiful faces with glittering earrings reflected in the mirror of the gem-like,
brilliant pink toe nails of His lotus feet.
5. Reciprocation w i t h serpent princesses: When the young serpent princesses, hoping
for His hlessings, smear His attractive long arms with a paste made of aguru, sandal-
wood and kuthkuma, that touch awakens their lusty desires. When the Lord looks at
them with a merciful smile, they become bashful and look upon His lotus face beautified
by reddish eyes rolling in intoxication and delighted by love for His devotees.
f    6 - 1 5 : CAUSELESS M E R C Y O F L O R D A N A N T A O N T H E C O N D I T I O N E D SOU^LS^^^
6. Controls anger: For tbe welfare of all jivas. Lord Ananta resides in His abode,
restraining His anger and intolerance until the proper time.
7a, Pleases H i s associates: The demigods, demons, Uragas, Siddhas, Gandharvas,
Vidyádharas and sages constantly offer H i m prayers. With joyful eyes. He pleases His
associates, the heads of the demigods, by the sweetness of His graceful words.
7b. Beauty: He bas a blue cloth, one earring, a plow on His shoulder, well-placed arms,
a golden beit around H i s waist, and a vaijayanti garland of ever-fresh tulasi blossoms
around His neck. The Lord enjoys His very magnanimous pastimes.
• Fnters the cores of their hearts
• Vanquishes the contamination of the three modes
• Cuts the knot of ignorance, tied tightly by impressions of karma since time
immemorial       [anddi-kála-karma-vasand-grathitam).
Nárada glorifies Ananta in Brahmá's assembly using his tamburu.
9. Incomprehensible: He is the cause of creation, maintenance and destruction of the
universe. By His glancé the gunas become capable of acting. His form is eternal [yad-
rüpam dhruvam), since it is never created. He is one, but manifested in many forms.
How can one understand his nature?
10. Purpose of H i s incarnations: The manifestation of subtle and gross matter exists
within H i m . Out of causeless mercy, to give pleasure to the hearts of His devotees. He
exhibits various transcendental (sarhsuddham) forms and pastimes.
I     Bhagavata Subodhinl   |Í3£^|M|g^|^ÜÍ^
11. Glories of H i s name: If a sinful person chants Lord Ananta's name after hearing
it, or chants suddenly, out of suffering, or in a joking mood, he becomes purified and
anyone who hears him is also freed from all sins. Therefore how can anyone seeking
liberation avoid chanting the name of Lord Sesa?

12. Unlimited glories: Since the Lord is unlimited who can count, even with thousands
of tongues, the powers of the Lord? This entire universe, with its mountains, rivers,
oceans and living beings, is resting just like an atom [anu-vat) on one of His many
thousands of hoods.
13. Unlimited prowess: There is no end to His great and glorious quaíities. Indeed,
His prowess is unlimited [duranta-viryoru). Though self-sufficient. He Himseif is the
support of everything. He resides heneath the lower planetary systems and easily
sustains the entire universe.
14-15. Conclusion: As I have heard from autborities, I have fully described the
destinations that are achieved according to the fruitive activities and desires of the
conditioned souls. What shall 1 speak of now?

- 3 : I G N O R A N T A C T I O N S L E A D O N E T O T H E HELLISH PLANETS
1. Pariksit inquired: " W h y are the living entities put into different matériái situations
(planets)?"
Sukadeva Gosvami replíed:
2. According to the faith [sraddhayá) of the worker in one of the three modes,
varieties of destinations are obtained for different actions (karma-gatayah prthag-
vidhdh):
Action In mode of         Result j
Goodness                  Happiness
Passión                   Mixed misery and happiness
Ignorance                 Misery
3. According to the extent of his ignorance [avidya), the worker engages in forbidden
[pratisiddha) acts of adharma and is placed into different grades of hellish life (see
below). Because of this ignorance, everyone has been carried by various desires
[kdmdndm) into thousands of different hellish planets. I shall describe them:
Ignorant action In         Resultant misery (helí)
Madness                    Least severe
Knowledge                  More severe
Atheism                    Most severe
Bhagavata Subodhini
4. Pariksit inquired: "Where are these hellish planets? Are they on the earth, outside
the universe or within the universe?"
Sukadeva Gosvami repiied:
5a. T h e hellish planets are located in an intermediate space between the three worlds
and the Garbhodaka ocean, on the southern side of the universe.
5b. Pitrloka is also located in this region. Desiring hlessings for their family members,
tbe Pitrs headed by Agnisváttá, meditate in great samádhi on the Lord.
6. Yamaraja, the king of the Pitrs and son of the sun-god, resides there. His agents,
the Yamadütas, bring him all the sinful men immediately upon their deaths. He then
judges them and sends them to suitable hells, in accordance with tbe Lord's orders.

3 7 - 4 0 : U N D E R S T A N D I N G THE V I R Á T - R Ü P A HELPS
IN U N D E R S T A N D I N G T H E L O R D ' S SPIRITUAL F O R M
37. Returning to earth: I n the kingdom of Yamaraja there are hundreds and thousands
of hellish planets. A l l impious people (including ones not mentioned above) must enter
these planets. And pious people enter the heavenly planets. But both categories of
people are again brought to earth after exhausting the results of their pious or impious
activities.
38. T h e virát-rüpa is the Lord's external body. I n the beginning (Cantos T w o and
Three) I have described the path of liberation. I n the Purdnas the virát-rüpa, which is
like an egg divided into fourteen parts, is described as the external body of the Lord
(sthavistharh rüpam). It is created by His energy and quaíities.
If one hears and expiains the virát-rüpa to propagate bhágavata-dharma       one's faith
and devotion will gradually increase and one will understand the topics of the Supreme
Lord, which are difficult to understand (upageyam).
39. F r o m external to spirituai form: The renunciant (yati), should first control his
mind by thinking of the virát-rüpa (sthülam) and then gradually think of the spirituai
form (süksmam) of Krsna after hearing of both forms. By devotional service one can
then realize the Lord's spirituai form, which is the destination of devotees.
40. Constituents of the virát-rüpa: Thus I have described: planet earth (bhü), dvipas,
varsas, rivers, mountains, sky, oceans, lower planetary systems, directions, hellish
planets and stars. These constitute the virát-rüpa, on which all living entities repose.

6. Question: N o w kindly teli me how humans may be saved from having to enter
hellish conditions in which they suffer terrible pain.
adhuneha mahá-bhága yathaiva narakán narah

7-19:   S U K A D E V A G O S V Á M Í TESTS MAHÁRÁJÁ PARÍKSIT
Note: Now áukadeva Gosvámi prescribes atonement according to karma, to test the
proficiency of his student Pariksit and to understand his opinion on the matter.
7-8: Karma-kándic atonement
7. Atonement: If a person does not perform prescrihed atonement (apacitim) for sins
committed by his mind, words or actions, he will enter the hellish planets after death.
8. Atonement according to the severity of sins: Like a doctor treats a disease
according to its gravity, one should undergo atonement as per sástra and according to
the heaviness or lightness of one's sins, quickly before death.
9-10: Paríksit Mahárája rejects atonement
drsta-srutábhyárh yat papám By seeing and hearing, the jiva knows that sinful
jánann apy átmano 'hitam         activity is injurious for him. Still...
karoti bhuyo vivasah         ...he helplessly commits sin again and again.
práyascittam atho katham       Therefore, what is the use of atonement?
kvacin nivartate 'bhadrát      Sometimes a person is very careful not to commit sins,
kvacic carati tat punah       only to again be victimized by sinful life.
Therefore I consider atonement useless. It is like the
práyascittam atho 'partham
bathing of an elephant (who throws dirt on his body
manye kunjara-saucavat
immediately after the bath).
Note: Paríksit rejected karma as a soiution for protecting one from sins. Now Sukadeva
Gosvámi prescribes jndna to further test Pariksit.
11-14: Jnána-kánda
11. Real atonement is jnána, or enlightenment in perfect knowledge (vimarsanam).
Karma-kándic atonement cannot completely (atyantika) destroy sin (karma-nirharah),
because it doesn't remove ignorance (avidvat), the seed of sin.
12. Jfiána is like a cure for disease. Just as a diseased person who eats the right food
is gradually cured, a person who follows the rules of jnána gradually progresses toward
liberation from matériái contamination.
13-14. Rules of jnána: By following austerity, celibacy, mind control, sense control,
charity, truthfulness, cleanliness, rules and regulations, a sober (dhirá) and faithful
person who knows dharma is temporarily purified of all sins. It is like a fire that
destroys bamboo trees (venu-gulmam ivánalah).
Note: But the roots of the bamboo will remain and grow again at the first opportunity.
Thus even after practising jnana sins may reappear.
L i n k : Therefore the answers concerning karma-kánda and jhana-kánda couldn't satisfy
the great devotee Pariksit. Seeing the dissatisfied Pariksit, Sukadeva Gosvámi now
speaks about the soiution according to the devotees.

15-19: Bhakti established
15. Unalloyed bhakti completely destroys sins
kecit kevalayá bhaktyá         Only a rare person who has adopted complete,
vásudeva-paráyanáh            unalloyed devotional service to Krsna...
...can completely uproot the weeds of sinful
agham dhunvanti kartsnyena
actions with no possibility that they will revive,
ntharam iva bhaskarah          just as the sun can immediately dissipate fog.
Note: The sun need do no more than merely manifest its first rays, for fog to
immediately disappear. Similarly, even with a slight appearance of bhakti, all the fog of
one's sinful life is immediately vanquished.
16. Bhakti is tbe best atonement
na tathá hy aghaván rájan     Mere austerities and other processes
I    püyeta tapa-ddibhih        cannot purify a sinful person...
yathá krsnárpita-pránas       .. .as much as dedication unto Krsna...
tat-purusa-nisevayá         ...by serving the Lord's devotee.
17. Most auspicious path: The path followed by the pure devotees, who are well
hehaved, virtuous and surrendered unto Lord Náráyana is certainly the most
auspicious path. It is free from fear.
sadhricino hy ayarh loke panthdh ksemo 'kuto-bhayah
susildh sddhavo yatra ndrdyana-pardyandh
18. Ineffective atonement: A liquor pot cannot be purified even by washing it in many
rivers. Similarly, nondevotees (ndrdyana-pardhmukham) cannot be purified by their
processes of atonement even if performed very well.
19. Devotees never see tbe Yamadütas even In dreams
Persons who only once absorb their mind
sakrn manah krsna-padáravindayor
on Krsna's lotus feet
and who have become attracted to His
nivesitam tad-guna-ragi yair iha
name, form, qualities and pastimes
do not see Yamarája or his order carriers
na te yamam pasa-bhrtas ca tad-bhatdn
with ropes, even in dreams. Such people
svapne 'pi pasyanti hi cirna-niskrtáh
have accomplished all atonement.
Note: Even once remembering the Lord is sufficient, what to speak of many times.
Even thinking within the mind is sufficient, what to speak of hearing or uttering His
name. Even the materially attracted mind (tad-guna-rdgi) is sufficient, what to speak
I     Bhágavata Subodhiní    ^flíg||l>|3£'^l>^^^
of a mind without matériái attraction. Thinking even in dreams is sufficient, what to
speak of actual meditation.
L i n k : To substantiate what is mentioned in 6.1.19, Sukadeva Gosvami narrates the story
of Ajamila.
20. A n old incident: I n this regard, the wise describe a very old incident involving a
discussion between the Visnudütas and the Yamadütas.
21. Fallen brahmana: I n Kányakubja there was a brahmana named Ajamila who
married a prostitute maidservant (ddsi-patir) and lost all his good conduct (nasta-
saddcdro) due to her association (ddsydh sathsarga-düsitah).
22. Sinful maintenance: He maintained his wife and children by the condemned
professions (garhitdth vrttim) of arresting (bandi), cheating in gambling (aksaih-
kaitavaih) or directly plundering (cauryaih) people.
23. Old age: Spending time in abominable, sinful activities to maintain his family with
his many sons, eighty-eight years of his life passed.
24-25. Youngest son Náráyana: He had ten sons, of whom the youngest was named
Náráyana, who was very dear to his parents. Because of his broken language [kala-
bhdsini) and awkward movements, old (pravayasah) Ajámila was very much attached
(baddha-hrdayah) to him and enjoyed (mumude) his activities (lild).
Note: Even at that old age, Ajámila had a very young child.
26. Attachment to his son: While eating (bhunjdnah), drinking (prapiban) and
chewing (khddan), Ajámila would feed the child and make him drink. Always taking
care of him and calling his name, Náráyana, Ajámila could not understand that death
had arrived (na veddgatam antakam).
27. When death arrived, foolish Ajámila thought exclusively of his son Náráyana.
28. He saw three Yamadütas, dreadful (ati-ddrundn), with twisted faces (vakra-
tunddn) and hairs standing erect (ürdhva-romna), carrying ropes to take him to hell.
Note: Ajámila's sins are classified into three types: bodily, verbal and mentái. Therefore
three Yamadütas approached him.
29. H e called out for Náráyana: With tears in his eyes (pldvitena) and in great anxiety
(dkulendriyah) He loudly (svarenoccair) called (djuhdva) his son Náráyana, who was
playing nearby.
Note: Because the name Náráyana has four syllables, four Visnudütas came.

6.1 T h e History of the Life of Ajamila
-55: CONVERSATION BETWEEN THE YAMADÜTAS AND VISNUDÜTAS
30. T h e Visnudütas arrived immediately upon hearing the name and glorification of
their master Lord Hari {hari-kirtanam, bhartur ndma) from the mouth of the dying
Ajámila (mriyamdnasya).
Note: Ajámila chanted "Náráyana" due to his attachment to his son. Nevertheless,
because of his past good fortune in having rendered devotional service to Lord
Náráyana, he apparently chanted the holy name in full devotional service and without
offences, in complete anxiety.
31. T h e Visnudütas stopped the Yamadütas from dragging Ajámila (the soul) from
the core of his heart [antah hrdaydt), by forbidding them with resounding voices.
Note A : By speaking loudly, the Visnudütas threatened to punish the Yamadütas.
Note B: This verse establishes that the soul is located within the heart.
3 2 - 3 6 : The Yamadütas reply to the Visnudütas
32-33. W h o are you, who challenge the jurisdiction of Yamarája (dharma-rdjasya
sdsanam)} Whose servants are you? Where are you from? Why are you forbidding us?
Are you demigods, sub-demigods or the best of devotees (siddha-sattamdh)}
34-36. Description of the Visnudütas
•   Eyes like the petals of lotus flowers         • Eresh and youthful
•   Dressed in yellow silk garments               • With four long arms, decorated with
•   Decorated with garlands of lotuses              bows and quivers of arrows, swords,
•   Wearing very attractive helmets                 clubs, conches, discs and lotus flowers.
•   Wearing beautiful earrings on ears            • Their effulgence dissipates all darkness
But why are you obstructing us?
37. T h e Visnudütas smiled: The order-carriers of Lord Vásudeva [vdsudevokta-
kdrinah) smiled and spoke with voices like rumbling clouds (megha-nirhrddayd gird).
Note A: Significance of their smiie - "You do not know dharma. Because of fear of
U S , you say you are the servants of Yama, but you are merely a type of ghost. Having
come into our hands, how will you live now?"
Note B: Both parties were surprised - The Yamadütas were surprised to see that the
Visnudütas, although polite, were hindering the rule of Yamarája. Similarly, the
Visnudütas were surprised that the Yamadütas, although claiming to be the servants of
Yamarája, the supreme judge of religious principles, were unaware of the principles of
religious action. Thus they smiled.

40-55: The Yamadütas reply
40. Dharma and Adharma
veda-pranihito dharmo   That which is prescrihed in the Vedas constitutes
hy adharmas tad-viparyayah dharma and the opposite of that is adharma.
The Vedas are directly Náráyana and are self-born
vedo narayanah saksat
(manifested from His breathing). We have beard this
svayambhür iti susruma
from Yamarája.
41. Náráyana, who is situated in H i s own abode (sva-dhamni), controls tbe entire
cosmic manifestation according to the three modes. Thus all the jivas appear with
different qualities, names (such as brahmana), duties and forms.
L i n k : One may think of doing sinful activity secretly when no one is seeing, but there
are witnesses. Náráyana causes these witnesses appear.
42. Witnesses: The sun, fire, sky, air, demigods, moon, evening, day, night, directions,
water, land and Supersoul Himseif (or Yamarája) all witness the activities of the jiva.
süryo 'gnih khath marud devah somah sandhyáhani disah
karh kuh svayam dharma iti hy ete daihyasya sáksinah
43. T h e candidates for punishment are those who are confirmed hy these witnesses
to have performed adharma. They are suhjected to punishment according to their sinful
acts.
44. Everyone under the modes is punishable. Y o u are sinless, but those within this
matériái world are contaminated hy the three modes and cannot he inactive. Thus they
perform both pious and sinful acts.
45. T h e process of punishment: I n proportion to adharma and dharma performed in
this life, one must suffer or enjoy the corresponding reactions in the next (according to
the decision of scripture).
6.1 Tho History of the Life of Ajámila
46a. Varieties of life: We can see three different varieties of life due to the
contamination of the three modes of nature (guna-vaicitryat).
Note: Thus the living entities are known as peaceful, restless and foolish; as happy,
unhappy or in-between; or as religious, irreligious and semireligious.
46b-47. Present determines past and future: The three kinds of matériái nature will
similarly act in the next life. The current life of happiness and distress in an indication
of dharma and adharma of past and future lives.
Note: A n example - Springtime in the present indicates the nature of springtimes in
the past and future.
48. Yamarája, heing omnipotent and omniscient, and as good as Brahmá, although
situated in his own ahode Sarhyamani, mentally ohserves the past activities [pürva-
rüpam) of a jiva and understands how he will act in future lives (apürvam).
Note: Yamarája follows the Supersoul (anumimáritsate), by whose grace he can see
the past, present and future of a jiva. The actual decisions concerning the next lives of
the jivas are made by the Supersoul. Yamarája is in consultation with the Supersoul
and in cooperation with Him, executes those decisions.
49. A man is not aware of his past or future birth, because of identification with the
current body and loss of memory at birth {nasta-janma-smrtih). It is like the bodily
identification of an animál or a dreaming person.
Note: Therefore, it is not surprising that the jiva has a tendency for sin, since he is not
aware of his previous or next birth.
50. T h e jiva enjoys w i t h the 16 elements: Ahove the 5 knowledge senses, the 5 action
senses, and the 5 sense ohjects is the mind (the 16^'' element). Ahove it is the soul (the
17'*' element) who, in cooperation with the other 16 elements, enjoys three kinds of
situations (happy, distressful and mixed) in this matériái world.
51. Perpetual transmigration: The subtle body {lingam), consisting of these 16 parts
(sodasa-kalam), is an effect of the three modes {sakti-trayarh). It causes unavoidahle
transmigration (anusarhsrti) that gives joy (as a demigod), lamentation (as a humán),
fear (as an animál) and generál misery (harsa-soka-bhaydrtiddm) to the jiva.
52. Entrapped like a s i l k w o r m : Just as a silkworm is entrapped in a cocoon made
from its own saliva, the jiva, inept to control his senses and mind (ajita-sad-vargo), is
forced to act under the modes against his desire. He is hewildered hy his own fruitive
activities and finds no way to release himseif.

54. T h e jiva's karmas are the unseen cause for his attainment of gross and subtle
hodies, which are sometimes similar to that of his mother or father.

esa prakrti-sangena               Bondage: This awkward position of forgetfulness of

58-60. H e sees a man embracing a prostitute: Once Ajámila, following father's order,
went to the forest to collect fruit, flowers and grasses. On the way home, he saw a
lusty südra shamelessly embracing a prostitute. Both were drunk. He was smiling,
singing and enjoying. Her eyes were rolling in intoxication, and her dress became loose.
6 1 . Lusty desires are awakened: Seeing (drstvd) the woman, emhraced hy the südra's
arms, decorated with turmeric powder, the dormant lusty desires in his heart awakened
and in illusion (vimohitah) he feli under their control [hrt-saya vasam).
62. He tried to control his lusty desires hy intelligence and hy rememhering sdstric
instructions, hut he was unable to control the mind, which was agitated hy Cupid.
stambhayann dtmandtmdnam ydvat sattvam yathd-srutam
na sasdka samddhdtum mano madana-vepitam
63. H e lost all his good sense {vicetanah) like when the sun or moon is covered during
an eclipse {graha-grasta). Because of lust arising from seeing her, he always meditated
{dhydyan) on her, and abandoned all the regulative principles of a brdhmana
{svadharmdd virardma).
Note: Ajamila could not protect himseif by jndna or his steadiness in dharma. But he
later attained Vaikuntha, restoring himseif through ndmábhdsa alone. This shows that
the inherent strength of bhakti is superior to that of dharma and jndna.
64. T o satisfy the prostitute with matériái presentations, Ajámila spent all the money
inherited from his father.
65. Gives up wife: His intelligence pierced hy the lusty glancé of the prostitute
{svairinydpdnga-viddha-dhih), Ajámila engaged in sinful acts and soon gave up his
young wife, who came from a respectahle brdhmana family.
66. H e earned money hy proper or improper means, and used it to maintain the
prostitute's sons and daughters. Thus he was hereft of intelligence {manda-dhih).
67. Sinful life: Transgressing the scriptures {sdstram ullanghya), acting irresponsibly
{svaira-cdry), condemned for his hehaviour {ati-garhitah), leading a sinful life
{aghdyur) and heing impure {asuci) because of eating a prostitute's remnants, he passed
a long time.
68. H e did not undergo atonement {akrta-nirvesam). Therefore we must take him to
Yamarája {danda-pdneh), where he will he punished according to his acts and purified
{suddhyati).

are always very expert in logic and arguments (naya-kovidáh), replied as follows.
2. Adharma i n dharma's place! Alas, how painful it is that adharma is touching the
assembly of authorities of dharma, where unpunishahle (adandyesu) and sinless
(apdpesu) persons are heing punished unnecessarily.
3. Protectors should be impartial ^ ^ ^ ^ ^ ^ ^ ^ ^ ^ ^ ^ ^ ^ ^ ^ ^ B   B B B B B B B B B B I

his hehaviour as the standard.

5-6. Kind-hearted leaders: A n innocent citizen, who does not know dharma or
adharma, is like an ignorant animál sleeping peacefuUy with its head on the lap of its
master. H o w can a kind-hearted leader, who should have the trust of all heings, punish
such a person who has fully surrendered in good faith?
L i n k : The Yamadütas might say, "But Ajdmila has not performed atonement for his
great sinsf"
7-8. Ajámila has atoned for millions of lifetimes of sins just by chanting the name of
Náráyana in a helpless condition (vivasah). Those names are a means of liberation
(svasty-ayanam). Even previously, whenever calling his son and uttering the four
syllables nd-rd-ya-na, he sufficiently atoned for all his sinful reactions.
9-10. Chanting the holy name is the best process of atonement for: a thief; a
drunkard; a hetrayer; one who indulges in sex with the wife of his guru; one who kills
a brdhmana, a woman, the king, his father, or cows; and for all other sinful men.
Simply by chanting the holy name of Lord Visnu, such sinful persons may attract the
t*X'
Kmmmíi'mm'mim:                                                       •1
attention of the Supreme Lord, who therefore considers, "Because this man has chanted
M y holy name, M y duty is to give him protection."
11-12: Chanting is more powerful than prescrihed atonement
11. One remembers the Lord's qualities: By following rituals or undergoing
atonement, sinful men do not become as purified as by chanting the holy name of Lord
H a r i , even once or just partially (ndma-padaih). Such chanting reminds one of the
Lord's fame, qualities, attributes, pastimes and paraphernalia {guna upalambhakam).
12. I t completely purifies: Even after atonement, one's mind again runs toward
matériái activities. Therefore, for one who wants liberation, the glorification of the
Lord is recommended. It actually purifies one's existence.
13. Therefore do not try to take Ajámila. He has chanted the holy name of the Lord
at the time of death and is free of all sinful reactions.
14. Chanting is effective irrespective of circumstances and intention
sdnketyath pdrihdsyam vá           While indicating someone else, or while joking,
stobham helanam eva vd            for musical entertainment, or out of neglect
vaikuntha-ndma-grahanam           chanting the holy name of the Lord
destroys one's unlimited sins and desires. Advanced
asesdgha-haram viduh
transcendentalists know this.
15. Chanting in conditions beyond one's control: A person who chants the name of
the Lord while falling from the top of a house {patitah), slipping {skhalitah) and
hreaking his bones (bhagnah) while travelling on the road, heing hitten hy a serpent
(sandastah), heing afflicted with pain and high fever (taptah), or heing injured hy a
weapon (dhatah), will not suffer hellish punishment.
1 6 . Atonement according to the severity of sin: The great sages have carefully
ascertained that one should atone for the heaviest sins with severe atonement and
lighter sins with lighter atonement.
guründm ca laghündm ca gurüni ca laghüni ca

Note: Because atonements have limited power, such arrangements are prescrihed.

activities, regardless of whether heavy or light, since it has inconceivably great power.
It is as easy as Balaráma's ability to destroy the all Kauravas, including Duryodhana, in
order to save Sámba.

2 0 - 2 5 : AJÁMILA'S RELEASE A N D REGRET
2 0 . Release: Having thus perfectly judged the principles of devotional service
(dharmam bhdgavatam), the Visnudütas released Ajámila from the bondage of the
Yamadütas and saved him from imminent death.
2 1 . T h e Yamadütas inform Yamarája of everything that had happened.
2 2 . Ajámila came to his senses, having been released from the Yamadütas' nooses,
and now free of fear. Pleased by the presence of Visnudütas, he offered obeisances to
them hy howing his head at their lotus feet.
2 3 . T h e Visnudütas disappeared as soon as they saw that Ajámila was attempting to
say something.
Note: The Visnudütas made Ajámila aware of devotional service so that He might
immediately become fit to return home, back to Godhead. But to intensify his eagerness
for glorifying the Lord in the mode of separation, they quickly disappeared.
2 4 . T w o types of dharma: After hearing the discourses between the Yamadütas and
the Visnudütas, Ajámila could understand:

i.   The dharma of the three Vedas that acts under the modes (trai-vedyarh
gunásrayam)
ii. Transcendental dharma which is pure (dharmam bhdgavatam suddharh).
25. Ajámila begins to repent: Having heard glorification of the name, fame, qualities
and pastimes of the Lord, Ajámila became fiiled with devotion. On rememhering his
sinful actions he hegan to repent, and thought as follows:
2 6 - 3 8 : AJÁMILA'S LAMENTATION A N D R E S O L U T I O N S
26. Alas, how degraded I became heing a servant of my senses! I feli down from my
position as a duly qualified brdhmana and hegot children in the womh of a prostitute.
27. A l l condemnation upon me! I acted so sinfully that I degraded my family tradition
(kula-kajjalam). I gave up my chaste and young wife to have sexual intercourse with a
fallen prostitute accustomed to drinking wine.
2 8 . 1 gave up my old parents who had no other son or friend to look after them. Thus,
they lived with great difficulty. Alas, like an abominable lower-class man, I
ungratefuUy left them in that condition.
29. I should be thrown into hell where those who destroy dharma (dharma-ghndh)
and are full of lust (kdminah) undergo torture hy Yamarája.
30. Was this a dream I saw, or was it reality? I saw fearsome men with ropes in their
hands coming to arrest me and drag me away. Where have they gone?
31. Where have the Visnudütas gone? Where have those four liberated (siddhdh) and
very beautiful persons (cdru-darsandh) gone who released me from arrest and saved
me from heing dragged down to the hellish regions?
32-33. Because of somé previous spiritual activities I was ahle to see those exalted
personalities, hy which I have become joyfui. Otherwise, how could I , a most sinful,
unclean keeper of a prostitute, have gotten an opportunity to chant the holy name of
Vaikunthapati when I was about to die?
Note: One must assume the mercy of somé devotee as the cause of his bhakti, because
without that, the seed of bhakti cannot be sown. Because of that mercy, he was able to
name his son Náráyana.
34. Comparison: What am I , a cheater, sinner and a shameless killer of hrahminical
culture, in comparison to the all-auspicious chanting of the holy name of Lord
Náráyana?

so 'ham tathá yatisyámi yata-cittendnyánilah
