36-y7. N o w I shall disentangle myself from matériái bondage which has heen caused
hy the Lord's maya in the form of a woman. Now I shall give up all lusty desires and
free myself from this illusion. I shall hecome a merciful, well-wishing friend to all living
entities and will always ahsorh myself in Krsna consciousness.
38. Determination: I shall not fali victim again to the falsé lures of matériái sense
gratification and I shall give up the falsé conceptions of " 1 " and "mine." I will fix my
mind on the lotus feet of Krsna hy chanting his name, hearing, etc.
39-44:   AJÁMILA'S AUSTERITIES AT H A R D W A R A N D ATTAINMENT O F V A I K U N T H A
39. Departure: Because of a moment's association with devotees, Ajámila hecame
detached and freed of all matériái attraction and went to Hardwar.
40-41. H e performed bhakti-yoga in Hardwar, where he took shelter at a Visnu
temple, controlled his senses and applied his mind on the service of the Lord. Thus he
detached his mind from the modes and became fully ahsorbed in thinking of the form
of the Lord [bhagavad-dhamni], the supreme Brahman, who is the ohject of realization
[anubhava-átmani].
42. Again he sees the Visnudütas: When his intelligence and mind were fixed upon
the form of the Lord, he once again saw the persons whom he had previously seen, and
he offered obeisances hy howing down before them.
43. Attains svarüpa: Upon seeing them, he gave up his matériái body on the bank of
the Ganges and regained his originál spiritual body [svarüpam jagrhe), which was a
body appropriate for an associate of the Lord (bhagavat-pársva-vartinám).
44. Goes to V a i k u n t h a in a vimana: Accompanied hy the order carriers of Lord Visnu
(mahápurusa-kinkaraih), Ajámila hoarded an airplane made of gold. Passing through
the airways, he went directly to the ahode of Lord Visnu (sriyah patih).

4 5 - 4 9 : SUKADEVA G O S V Á M Í GLORIFIES THE H O L Y NAME
45. Ajámila was saved by the holy name. He:
•     Gave up all religious principles (vipldvita-sarva-dharmá)
•     Became the husband of a prostitute (ddsydh patih)
•     Degraded himseif hy condemned actions (patito garhya-karmand)
•     Was destined to he thrown into hell (nipdtyamdno niraye)
•     Broke all his vows (hata-vratah).
Yet he was immediately rescued hy merely a glimpse of the chanting of the holy name
of Náráyana (sadyo vimukto bhagavan-ndma grhnan).
46. Permanent purification: Therefore for one who desires liberation there is no
hetter way than chanting ahout the Lord (ttrtha-pada). By chanting, the mind does not
again hecome attached to matériái actions. By other processes, the mind again becomes
contaminated hy rajas and tamás.
47-48. Phala-sruti: One who hears or descrihes this confidential historical narration
with faith and devotion is:
/.   No longer doomed to hellish life
ii.   Not approached hy the Yamadütas
iii.   RespectfuUy received and worshipped in Visnu-loka.

1-2:   PARÍKSIT'S INQUIRY A B O U T YAMARÁJA'S R E A C T I O N
1 . What did Yamaraja say after hearing the statements of the Yamadutas, who were
obstructed by the Visnudütas?
2. I t is unheard of that Yamarája's punishment can he avoided (na danda-bhangah).
People will have douhts ahout this, that no one hut you can eradicate.
3-10: T H E YAMADÜTAS' Q U E S T I O N S A B O U T THE SUPREME CONTROLLER
Suitadeva Gosvámi replied:
3. T h e Yamadütas, whose efforts have heen thwarted hy the Visnudütas approached
Yamarája, the master of Sariiyamani-puri and spoke as follows:
4. H o w many controllers (sdstarah) of jivas are there in this world, who are the causes
(hetavah) of the results of activities performed under the three modes?
5. I f there are many controllers, who disagree about punishment and reward, then no
one would get punishment or reward, or everyone might get both.
Note: The Yamadütas were defeated in carrying out Yamarája's orders and their
master could not protect them. So, they were inclined to say that there was no need to
sérve such a master and doubted whether Yamarája actually had the power to punish
the sinful and if there are higher authorities.
6. There can be only one supreme controller, even if multiple judges exist to give
justice to different karmis. He guides the multiple departmental heads.
7. We thought you are that supreme controller (adhisvarah) and punisher (danda-
dharah) of all jivas including the demigods, who decides the happiness and distress
(subhdsubha-vivecanah) of all humans.
8-9. But your order is has been transgressed by four wonderful and perfect persons,
who cut our ropes when we were bringing the sinful Ajámila towards the hellish planets
(ydtand-grhdn) under your order.
10. When Ajámila chanted "Náráyana," these four men quickly came and said, "Do
not fear." Kindly teli us who they are, if you think it is heneficial for us.

14-15. Demigods and sages cannot understand the Lord's activities, although free
from the lower modes and situated in the mode of goodness. What then to speak of
others?
16. L i v i n g entities cannot ascertain the Lord's real position hy their senses, minds, life
airs, thoughts, or words, just as bodily limhs cannot see the eyes.
17-18. Qualities of the Visnudütas:
i.   Very attractive (manoharah) with forms (rüpa), qualities (guna) and natures
(svabhavah), almost like that of the independent (atma-tantra) Supreme Lord,
the master of the illusory energy (máyddhipati).
ii.   They are worshipped hy the demigods (sura-püjitdni)
iii.   Their wonderful forms are very rarely seen (durdarsa-lingdni mahddbhutdni)
iv.    They protect the Lord's devotees from enemies, Yamarája and natural
disturbances.
Note: Yamarája has specifically described the qualities of the Visnudütas to convince
his own servants not to be envious of them.

truth about dharma, which even great sages cannot understand?"

1-2. Pariksit inquires about visarga (anusargam). I wish to know about:
• T h e creation of demigods, demons, humán beings, Nágas, beasts and birds
during the reign of Sváyambhuva Manu, which you spoke about briefly (in the
Third Canto).
• T h e Lord's potency by which He brought about the secondary creation.
3. Sukadeva Gosvámí praised Pariksit's inquiry and replied as follows:
L i n k : In order to explain how Daksa carried out the secondary creation, first the way
in which he took birth again, after giving up his body with a goat's head is explained.
4. Trees covered the earth: When the Pracetas emerged from the waters, they saw
that the entire surface was covered with trees.
Note: The same incident is narrated in SB 4.30. Before the Pracetas returned from their
austerities, their father Pracinabarhi retired to the forest. Thus, there was no king to see
to the management of the world and the earth became overgrown with trees.
5. T h e Pracetás were angry due to long austerities. Desiring to burn the trees to ashes,
they generated wind and fire from their mouths.
6. Soma felt sympathy when he saw the trees burning, because he is the maintainer of
all herbs and trees. T o appease the anger of the Pracetás, he spoke as follows:
7-15: Soma speaks to the Pracetás
7. Do not k i l l these poor trees. Your duty is to wish the citizens [praja) all prosperity
and to act as their protectors.
8. T h e L o r d created them: The indestructible Lord, the master of all Prajapatis, has
created these trees and plants with fruits and grains as eatables for other living beings.
9. Foods for different species: By nature's arrangement - (/) non-moving beings are
food for the moving beings, [ii) beings without legs are food for those with legs,
[iii) animals without hands are food for animals with hands, and [iv) four-legged
animals and plants are food for the humán beings.
10. Trees are needed for maintenance: Your father, Prácinabarhi, and the Supreme
Lord have ordered you to generate population. Therefore, how can you burn to ashes
these trees, which are needed for the maintenance of your subjects?
11. Follow the path of your predecessors (your father, grandfather and great-
grandfathers) by protecting the subjects and controliing your intense anger.
12. The king protects his subjects (including trees) like:
tokándm pitarau bandhü             Parents protect children
drsah paksma                 Eyelids protect the eyes
striydh patih               Husband protects a woman
bhíksundrh grhy               Householder maintains beggars
ajndndm budhah suhrt               Learned person is the friend of the ignorant_
13. See all beings as the Lord's abode (tat-dhisnyam) as He is situated within the their
hearts as the Supersoul. By such vision, you will satisfy the Lord.
14. Transcending the modes: One who subdues the sudden appearance of his anger,
hy inquiring into self-realization, transcends the influence of the modes.
15. Accept M a r i s a : There is no need to hurn these trees any longer. Let the remaining
trees he happy. Accept Marisa, who was raised hy the trees as their daughter, as your
wife.
1 6 - 2 2 : D A K S A ' S B I R T H A N D INITIAL A C T I V I T I E S
1 6 . T h e Pracetás marry Márisá: After thus pacifying the Pracetás, Soma gave them
the beautiful girl horn of Pramlocá Apsará and they married her.
17. T h e Pracetás heget Daksa in the womb of Márisá. Daksa fiiled the three worlds
with living entities (during the Cáksusa manvantara).
18. Please hear from me how Daksa, who was very affectionate to his daughters,
created different types of living entities through his semen and mind.
19. W i t h his mind, Prajapati Daksa first created all kinds of demigods, demons, humán
beings, hirds, beasts, aquatics and so on.
20-21.  Austerities: But when he saw that the population was not increasing, he went
to the sacred place of Aghamarsana near the Vindhya mountains. There, he executed
very difficult austerities and satisfied the Supreme Lord.
2 2 . Daksa offered the Hamsa-guhya prayers to the Lord. Now I shall explain those
prayers and how the Lord was pleased with Daksa for those prayers.
Note: The Harhsa-guhya prayers were not composed by Daksa but were existing in the
Vedic literature.

2 3 - 3 4 : T H E H A M S A - G U H Y A P R A Y E R S BY D A K S Á
M Y RESPECTFUL OBEISANCES UNTO THE LORD W H O I S . . .
23. • Transcendental to maya and its products (pardya).
• Real knowledge [avitatha anubhütaye).
• T h e controller of the living entities (guna-traydbhdsa) and mdyd {nimitta).
• Unseen (adrsta-dhdmne) by persons wbose intelligence is absorbed in matériái
objects (guna-tattva-buddhibhih).
• Self-manifest {svayambhuve) and surpassing all matériái                           measurements
[nivrtta-mdndya).
24-25: The Lord's knowledge is unlimíted while the jíva's is limited
24. T h e supreme controller and friend: As the sense objects cannot understand how
the senses perceive them, so the conditioned soul, although residing in his body along
with his friend (sakhd vasán), the Supersoul, does not recognize the friendship
(sakhyam) of that friend [sakhyuh) and cannot understand how He directs his senses
{vyakta-drsteh).
25. Omniscient and unlimited:
• Matter cannot perceive: Because they are only matter, the body, the life airs,
the senses, the elements and the sense ohjects cannot know themselves, or other
things, or the pva.
• But the jiva can know these items and the three qualities that form their roots.
He can also know Paramátmá (taj-jnah).
• But the jiva doesn't know the omniscient (sarva-jham) Lord, because He is
unlimited (anantam).
Note: In the liberated state, the jiva can know Paramátmá, but he cannot know the
Supreme Lord because the Lord's spiritual qualities are xmlimited.
L i n k : The Lord can be known in two aspects: His impersonal aspect, which is easy to
understand (sujheyam), and His form with qualities, which is difficult to understand
(durjheyam). Daksa is first describing the easily understood aspect.
26-27: The Lord's personal and impersonal forms
26. Supremely pure: The Lord is perceived as impersonal in the stage of trance, where
one's consciousness is completely purified, and the memories in the mind, which
manifest matériái names and forms, are vanquished.
L i n k : Now, the form with qualities (durjheyam) is described.
1 hl' I Limsa-guhya P r a \ i ' r s
27. Realized by expert devotees: Just as brahmanas extract fire from wood through
mantras, the advanced devotees can find the Supersoul in the heart. By His own
spiritual potency He is situated there, along with the twenty-seven elements that
constitute His external energy.
28-30: Matériái forms do not arise from the Lord's svarüpa
28. T h e reservoir of spiritual potencies. He is: (/) Inconceivable [anirukta), (ii)
Realized as biiss without matériái suffering (nirvdna-sukhdnubhütih) hy those who are
eager for liberation, (iii) Addressed hy various spiritual names, (iv) The visva-rüpa.
May He he pleased with me.
29. Beyond matériái faculties: The Lord's real nature (svarüpam) cannot he
ascertained hy words, intelligence, senses or mind, which are the effects of the matériái
modes (guna-rüpam). The Lord is the cause of the creation (visarga-laksanah) and
destruction of aii matter (gundpdya).
Note: When it is said that the Lord cannot be explained by words, it means "by words
that are not favoured by Him." The same is applicable for the mind, senses and the
intelligence. There are many verses in Vedas which state that the Lord can be known
by the mind, etc. Somé say that the Lord cannot really be ascertained, since He cannot
be completely ascertained as His qualities are unlimited.
30. T h e cause of all causes: The Supreme Brahman, Krsna, is the cause of aii lower
and higher causes (hetuh):
• yasmin - I n whom everything rests (ultimate resting place)
• yatah - From whom everything emanates (ultimate source)
• yena - By whom everything is enacted (ultimate actor)
• yasya - T o whom everything belongs (ultimate proprietor)
• yasmai - T o whom everything is offered (ultimate beneficiary)
• yat - Who is the desired ohject of every action (ultimate object)
• yah - Who does everything hy Himseif or through others (ultimate doer)
He is well known (prasiddham), existing before all activities, one without a second
(ekam) and with no other cause (ananyad).
yasmin yato yena ca yasya yasmai
yad yo yathd kurute kdryate ca
pardvaresdm paramam prdk prasiddham
tad brahma tad dhetur ananyad ekam

31-32: The statements of the hhaktí and jnána scriptures are not contradictory
31. T h e possessor of unlimited divine qualities: The Lord's maya causes various
philosophers to forget their own souls {dtma-moham) by causing their agreement
(samvdda) and disagreement (vivdda) by which they cannot reach a conclusion.
L i n k : If someone says that these philosophers argue because there are different opinions
stated in the scriptures, then that is not true. Both bhakti (yoga) and jndna (Sdnkhya)
perceive the same Brahman without disagreement.
32. T h e goal of both jnanis and bhaktas: Both, the bhaktas (yogis) and the Sdnkhyites
(who analyse matter) approach the same Supreme Brahman. Although they offer
opposing statements, their ohject is the same ultimate cause (eka-sthayoh).
33. The possessor of transcendental names and forms
To show mercy towards those who

the unlimited Lord who has no matériái

names and forms.
exhihits transcendental names and forms

with His different appearances and
bheje sa mahyam paramah prasidatu
pastimes. May He he pleased with me.
34. Also realized i n lower forms: As the air carries different aromás and colours, the
Lord appears through lower systems of worship (prdkrtair jndna-pathair) according to
one's desires. He appears as the demigods (or as the jiva for the impersonaiists) and
not in His originál form. May He fulfil my desires.

6.4 T h e Hamsa-guhya Prayers   "
He was accompanied by great devotees like Narada, Nanda and all the principal
demigods led by Indra. He was praised by the Siddhas, Gandharvas and Cáranas.
40. Danda-vat: Seeing that wonderful form, Daksa was first somewhat afraid, but then
very pleased. He feli to the ground like a stick to offer his respects.
41. Daksa's senses were fiiled w i t h pleasure, just as rivers are fiiled by waterfalls. He
could not say anything, but simply remained fiat on the ground.
42. T h e L o r d spoke: The Lord, who knows the heart of every living entity [citta-jnah),
spoke to Daksa who was prostrated before H i m , desiring create progeny.
43-53: The Lord speaks to Daksa
43. Y o u r life is successful because of your austerities by which you have attained
devotion [mayi bhávam) through your great faith directed to Me.
44. I am pleased w i t h you because you are endeavouring by your austerities to fulfil
M y desire for increasing the population of the world.
45. Y o u are all M y vibhütis: Brahmá, Siva, the Manus, the demigods and you
Prajapatis are working for everyone's welfare by increasing the population. Y o u are
expansions of M y marginal energy and incarnations of M y various qualities.

53. Y o u r children will also be captivated by M y mdyd and will engage, like Y o u , in

to Me in devotion.
54. T h e L o r d disappeared: After speaking in this way, the Lord immediately
disappeared as if He were an object experienced in a dream.

Náráyana-saras in the west, where great sages live.
4-5. Nárada's arrival: Bathing regularly in that holy laké they became purified and
inclined towards the activities of paramahamsas. While they performed austerities to
fulfil their father's desire, one day, Nárada Muni approached them.
L i n k : Ndrada Muni thought, "The Haryasvas are pure-hearted and qualified for
liberation. Why then should they be entangled in family life? I will liberate them by
giving them confidential teachings." Thus, he presented them an allegory with ten
subjects (6.5.6-9). The Haryasvas,       with their natural intelligence, understood its
meaning (6.5.10) and decided to renounce the path of fruitive activities (6.5.11-20).

22. Nárada M u n i travelled, with his mind fixed (akhandam cittam dvesya) on the
lotus feet of the Supreme Lord, playing musical notes in glorification of Lord Krsna.
23-25. Daksa lamented upon hearing about the renunciation of the Haryasvas.
Brahmá pacified him and he begot another 1000 sons called the Savalásvas. Daksa
ordered them to also heget children and sent them to Náráyana-saras.
Note: Vedic culture necessitates training in spiritual understanding as a brahmacdrí
before entering household life to heget children. Thus, Daksa, being a dutiful father,
did not hesitate to send his second group of sons to the same place where his previous
sons had met Nárada Muni and attained perfection.
26-28. T h e Savalás'vas were purified of all matériái contamination by chanting,
bathing in the holy waters, and performing severe austerities. For somé mouths they
took only water and chanted the following mantra:
oth namo ndrdyandya purusdya mahdtmane
visuddha-sattva-dhisnydya mahd-hamsdya dhtmahi
29. Nárada approached and spoke enigmatic words to them, just as he had done to
their elder brothers, the Haryasvas.
30-31. Nárada to the Savalásvas, " Y o u are all affectionate to your elder brothers, so
you should follow their path. A pious brother gets the opportunity to associate with
the Maruts (who were also affectionate to their brothers)."
Note: Nárada indicates that the Savalasvas will jóin the Haryasvas in Vaikuntha.
32a. Nárada then left. His merciful glancé is all-auspicious (amogha-darsanah).
32b-33. T h e Savalásvas took to the correct path, favourable for attaining the mercy of
the Lord, like their elder brothers. They never returned, like nights that have passed.
34-35. Daksa's anger and lamentation: Daksa observed inauspicious signs and heard
the news of his sons' renunciation, caused by Nárada. He almost fainted due to
lamentation. When he met Nárada, he spoke as follows:
Note: Why did Nárada meet Daksa? Nárada considered, "The dynasty of
Sváyambhuva Manu - Priyavrata, Uttánapáda, Dhruva, Pracinabarhi, the Pracetás,
Haryasvas and Savalásvas - all attained the Lord by my efforts. How is it that only
Daksa remains in the blind hole of household life? I will deliver him also. Hearing that
his sons have renounced, Daksa must be lamenting and developing detachment. Now
is a suitable time to give him mercy. If he does not come to me, I will go to his house."
Thus Nárada went to see Daksa.

44. Nárada's tolerance: Though capable of cursing Daksa in return, Narada, tolerantly
accepted the curse. Such hehaviour is befitting his saintly character.
Note: Why did Nárada tolerate Daksa's accusations and curse? Nárada could have
left immediately, but he thought, "Let Daksa exhibit his anger and curse me. When his
anger subsides, he will understand that I tolerated everything and did not criticize him
or curse him in return. Then he will repent for his misbehaviour. Then he will be a
suitable field for me to sow the seed of bhakti." Thinking in this way, Nárada remained
there for the appropriate moment. But seeing that Daksa was not repentant, he then
left, thinking "Oh! What power the remnants of offence to Lord Siva have!"

sons so that Ndrada would not disturb them for accepting the renounced order.

23-24a. T h e moon had no offspring: The 27 constellations (Krttiká, etc.) were all the
wives of the moon-god. Because he was very attached to one wife Rohini and neglected
the others, Daksa cursed him and caused his gradual destruction. Thus the moon-god
could not heget children. Thereafter he pacified Daksa and regained the portions of
light he had lost during his disease. During the dark fortnight he loses his shining
power, and during the bright fortnight it manifests again.

L i n k : Hearing how the demigods had offended their guru, Pariksit asks the details.
1 -10:   INDRA FAILS T O RESPECT BRHASPATI
1 . Pariksit inquired: " W h y did Brhaspati reject the demigods? What was their offence
to their guru}"
Sukadeva GosvámI:
2-8. Proud Indra: Once, Indra, being extremely proud of his great opulence,
transgressed the law of Vedic etiquette.
Opulence of Indra: He was seated on his throne,
•    Surrounded by the Maruts, Vasus, Rudras, Adityas, Rbhus, Visvadevas,
Sádhyas, and Asvini-kumaras.
•    Praised by Siddhas, Cáranas, Gandharvas, Vidyádharas, Apsarás, Kinnaras,
Patagas and Uragas.
•    Served w i t h royal paraphernalia - over him was a white umbrella as effulgent
as the full moon. He was being fanned by yak-tail whisks and sitting with his
wife, SacidevI, who occupied half the throne.
Indra's offence to his guru: Brhaspati, the guru of the demigods, appeared in that
assembly. Although Indra saw him, he did not rise from his seat, or offer him a seat,
or welcome him. Indra did nothing to show him respect.
9. Brhaspati left: Knowing about Indra's future, and understanding that he is püffed
up by matériái opulence (sri-mada), Brhaspati, although ahle to curse Indra, silently
left the assembly and went home.
10. Indra immediately realized that he had disrespected his guru and condemned
himseif in the presence of all the members of the assembly.
11-17: INDRA'S LAMENTATION
11-15: Indra regrets his act
11. I am very proud: What a regrettahle deed (asádhu-krtam) I have committed due
to lack of intelligence (dabhra-buddhiná) and pride in my opuiences [aisvarya-
mattena)\ I failed to respect to my spiritual master when he entered this assembly.
12. What learned person would accept the opuiences of the king of heaven hy which
I , the king of the demigods, have developed a demoniac mentality (dsuram bhdvam)
and have hecome proud?
ÍMft   'ÍÍIMÍÍ '&~ti   '&'H   '&'tí           6 7 Indra Offends His SpirituarMastei] Brhaspati
13. Ignorant opinions: One who says that the person situated on the royal throne of
Brahmá (pdramesthyam) should not rise to show respect to anyone does not actually
know dharma.
14. A stone boát: Ignorant leaders mislead people to the path of destruction (kupatha-
destfndm). Those who put faith in them sink down like persons riding in a hoat made
of stone (asma-plavd).
15.1 w i l l now bow down at the lotus feet of Brhaspati, without duplicity (nisathah).
I will try to satisfy him, the spiritual master of demigods (amardcdryam), who has deep
knowledge (agddha-dhisanam).
16. Brhaspati disappeared: While Indra was thus thinking, Brhaspati, understanding
Indra's mind, left home and disappeared hy his great power (adhydtma-mdyayd).
17. Indra could not find Brhaspati, although he searched vigorously with the assistance
of the other demigods. Thus he found no peace of mind (sarma ndlabhatdtmanah).
18-19. T h e demons attack the demigods. Hearing of Indra's pitiahle condition, the
demons, following the instructions of their guru Sukrácárya, declared war against the
demigods. They injured their heads, thighs, arms, etc. Thus the demigods approached
Lord Brahmá with howed heads, seeking shelter and proper instruction.
20. Powerful Brahmá pacified the demigods hy his great mercy and spoke as follows:
21. Y o u r offence: Due to the madness of matériái opulence, you failed to receive
Brhaspati properly, who is a sense controlled brdhmana, aware of the Supreme
Brahman.
22. Consequence: Due to this misbehaviour, you who are strong have heen defeated
hy the weak demons, who have heen defeated hy you several times before.
23-24a. T h e power of worshipping guru: Formerly, the demons were weak due to
disrespecting Sukrácárya. But now, by worshipping him (drddhya bhaktitah), they have
gained so much strength that they may even seize my abode. Determined to carry out
the orders of their guru (abhedya-mantrd, anusiksita-arthdh), they are now
unconcerned with the demigods.

35-42: T H E POWER O F THE NÁRÁYANA-KAVACA                ..
35-37: Benefíts of wearing the Náráyana-kavaca
• Y o u will be able to conquer the leaders of the demons
• Anyone seen or touched is immediately freed of all fears and dangers
• No disturhance or danger from the government, plunderers, evil demons, or disease.
38-40. Previous example of K a u s i k a : A brdhmana named Kausika formerly used this
armour when he purposely gave up his hody in the desert hy his mystic power.
Citraratha, who was passing in his airplane, was forced to fali from the sky headfirst
with his airplane. H e was struck with wonder and was ordered hy the Válikhilyas to
throw the brdhmana's hones in the nearby Sarasvati River. After bathing, he returned
to his abode.
41. Phala-sruti: One who employs this armour or hears about it with faith is
immediately freed from all dangers and is worshipped hy all living entities.
42. Indra's victory: Indra, after receiving this prayer from Visvarüpa and conquering
the demons, enjoyed all the opuiences of the three worlds.

1 ^ I g ^ l N D R A KILLS^^IÍV^ÜPA AND D l S T R l B ü t t ^ l ^ l j ^ U L REACTIONS^
1. Visvarüpa had three heads: one for drinking soma-rasa, another to drink wine and
the third to eat food.
2. He visibly offered oblations to the demigods in the sacrifice whiie ioudiy chanting
mantras. They were reiated to him through his father.
3. H e secretly offered oblations to the demons as they were his maternai reiatives.
4. Indra beheads Visvarüpa: When Indra understood that Visvarüpa was cheating
them, he became extremely afraid and angrily cut off Visvarüpa's three heads.
5. Transformation of Visvarüpa's three heads: The head meant for -
i.    drinking soma-rasa was transformed into a kapihjala (francoiin partridge).
ii.    drinking wine was transformed into a kalavinka (sparrow).
iii.    eating food became a tittiri (common partridge).
6. Indra suffers: Although Indra could neutralize his sinful reactions, he accepted them
with földed hands. He suffered for one fuii year, and then to purify himseif, he
distributed these reactions among the earth, water, trees and women.
Note: The intensity of the reaction lessened after a year of repentance. After that, to
purify the earth, water, air and fire elements in his body which had become impure by
his sinful action, he divided the sin into four parts. Since ether cannot become impure,
he purified only four elements. But because of this, sin remained in his antah-karana in
subtle form. That seed fructified in the form of killing a brahmana again, with the
killing of Vrtrásura.
7 - 1 0 : Dístríbutíon of Indra's sinful reactions
Note: Indra offered benedictions to the earth, trees, women and water for accepting
one-fourth of his sinful reaction each.

11. Mispronunciation: After Visvarupa was killed, his father, Tvasta, performed a
sacrifice to kiii Indra, saying, " O enemy of Indra, fiourish to kiii your enemy without
deiay."
Note: Instead of chanting the mantra short, Tvasta chanted it long. H i s intention was
to kiii the enemy Indra. Thus he prayed for increase of strength of the enemy of Indra
(Vrtrásura). However, the same words can be taken to mean "increase the strength of
Indra, the enemy."
12. Appearance: From the sacrificiai fire came a fearful personaiity who iooked Üke
the destroyer of the entire creation at the end of the millennium.
13-17. T h e demon's fearful features: T a i i , biackish, gigantic, iustrous; coppery hair,
beard and moustache; red eyes; with blazing trident; Dancing and shouting with a loud
voice, he made the earth tremble as if from an earthquake. As he yawned, he seemed
to swaiiow the sky with his cave-iike mouth. He seemed to be iicking up the stars with
his tongue and eating the entire universe with his long, sharp teeth. Seeing this gigantic
démon, everyone ran in aii directions, in great fear.
18. Named as V r t r a : That very fearful démon, the son of Tvastá, covered aii the
pianetary systems. Therefore, he was named Vrtra, or one who covers everything.
19. T h e demigods attacked Vrtrásura with their soldiers, striking him with heavenly
bows and arrows and other weapons, but Vrtrásura swallowed aii their weapons.
20. Worshipping the L o r d : Struck with wonder and disappointment, having lost their
own strength, the demigods met together and worshipped the Supersoul, the originál
person.
21. Let US take shelter of the L o r d w h o m even time (death) fears. The five elements
- ether, air, fire, water and earth - are controlled by us, the demigods, but we are
afraid of time and offer our presentations by performing our work as time dictates.
22. T h e unparalleled shelter, the Supreme Lord is: (/) never wonder-struck by
anything {avismitam), [ii) fully satisfied by His own spiritual perfection (paripürna-
kámam svenaiva Idbhena), (iii) equipoised (samam), and (iv) very steady (prasdntam).
A person desiring protection without approaching H i m is certainly a great fooi who
tries to cross the sea by holding onto a dog's tail (sva-ldngulena).

23-24: The Lord protected us eariier also
23. M a n u saved by Matsya: May the Lord, the deliverer of dependants (tratasritan),
who saved Manu in the ocean of devastation, by binding the boát of the earth on His
horn, save us from the fearful danger caused by Vrtrásura.
24. Brahmá saved by the L o r d : M a y the Lord who saved Brahmá from danger, in the
beginning of the creation when he almost feli from his lotus seat in fear of the loud
waves of devastation, protect us now.
L i n k : Though the Lord is present everywhere. He is not visible to our matériái senses.
By His mercy may He become visible and protect us!
25-27: May the Lord be visible to us
25. We cannot see H i m . The Lord created us by His own mdyd and by His mercy we
expand the creation of the universe. We cannot see H i m because we think that we are
separate and independent gods (prthag isa-mdninah).
26. H e appears in various incarnations. Considering us his own (krtva dtmasdt), he
protects US when we are greatly suffering from the demons, by becoming avatdras in
various forms among demigods (Vámana), sages (Parasuráma), animals (Nrsimha and
Varába), aquatics (Matsya and Kürma), and men (Ráma and Krsna).
27. We approach and take shelter of H i m , who is (i) the shelter of devotees (dtma-
daivatam), (ii) the supreme cause (páram), (iii) matériái nature (pradhdnam), (iv) the
supreme enjoyer (purusam), (v) the universai form (visvam), (vi) different from
universe (anyám), (vii) worthy of worship by aii (sarve saranam saranyam), and (viii)
the Supersoul (mahdtmd).
28-30:   T H E L O R D APPEARS BEFORE THE D E M I G O D S
28. T h e Lord's appearance: When the demigods offered prayers. Lord H a r i , carrying
His weapons (conch, disc and club) appeared first within their hearts and then before
them.
29. Sixteen attendants: Surrounding and serving the Lord were sixteen personal
attendants, appearing exactiy Üke H i m (dtma-tulyaih) but without the mark of Srívatsa
and the Kaustuhha jewel.
30. T h e demigods saw the iotus eyed Lord smiling and were overwheimed. They
immediately feli down Üke rods (dandavat), then siowiy rose and offered prayers.
31. We offer our respects to Y o u , who:
/.   bestow the results of sacrifice (yajha-viryáya)
ii.   destroy the results of sacrifice, as the time factor {vayase uta)
iii.   release the cakra to kiii the demons (asta-cakrdya)
iv.    possess many varieties of names (supuru-hütaye).
L i n k : The Lord might say, "You are praising Me to obtain insignificant, temporary
Svarga. Why don't you ask for Vaikuntha?"
32. We cannot understand V a i k u n t h a because we appeared later in the creation than
You, who are the controller of three destinations (heaven, humán birth, and hell).
L i n k 31 (iv) —> 33: The demigods said that the Lord is invoked by many names (above).
Now they praise him by addressing him with those names.

O most exalted person, supreme experience, welfare personified! O supreme
benediction, supremely merciful! O support of cosmic manifestation, soie proprietor of

transcendental biiss of Your form. We simply offer Y o u our obeisances.

a) like an ordinary humán that enjoys or suffers his good or had reactions
under the influence of time, past work, etc?
b) Or are Y o u present here as a neutral witness (udáste) who is seif-satisfied
(dtmárdmah), free from aii matériái desires (upasama-silah), and always fuii
of spiritual potency (samanjasa-darsanah)}
36. A l l contradictions are reconciled i n Y o u : These two conditions or anything seen
in Y o u are not contradictions (virodhah), because nothing is impossible for Y o u who

37. Y o u inspire dualities as the Supersoul, according to one's intelligence (such as
equaiity and inequality, proper and mistaken conceptions, peace and disturhance, fear
and feariessness). A rope causes fear in one who considers it a snake, but not for one
with proper intelligence. But in Y o u there is no duality.
38. A s the Supersoul, Y o u are: (i) the basic principle of everything (sarva-vastuni
vastu-svarüpah), (ii) the cause of the totai matériái energy, (iii) the cause of aii causes.
You are (iv) inferrabie by the reveaiing power of the senses and intelligence, and (v)
only Y o u remain after negating matter and its quaiities.
39. Devotees never give up bhakti: Devotees who have even once tasted a drop of the

become free of desires
•   become the real friends of aii living entities (sarva-bhüta-priya-suhrdi)
•   become expert in achieving the real goal of life (svdrtha-kusald)
•   never return to this matériái world.

•    always engaged in pastimes with Your spiritual and matériái energies
•    existing internaiiy as Para-brahman and externaiiy as the ingredients of the
matériái creation
•    the originál cause of aii causes, manifested in various stages, at different times
and places, and in various bodies
•    the witness of aii activities
•    with a body uncontaminated by matter, like sky
•    the source of Brahman and Paramátmá.
43. Please fulfil our purpose: Since Y o u are omniscient, Y o u know why we have taken
shelter at Your lotus feet, which provide shade that gives relief from aii matériái
disturbances. So, please give us relief hy counteracting our present distress.

4 7 - 5 0 : The Lord condemns matériái desires
4 7 . Y o u r prayers are full i n knowledge. Through them one can remember M y exalted
position and develop bhakti to Me. I am pleased with you.
L i n k : "How unfortunate! You praised me with that knowledge but out of foolishness
you didn't pray for bhakti."
48. Pure devotees do not ask anything but bhakti
kim durdpam mayi prite          O best of the demigods, although nothing is difficult
tathdpi vibudharsabhdh          for one to obtain when I am pleased with him.
mayy ekanta-matir nanyan          a pure devotee, whose mind is exclusively fixed upon
matto vdnchati tattva-vit        Me, does not ask Me for anything hut bhakti.
Note: The Lord regretted that the demigods prayed for an opportunity to kiii their
enemy, instead of unalloyed devotional service.
4 9 . Fools fulfil matériái desires. Misers (krpanas) consider matériái assets to he
everything, and they do not know the ultimate necessity of the soul. If one awards that
which is desired hy such foois, he must also he considered foolish.
50. Devotees discourage k a r m a . A pure devotee wiii never instruct a foolish person to
engage in fruitive activities, like an experienced physician never encourages a patient
to eat food injurious to his heaith, even if the patient desires it.
5 1 - 5 5 : The Lord gives solutioni to the demigods

to the Asvini-kumaras through the head of a horse. Therefore the mantras are called
Asvasira. The Asvini-kumaras then became jtvan-muktas.
53. Dadhici gave the Náráyana-kavaca to Tvastá, who delivered it to his son
Visvarüpa, from whom you (Indra) have received it.
54. When the Asvini-kumáras beg for Dadhici's body on your behaif, he wiii sureiy
give it as he knows dharma. Then, Visvakarmá wiii prepare a thunderbolt from his
hones. This thunderbolt wiii certainly kiii Vrtrásura because it wiii he invested with
M y power.
L i n k : The demigods might say, "But we cannot fight with Vrtrasura who has devoured
everything. You should kill him personally."
55. Vrtrásura will not harm y o u . When Vrtrásura is killed, you wiii regain your
strength, weapons and wealth. He is also a devotee and wiii never he envious of you.

1 3 - 2 9 : T H E DEMIGODS ATTACK THE DEMONS
13-14. Indra took up the thunderbolt manufactured by Visvakarmá, charged with
Dadhici's power, and endowed hy the Lord's potency. Riding on Airavata, surrounded
hy the demigods, and praised hy the sages, Indra appeared glorious, giving deiight to
the three worlds.
15. Indra angrily attacked Vrtrásura, surrounded hy the leaders of demoniac armies,
just as Rúdra had formerly attacked Antaka (Yamarája).
16. Place and time of battle: At the end of Satya-yuga and the beginning of Tretá-
yuga, in the first yuga cycie of the Vaivasvata manvantara, a fierce battle took place
hetween the demigods and the demons (headed hy Vrtrásura) on the bank of the
Narmadá River (in the higher planets).
17-18. A r m y of the demigods: King Indra was shining effuigentiy, carrying the
thunderbolt, surrounded hy Rudras, Vasus, Adityas, Asvini-kumáras, Pitrs, Vahnis,
Maruts, Rbhus, Sádhyas and Visvadevas. It was intoierahie to see for the demons.
19-22. A r m y of the demons: Thousands of demons, demi-demons, Yaksas, Ráksasas,
headed by Sumáii and Maii, and others such as Namuci, Sambara, Anarvá, Dvimürdhá,
Rsahha, Asura, Hayagríva, Sankusirá, Vipracitti, Ayomukha, Pulomá, Vrsaparvá,
Praheti, Heti and Utkaia, roared feariessiy like lions. Aii dressed in golden ornaments,
they gave pain to the demigods with weapons like ciuhs and hiudgeons.
Bhágavata Subodhiní        ^J^^kM^^^^M^MMM^^^.
23. T h e demons attacked the demigods with lances, tridents, axes, swords, sataghnis
and bhusundis and scattered all the chiefs of the demigod armies.
24-26. T h e demigods defeat the demons: The demigods were covered by arrows like
the stars are covered by dense clouds, but they quickly cut those weapons to pieces
before they even reached them. The demons showered mountain peaks, trees and stones
on the demigods, hut they nullified them hy hreaking them to pieces in the sky.
27. T h e demons became afraid when they saw that the soldiers of King Indra were
quite well and not at aii injured by their weapons.
28. Futile attempts: The demons' attack on the demigods, who were protected by Lord
Krsna, was futile, just as the harsh words of low persons do not affect saints.
29. T h e demons decidé to flee: The demons, since they were not devotees of Lord
Krsna, lost their pride in fighting. Leaving aside their leader (Vrtrásura) even in the
very beginning of the fight, they decided to flee.
30-33:   VRTRÁSURA CHASTISES THE FLEEING D E M O N S
30. Vrtrásura, a great-minded hero, seeing his army broken and all the demons fleeing
from the battlefield out of intense fear, smiied and spoke as foiiows.
31. Vrtrásura spoke pieasing, wise words suitable to the time: " O Vipracitti! O
Namuci! O Pulomá! O Maya, Anarvá and Sambara! Please hear me.
32. "Death is unavoidahle and providence has not provided a means to escape it. In
that situation who wiii not accept a glorious death that promotes one to the higher
pianetary systems?

1. T h e demons could not accept the words of Vrtrásura, their commander, who had
advised them in dharma, as they were fearful and intent upon fleeing.
2. T h e demigods chased the demons, taking advantage of this favourable situation
and attacking them from the rear.
3. Vrtrásura rebuked them, with anger and intolerance, as follows:
4. "Attacking the demons is useless. These demoniac soldiers (or you, the demigods)
have come from their mothers' bodies like stool. What is the benefit of killing enemies
from behind if they are fleeing in fear? It is inglorious and cannot bring one to heaven.
5. Stand before me for a moment if you have: faith in your heroism, patience in your
hearts, and no ambition for sense gratification (since I will kill you)."
6 - 1 2 : VRTRÁSURA FIGHTS T H E D E M I G O D S
6-7. Angry Vrtrásura terrified the demigods. When he roared with a resounding
voice, resemhling a lion, the demigods fainted as if struck by thunderbolts.
8. Vrtrásura tramples the demigods: As the demigods closed their eyes in fear,
Vrtrásura, taking up his trident and making the earth tremble, trampled the demigods
like a mad elephant tramples hoUow bamboos.
9. Indra threw a club: Seeing Vrtrásura's disposition, Indra became intolerant and
threw a great club at him, but Vrtrásura easily caught it with his left hand.
10-11. Vrtrásura angrily struck Airávata's head with the same club and roared
loudly. For this heroic deed, the soldiers on both sides glorified him. Spitting blood
from its broken mouth, Airavata was pushed back fourteen yards along with Indra.
12. Vrtrásura refrains from attacking: Seeing the condition of Airavata and
despondent Indra, Vrtrásura, following religious principles, refrained from again
striking Indra with the club. Indra relieved Airavata with his nectar-producing hand.
1.3-17: VRTRÁSURA CHASTISES INDRA
13. Remembering Indra's sinful act of cruelly killing his brother, Vrtrasura became
mad with lamentation and bewilderment. Laughing sarcastically, he spoke as follows:
14. " I w i l l be free from my debt to my brother when I pierce your stone-like heart
with my trident, O abominable one! M y enemy who has killed - a brdhmana, his
spiritual master, and my brother - is now, by good fortune, standing before me!
>m^mmíi£mm£m^mim:i^mm
6.11 T h e Transcendental Qualities of Vrtrásura
15. Y o u acted like a butcher by mercilessly severing the (three) heads of my brother,
who was: self-realized, sinless, a qualified brahmana, your guru and appointed by you
as the chief priest only for the sake of living in the heavenly planets.
16. Vultures w i l l eat your body. Deprived of shame, mercy, glory and good fortune
by your own karmic reactions, you are to be condemned even by Ráksasas. Pierced
with my trident, even fire will not touch your body.
17. I f the other demigods follow you by attacking me, I shall sever their heads. With
those heads I shall perform a sacrifice to Bhairava and his followers.
1 8 - 2 3 : VRTRÁSURA INSTRUCTS INDRA
18. But if you k i l l me and my soldiers, I shall:
i.   be relieved of my debts (anrnah) in this world
ii.   offer my body to other living entities (bhüta-balim)
iii.   attain the dust of the lotus feet of great devotees like Nárada (manasvindm
pdda-rajah prapatsye).
1 9 . Y o u r thunderbolt is infallible. W h y don't you huri it at me? Although your attack
with your club was useless, like a request of money from a miset, your thunderbolt will
not be useless. Have no doubts about this.
L i n k : Indra might think, "Vrtrdsura is cheating me. He will make the thunderbolt
useless by his powers and again attack me." To reassure him, Vrtrdsura says:
2 0 . Y o u r victory is assured: The thunderbolt has been empowered by the prowess of
Lord Visnu and the strength of Dadhici's austerities. Ordered by Visnu, kill your enemy
with it. When Visnu favours a person, victory, opulence and all good qualities are
assured (yato harir vijayah srir gunds tatah).
2 1 . I w i l l attain the destination of sages: By the force of your thunderbolt, I will be
freed of the rope of matériái attachment (grdmya-pdsah) and this world of matériái
desires (apaviddha-lokah). A s L o r d Sahkarsana has said, I will fix my mind upon His
lotus feet and attain the destination of great sages like Nárada Muni.
L i n k : When Lord Sankarsana is pleased, he will not give the opuiences of heaven.
22. T h e L o r d never bestows the great opuiences of heaven, earth or the lower planets,
upon His dedicated servants (ekdnta-dhiydm), for such opuiences cause hatred,
agitation, anxiety, pride, quarrel, calamity and hard labour.

6.12 Vrtrasura's Glorious Death
L i n k : Vrtrasura prepares for battle again, thinking that if he shows his prowess, Indra
will become determined and angry and quickly kill him.
f/////J//^                       V R T R A S U R A F I G H T S WITH INDRA
1 . Vrtrásura attacks I n d r a : Desiring to give up his body, considering death in the battle
preferable to victory, Vrtrásura attacked Indra with his trident just as Kaitabha had
attacked the Lord when the universe was inundated.
2. T h r o w s his trident: Then Vrtrásura whirled his trident forcefully with anger and
threw it at Indra exclaiming loudly, " O sinful one, thus shall I kill y o u ! "
3. Indra cuts off Vrtrásura's a r m : Indra feariessiy cut the blazing trident to pieces
with his thunderbolt and also cut off one of Vrtrásura's snake-like arms.
4. Vrtrásura strikes Indra: Although one of his arms was severed, Vrtrásura angrily
struck Indra on the jaw with an iron mace. He also struck Airávata. Thus Indra
dropped the thunderbolt from his hand.
5. T h e denizens of various planets praised Vrtrásura's deed, but seeing Indra in
danger, they lamented, "Alas! A l a s ! "
6. Vrtrásura encourages I n d r a : Having been practically defeated, Indra felt ashamed
to pick up his thunderbolt. But Vrtrásura encouraged him, saying, "Take up your
thunderbolt and kill your enemy. This is not the time for lamentation."
7-17:   V R T R Á S U R A FNLIGHTENS T H E D I S C O U R A G E D INDRA
7. Continuous victory against enemies is possible only for the Lord or those fully
dependent on H i m . Others, who fight with weapons, are sometimes victorious and
sometimes defeated.
8. T i m e controls all living entities in all planets, including their presiding deities, like
birds are caught in a net. Time is the cause of victory or defeat.
lokdh sapdld yasyeme svasanti vivasd vase
dvijd iva sicd baddhdh sa kdla iha kdranam
9. T i m e controls everything - sensory prowess {ojah), mentái power (sahah), bodily
strength (balam), the living force (prdnam), immortality (amrtam) and death (mrtyum).
Not knowing this, fools think the dull body to be the cause of their activities.
L i n k : The controller of time is the Supreme Lord, the cause of all causes.
Bhágavata Subodhiní j
'^2Sg.ílí?Zí-g§;£^L±g^£!^^
jJ^O. All living beings are under the control of the Lord
yathd dárumayi ndri           As a wooden doll of a woman, or as an animál made of
yathd patramayo mrgah          grass is under the control of a puppeteer.
evam bhutáni maghavann        all living beings are under the control of the supreme
ísa-tantrdni viddhi bhoh     controller. No one is independent.
11. Without the Lord's mercy, the three purusas, prakrti, mahat-tattva, the falsé ego,
the five matériái elements, the matériái senses, the mind, the intelligence and
consciousness cannot create the matériái manifestation.
L i n k : But Mimdmsakas say that the jiva causes the creation by his own actions.
12. Ignorant fools think themselves creators [isvaram), although always dependent
(anisam). It is the Lord who creates and devours all living beings through other living
beings.
Note: If one thinks that according to previous karma, one's matériái body is created by
his father and mother, and that the same body is annihilated by another agent like a
tiger, this is not a proper understanding (considering them to be independent causes).
13. Good or had depends on time
dyuh srih kirtir aisvaryam           A man's longevity, opulence, fame, power
dsisah purusasya yah               and other blessings
bhavanty eva hi tat-kdle            arise according to a favourable time.
Similarly the opposite arises according to
yathdnicchor viparyaydh
time, although one is unwilling.
14. Therefore, one should be equipoised in fame and defamation, victory and defeat,
happiness and distress, life and death.
tasmdd akirti-yasasor jaydpajayayor api
samah sydt sukha-duhkhdbhydm mrtyu-jivitayos tathd
15. Vision of liberated persons: One who knows that the gunas of prakrti - sattva,
rajas and tamás - are not the qualities of the soul, and that the soul is simply an
observer, is not bound by these qualities.
sattvam rajas tama iti prakrter ndtmano gundh
tatra sdksinam dtmdnam yo veda sa na badhyate
16. See my example: Even though I have been defeated, with my weapon and arm cut
to pieces, I am still trying my best to fight, without being morose.
f I        f I W3   f I *tX17. Consider this battle a gambling match, in which our lives are the stakes, the
arrows are the dice, and the animals acting as carriers are the game board. No one can
understand who will be defeated and who will be victorious.
1 8 - 2 2 : INDRA PRAISES V R T R Á S U R A
18. I n d r a praised Vrtrásura, hearing his straightforward and instructive words, and
took up his thunderbolt again. Giving up his amazement, he smiled and spoke.
Note: Indra first thought, "How can a démon have such devotion, knowledge and
detachment?" He was surprised and had no smiie. Then, rememhering Prahlada, Bali
and others, he thought, "Bhakti millions of times greater than mine is possihle even in
the demons." Thus, his amazement disappeared and he hecame joyfui and smiled.
19. Y o u are a perfect devotee. I see it by your discrimination and endurance in bhakti
towards the Lord of the universe, the Supersoul and friend of everyone, despite your
dangerous position.
2 0 . Y o u have surmounted L o r d Visnu's máyá {vaisnavim). Therefore, you have given
up the demoniac mentality and have attained the position of an exalted devotee {mahd-
purusatdm gatah).
21. I t is a great wonder that although you are a démon influenced by passión, you
have firmly fixed your mind on the Supreme Lord Vásudeva (suddha-sattva).
22. Devotees swim i n the ocean of nectar
yasya bhaktir bhagavati              A person fixed in bhakti to the Supreme Lord, Hari, the
harau nihsreyasesvare                Lord of the highest auspiciousness,
vikridato 'mrtdmbhodhau               swims in the ocean of nectar.
kim ksudraih khdtakodakaih             What is the use of the water in small ditches for him?
2 3 - 3 5 : INDRA KILLS VRTRÁSURA
23. T h e fight resumes: Vrtrasura and Indra, both powerful fighters, spoke about
devotional service even on the battlefield, and again began fighting dutifully.
24. Vrtrásura whirled his iron club and threw it at Indra and with his left hand.
25. I n d r a cut to pieces Vrtrásura's club and his remaining hand, with his thunderbolt
named Sataparvan.
26-29. Vrtrásura resembled a mountain with its wings cut off by Indra. He was
bleeding profusely. With his lower jaw on the ground and upper jaw in the sky, with a
Bhágavata Subodhir
mouth deep like the sky, a tongue like that of a large serpent, and teeth fearful like
death, he seemed to devour the entire universe. He shook the mountains, crushing the
surface of the earth, as if he were the Himalayas walking about.
Vrtrásura swallows Indra: He came before Indra and swallowed him along with
Airávata, just as a big python might swallow an elephant.
30. T h e demigods lament: Seeing this, the demigods, along with Brahmá, the
Prajápatis and other great sages became morose, and cried, "What a calamity!"
31. Protected by the Náráyana-kavaca and by his own mystic power, King Indra,
although swallowed by Vrtrásura, did not die within the demon's belly.
32. Indra came out of Vrtrásura's abdomen by piercing it with his thunderbolt. Then
he cut off his head, which was as high as a mountain peak.
33. Indra took one year to cut off Vrtrásura's head, although his thunderbolt
revolved around and cut Vrtrásura's neck with great speed.
Note: When Vrtrásura swallowed Indra, he thought, " N o w I killed Indra. There is no
more need of fighting. Let me go hack to Godhead." Thus he stopped all his hodily
activities and hecame situated in trance. Taking advantage of his silence, Indra pierced
his ahdomen. Actually it was the hody left hy Vrtrásura that was cut hy Indra;
Vrtrásura himseif was not killed. I n his originál consciousness, Vrtrásura returned hack
to Godhead and hecame an associate of Lord Sahkarsana.
34. Celebrations: The Gandharvas and Siddhas beat kettledrums. With Vedic hymns
they celebrated the prowess of Indra, the killer of Vrtrásura, and showered flowers
upon him with great pleasure.
35. T h e living spark (dtma-jyotih) then came forth from Vrtrásura's body and while
all the demigods Iooked on, he entered the transcendental world (alokam), the spiritual
abode of Lord Sahkarsana.

2 2 - 2 3 : Phala-sruti
1 - 3 : PARÍKSIT'S INQUIRY A B O U T INDRA'S UNHAPPINESS
1 . Everyone except Indra was pleased: When Vrtrasura was killed, all the presiding
deities were immediately pleased and free from trouble, except Indra.
2. Everyone returned: Thereafter, the demigods, the sages, the Pitrs, the Bhütas, the
demons, the demigods and their followers, Brahmá and Siva all returned to their
respective homes. But while departing, no one spoke to Indra.
Bhágavata Subodhin,                ,,^,^^^^^^^^^^^^^^iMM,
Note: Indra was not fit to return to his abode for he was disturbed at having killed
Vrtrásura, who was actually a brdhmana. Instead he went to Mánasa-sarovara to
become free of sins. Then he performed an asvamedha-yajita and returned home.
3. Paríksit asked: " W h y was I n d r a unhappy after the killing of Vrtrásura, when the
other demigods were happy?"
4 - 9 : INDRA'S P R E V I O U S TALKS WITH THE SAGES
»^^iXMf^j»xs
4 - 5 : Indra's hesitation to kill Vrtrasura
4. A f r a i d of killing a brahmana, Indra had declined the request of the sages and the
demigods when they asked him to kill Vrtrásura, who was disturbing them.
5. " W h a t about the sinful reactions? When I killed Visvarüpa, I received extensive
reactions, but was favoured by the women, land, trees and water. But now if I kill
Vrtrásura, another brdhmana, how shall I free myself from the reactions?"
6-9: The sages encourage Indra to kill Vrtrásura
6-7. " W e shall perform an asvamedha sacrifice to release you from any sin you may
accrue hy killing the brdhmana. Pieasing Lord Náráyana hy the sacrifice, one can he
relieved even of the sinful reactions for killing the entire world. Do not fear."
8. T h e holy name relieves one of all sins: One who has killed a brdhmana, a cow, his
father, mother or spiritual master can he immediately freed from all sinful reactions hy
chanting the holy name, even if he is a dog-eater or a canddla.
brahma-hd pitr-hd go-ghno mdtr-hdcdrya-hdghavdn
svddah pulkasako vdpi suddhyeran yasya khirtandt
9. B y the asvamedha-yajna, you will he freed of all sins even if you kill brdhmanas,
what to speak of killing a disturbing démon like Vrtrásura."
Note: One should not intentionally commit sinful acts and think himseif free from the
reactions because he is chanting the holy name. Such a mentality is a most abominable
offence at the lotus feet of the holy name, ndmno baldd yasya hi pdpa-buddhih -
committing sinful activity on the strength of the holy name.
10. Sin approached Indra: Encouraged by the sages, Indra killed Vrtrasura. The sinful
reaction for killing a brdhmana (brahma-hatyd) then took shelter of Indra.
11. I n d r a suffered and was unhappy. One who commits a shameful act (hrimantam)
and has hecome infamous {vdcyatdm) cannot he happy despite having good qualities.
12-13. T h e personified sinful reaction chased Indra, in the form of an old canddla
woman afflicted with tuberculosis. With hody covered in hlood, scattered grey hair
and unbearahie fishy hreath that poiiuted the entire street, she called, "Wait! W a i t ! "
14. T h e w i t c h followed h i m wherever he went - in the sky and in all directions. At
last he quickly went to the northeast and entered Mánasa-sarovara Lake.
15. I n d r a lived invisibly i n Mánasa-sarovara Lake for 1000 years always thinking of
how he could he relieved from his sinful reaction. He stayed in the subtle fihers of a
lotus stem, practically starving, because Agni's messenger was too afraid to enter the
water and deliver Indra's share of sacrifice.
Note: It is not difficult for Agni to enter water, since he delivers the share to Varuna
(who lives in water). However, this water was difficult to enter because the followers
of Siva were guarding it. Thus, the sin of killing a brahmana, could also not enter it.
^            1 6 - 2 3 : INDRA R E L I E V E D O F S I N A N D R E I N S T A T E D IN H I S P O S I T I O N
16. Nahusa ruled heaven during Indra's absence, hy his knowledge, austerity and
mystic power. But, hlinded and maddened hy power, he made undesirable proposals to
Indra's wife and was cursed hy a brdhmana to later hecome a snake.
17a. Indra's sins were diminished hy
i.    The influence of Rúdra, guardian of the northeast (dik-devatayd)
ii.    Laksmi, who resides in the lotus, and who gave protection to him
iii.    Meditation on Lord Visnu, hy which he was ultimately relieved of all
reactions for his offence of committing sin on the strength of atonement.
17b-18. T h e n he was called back to heaven hy the brdhmanas. They initiated him to
perform an asvamedha-yajna meant to please the Supreme Lord.
19-20. B y worship of the L o r d in that sacrifice, Indra's sin of killing Vrtrásura was
vanquished, just as fog is vanquished by the sun.
21. I n d r a regained his position hy that sacrifice executed hy Marici and other sages,
and worshipping the Supreme Lord. Thus he was again honoured hy everyone.
22. T h i s great narrative includes: glorification of the Lord, the glories of bhakti,
descriptions of devotees, and Indra's victory and release from sinful life.
23. Phala-s'ruti: The learned are always advised to read this narration and hear it on
sacred days for it brings - active senses, wealth, fame, freedom from all sins, victory,
good fortune and longevity.

6.14 King Citraketu's Lamentation
L i n k : Since Vrtrasura was a démon, Pariksit wondered how he could have become such
an exalted devotee, even on the battlefield.
1 - 8 : PARÍKSIT'S INQUIRY A B O U T VRTRÁSURA B E I N G \ , \ E X A I T E D D E V O T E E
1 . H o w could Vrtrásura develop f i r m attachment to L o r d Narayana, since the
demons are generally sinful and obsessed with the modes of passión and ignorance.
2. R a r i t y of bhakti: Pure devotional service at the lotus feet of Mukunda is rare even
for the demigods situated in the mode of goodness, and pure saints.

3-5. R a r i t y of a devotee (see the above figure)
• I n this matériái world there are as many living entities as atoms.
• Among these, a very few are humans who are interested in following dharma.
•     Out of many persons who follow dharma, only a few desire liberation.
•     Among many thousands who desire liberation, one may actually achieve
liberation (vdnaprastha).
•     And among many thousands of such liberated persons, one who can understand
the true meaning of liberation (sannydsi) is very rare.
•    Among many millions who are liberated and perfect in knowledge of liberation,
one may be a devotee of Lord Náráyana, or Krsna. Such devotees, who are fully
peaceful, are extremely rare.

6-7. T h i s is my doubt. H o w could such an infamous, sinful démon, always troubling
others, become greatly Krsna conscious during the raging battle? I am curious to hear
about Vrtrásura who showed prowess and satisfied Indra in the battle.
8. Answered w i t h affection: After hearing Mahárája Pariksit's very intelligent
question, Sukadeva Gosvámi began answering his disciple with great affection.
9 - 1 3 : CITRAKETU ANXIOUS FOR A       SoN^^^Jjjjjj^^
9. Please listen with attention to the history that I have heard from the mouths of
Vyásadeva, Nárada and Devala.
10. K i n g Citraketu, of the province of Súrasena, ruled the entire earth. During his
reign, the earth produced all the necessities for life.
11. N o children: He had ten millión wives, hut none of them produced a child.
Note: King Citraketu married one wife and when she could not heget a child he married
another. Like that he married ten millión wives to have offspring and not for enjoyment.
12-13. F u l l of anxiety: Though endowed with beautiful form, magnanimity, youth,
high birth, education, wealth and opulence, he was full of anxiety because he did not
have a son. His wealth, beautiful queens and control of all lands were not sources of
happiness for him.
1 4 - 2 2 : ANGIRA RSI ARRIVES AND Q U E S T I O N S CITRAKETU
14. A r r i v a l of A n g i r a R s i : Once, the powerful sage Afigirá, travelling all over the
universe, hy his sweet will came to the palace of King Citraketu.
15. Citraketu receives Angira: Citraketu immediately stood up and received him with
worship and offerings. When the rsi was seated comfortahly, Citraketu sat down.
16. Angira congratulated C i t r a k e t u for his humility and hospitality when tlie king
was howing at his lotus feet. He addressed him as follows:
ww                6.14 King Citraketu's Lamentation
1 7 - 2 1 : Aiigirá to Citraketu
17. Is everything well? Are your body, mind, associates and paraphernalia in proper
condition? When the seven properties of matériái nature (mahat-tattva, ahankara, and
the five tan-mdtras) are in proper order, the living entity within the matériái elements
is happy. Similarly, a king is protected hy seven elements - his guru, ministers,
kingdom, fort, treasury, friends and system of law and order.
18. M u t u a l dependency: When a king directly depends upon his associates and follows
their instructions, he is happy. Similarly, when his associates offer their gifts and
activities to the king and follow his orders, they are also happy.
1 9 . Are you i n control of your wives, citizens, secretaries, servants, merchants,
ministers, palace inhahitants, governors, landholders, sons and other dependants?
2 0 . I f the king's mind is fully controlled, everyone will be under his control. His
provinciai governors and others offer tribute to him.
2 1 . Y o u r pale face reflects anxiety. I see that your mind is not pleased due to yourself
or others. Y o u seem not to have achieved your desired goal.
22. Although the great sage A n g i r a knew everything, he inquired in this way. Thus
Citraketu, desiring a son, bent low in great humility and spoke as follows:
2 3 - 2 6 : CITRAKETU EXPLAINS THE C A U S E O F H I S ANXIETY

regarding the emhodied, conditioned souls.

aware of everything.
2 5 . M y opuiences do not please me, although they are desirahle even for the

is not pleased hy garlands and sandalwood pulp.
26. Please help me get a son and thus save me and my forefathers, who are descending
to the darkness of hell due to my not having progeny.
2 7 - 3 7 : CITRAKETU'S JOY U P O N RECEIVINGA S O N
27-28.  M e r c i f u l A n g i r a performed a sacrifice by offering oblations of sweet rice to
Tvastá. He gave the remnants of the food offered in the yajna to Krtadyuti, the first
(jyesta) and most perfect (sresta) among Citraketu's millions of queens.

29. Harsa-soka: Angira told Citraketu that he will have a son who will be cause of
both juhilation and lamentation (harsa-soka-prada) and then left.
Note: Citraketu understood Arigirá's words in this way: "There will certainly be
jubilation because of the birth of a son, but because be will be my only son, be will be
proud of bis great wealth and empire, and will not be very obedient. I will not be able
to control bim, since be will have more power than me, wbicb will bring me grief."
30-31. Krtadyuti became pregnant after receiving semen from Citraketu, having eaten
remnants of food from the yajna. She gradually developed in her pregnancy, just as the
moon develops during the bright fortnight.
32. A son was born in due course of time. Hearing this news, all the inhahitants of the
state of Súrasena were extremely pleased.
33-35. Citraketu was pleased. He engaged brdhmanas in offering benedictions to the
child and performing the birth ceremony. He generously gave them charity of gold,
silver, garments, ornaments, villages, horses, elephants and six hundred millión cows.
Like a cloud pours water, he gave all desirahle things to everyone, to increase the
reputation, opulence and longevity of his son.
36-37a. T h e parents' affection for the son: Citraketu's affection for his son increased
daily, just like a poor man's affection for money that he earned with great difficulty.
Krtadyuti developed extrémé affection for her son, out of illusion.
37b. T h e other queens were agitated as if hy high fevers, with a desire to have sons.
3 8 - 4 3 : K R T A D Y U T I ' S C O - W I V E S P O I S O N HER S O N
38. Citraketu neglected his other wives, as he fostered his son very carefully. His
affection for Krtadyuti increased.
39. T h e unhappy queens condemned themselves in envy because of the distress of
heing sonless and the king's neglect. They said:
40. " A sonless wife is neglected hy her husband and treated like a servant hy co-wives
who have children. Certainly such a woman is condemned as sinful.
41. Even maidservants of husband are honoured hy him. But we are most unfortunate
like the maids of a maidservant."
42. T h e i r envy became strong, being neglected hy their husband and seeing the
opulence of Krtadyuti in possessing a son.
6.14 King Citraketu's Lamentation
43. T h e y administered poison: Out of envy (vidvesa) they lost their intelligence
(nasta-matayah) and became hard-hearted (ddruna-cetasah) and intolerant
(durmarsdh) of the king's neglect. Finally, they administered poison to the son.
4 4 - 6 1 : LAMENTATION O F KRTADYUTI AND O T H E R S
44-45. Unaware of the sinful act of her co-wives, Krtadyuti thought that her son had
been sleeping for a long time and ordered the nurse to bring him.
46-47a. T h e maidservant realized that the child was dead, seeing him without life
and eyes turnéd upwards. She immediately feli to the ground. I n great agitation, she
struck her hreasts and cried loudly.
47a-48. Krtadyuti came immediately, and saw that her son was dead. I n great
lamentation, her hair and dress in disarray, she feli to the ground unconscious.
49. Everyone laments: A l l the inhahitants of the palace came and hegan to cry. The
queens who had administered the poison also cried pretentiously.
50-51. Citraketu's great lamentation: When Citraketu heard of his son's death, he
hecame almost blind. His lamentation grew like a blazing fire. Slipping and falling, he
approached the dead child and feli unconscious at his feet, with hair and dress
scattered. Regaining consciousness, his eyes were tearful, and he could not speak.
52-53. K r t a d y u t i , seeing her husband's lamentation and the dead child, lamented in
various ways. This increased the pain of all the inhahitants of the palace. The garland
on her head feli and her hair scattered. Tears melted the collyrium on her eyes and
moistened her hreasts. Her loud crying resembled the wailing of a kurari bird.
54-55: Krtadyuti condemns providence
54. Alas, O Providence, Y o u are certainly inexperienced (bdliso) in creation, for
during the lifetime of a father, Y o u have caused the death of his son. This contradicts
Your creative laws and makes Y o u the enemy of living entities.

1 - 8 : NÁRADA AND ANGIRÁ INSTRUCT CITRAKETU
1 . When Citraketu lay Üke a dead body next to the dead body of his son, Narada and
Angirá instructed him about spiritual consciousness (sat-uktibhih) as follows:
2. N o factual reiationship: What reiationship does the dead body, for which you
lament, have with you? And what reiationship do you have with him? Did this
reiationship exist before? Does it truly exist now? W i l l it continue in the future?
3. Union and separation of the jivas by the force of time
yatha prayanti sarhyanti       As small particles of sand come together and
sroto-vegena bdlukdh          are separated by the force of the waves,
samyujyante viyujyante        the living entities sometimes come together and
tathd kdlena dehinah         are sometimes separated by the force of time. j
4. Artificial reiationship of parenthood depends on Lord's máyá
yathd dhdndsu vai dhdnd         Just as grains appear or do not appear from
bhavanti na bhavanti ca         seeds,
evam bhütdni bhutesu         children appear or do not appear from fathers,
coditdnisa-mdyayd           impelled by the Lord's mdyd.
5. A l l matériái relationships are temporary: Both you and we, as well as everything
moving and non-moving existing at this time, were not together before birth and will
not be together after death. Even now we are not together.
Note: That which does not exist in all three phases of time cannot be called substantial.
A dream does not exist before one fails asleep, nor does it continue after one wakes up.
Tbe period for dreaming exists only between tbese two states and therefore it is falsé
in tbe sense tbat it is impermanent. Similarly tbe relationships in tbis world are
temporary, tbougb not falsé.
6. T h e Lord's play: The Lord, though not interested, still, like a child playing, creates,
maintains and destroys all beings by other beings (such as fathers create, kings maintain
and snakes destroy), who are created by H i m (dtma-srstaih) and are dependent on H i m
(asvatantraih).
L i n k : Citraketu may say, "1 as a father, produced a son. Because he is dead now, I am
lamenting."

so from one body (the father), through another body (the mother), a third body (the

21-23. Gandharva-nagara: N o w you are actually experiencing the misery of a parent.
One's wife, house, kingdom, opuiences, sense objects and others are all temporary and
are the causes of lamentation (soka), illusion (moha), fear (bhaya) and distress (drta).
They are like a gandharva-nagara, and they are no better than dreams (svapna),
illusions (mdyd), and mentái concoctions (mano-rathdh).
24. T h e mind is the cause of actions: Visible objects that we see are like dreams and
mentái concoctions with no permanent existence. Only due to our past actions do we
create such mentái concoctions that in turn make us perform further activities.

12. Freed from lamentation: After the jiva had spoken in this way and left, Citraketu
and all others were astonished. Thus they cut off their shackles of affection
(chittvdtma-sneha-srnkhaldm) and gave up their lamentation.
13. G i v i n g up affection: After the reiatives had performed the funeral ceremonies,
they gave up the affection that leads to illusion, lamentation, fear and pain, and which
is difficult to give up.
14. Krtadyuti's co-wives were ashamed and lost their bodily lusters. While lamenting,
they remembered the instructions of Ahgirá and gave up their ambition to hear
children. They atoned for the killing of the child (bdla-hatyd-vratam) on the bank of
the Yamuná River as directed by brdhmanas.

elephant emerges from mud.

his senses and mind, Citraketu then offered his respects to Ahgirá and Nárada.

devotee and surrendered soul, Nárada spoke the following instructions:

21-25: Various features of the Lord
21. Impersonal Brahman: The Lord is: (?) unapproachable by words and mind, [ii) one
entity, {iii) entirely spiritual without matériái names or forms and {iv) the cause of the
matériái world. May He protect us!
22. Cause of the universe: Obeisances unto Brahman from whom the universe arises,
in whom it remains, and into whom it dissolves, just as earth is the cause of clay pots.
23. Beyond the senses: The Supreme Brahman emanates from the Lord and expands
like the sky, existing inside and outside, untouched by anything matériái. Yet the mind,
intelligence, senses and living force can neither touch nor know H i m .
24. Source of consciousness: Only when infused with a partiele of consciousness by
the Lord, the body, senses, living force, mind and intelligence, although matériái, can
function. As iron cannot burn unless beated by fire, the bodily senses cannot act unless
favoured by the Supreme Brahman.
25. Served by devotees: Respects to the Lord, who is the supreme enjoyer {mahd-
purusdya), the great authority (mahdnubhdvdya) and the master of all mystic power
(mahd-vibhüti-pataye). His lotus feet are always massaged by a multitude of the best
devotees with their lotus-bud hands and He is situated in the topmost spiritual planet
iparama-paramesthin).
2 6 - 3 3 : CITRAKETU ATTAINS THE LORD'S DARSANA
26. Nárada and Ahgirá leave to Brahmaloka, having fully instructed the surrendered
devotee {prapanndya), Citraketu.
27. Citraketu's practice: Fasting and drinking only water, Citraketu continuously
chanted for one week, with great attention, the mantra given by Nárada Muni.
28. Intermediate result: Citraketu achieved the rulership of the Vidyádhara planet.
29. Primary result: His mind became increasingly eniightened (iddha-manah-gatih),
and he attained shelter at the lotus feet of L o r d Sesa (Anantadeva).
