3 2 . Unable to offer prayers: With tears of love (premdsru), he moistened the resting
place of the Lord's lotus feet {paddbja-vistaram). Because his voice was choked in
ecstasy, for a considerable time he was unable to utter any of the letters of the alphabet
{akhila-varna) to offer the Lord suitable prayers (prasamiditum).
33. He regains his speech: Thereafter, by controliing his mind and restricting his senses,
he recovered his speech and offered prayers to the Lord, who is the spiritual master of
all (jagad-gurum) and the personification of scriptures (sdtvata-sdstra-vigraham).
3 4 - 4 9 : CITRAKETU'S PRAYERS T O THE L O R D
3 4 . Y o u and Y o u r devotees conquer each other: O unconquerable Lord (ajita), Y o u
are certainly conquered (jitah) by devotees who have control of the mind and senses.
You also conquer them and mercifully (ati karunah) give Yourself to them, who desire
no matériái profit (bhajatdm akdmdtmandm) from Y o u .
3 5 - 3 7 : Powers of the Lord
3 5 . Falsé lordship of others: This universe and its creation, maintenance and
annihilation are all but Your opuiences. The other creators who are just portions of a
portion of Y o u , compete in vain (spardhanti) due to their consciousness as separate
lords (prthag abhimatyd).
6.16. King Citraketu Meets the Supreme Lord
36. Y o u exist i n all time: Y o u exist in the beginning, middle and end of everything,
from the atom to the universes. Yet Y o u are eternal, having no beginning, end or
middle. Y o u are steady as the cause of all effects (sattvdnám) in these three phases, and
thus Y o u are permanent (dhruvam).
37. Y o u exist i n all space: Innumerable universes, covered by seven layers each ten
times thicker than the previous, move about like atoms in Y o u . Therefore Y o u are
called Ananta (unlimited).
L i n k : After glorifying the Lord, Citraketu glorifies the devotee. Before that he criticizes
the nondevotee.
38. T h e benefits of demigod worship are temporary: Animal-like men thirsty for
sense enjoyment worship the demigods and not Y o u . However, the benedictions
received from those demigods are destroyed when the demigods are destroyed.
39. Even motivated worship to Y o u destroys samsara: If men obsessed with matériái
desires worship Y o u , who are the source of all knowledge {jndndtmany) and are
transcendental to matériái qualities (agunamaye), they are not subject to matériái
rebirth, just as sterilized or fried seeds do not produce plants.
40. Devotees accept Bhágavata-dharma: O unconquerable one, when Y o u explain
Bhdgavata-dharma, the faultless religious system (anavadyam), Y o u conquer the
devotees. Because they have no matériái desires (niskincandh) and are self-satisfied
(dtmdrdmdh), they accept this process to achieve Your shelter.
4 1 . Bhágavata-dharma vs. other dharmas: I n Bhdgavata-dharma (bhakti-yoga) there
is no conception of difference (visama-matih) such as " Y o u and I " or "Yours and
Mine." But such conceptions exist in other systems (kdmya-dharma), which are impure
(avisuddhah), temporary (ksayisnuh) and full of irreligion (adharma-bahulah).
42. Envy-producing religion benefits no one: A religious system that produces envy
of one's own self (sva-drohdt) and causes pain to others (para-sampidayd) only - (?)
arouses Your anger (tava kopah) and (??) makes one sinful (adharmah).
43. Qualities of Aryans:
?. Followers of Bhdgavata-dharma, under Your supervision
??. Equal to all jivas, moving and nonmoving, high and low (aprthag-dhiyah)
iii. Worshippers of Y o u
iv. Never deviating from the highest goal of life.
44a. Darsana: It is not impossible for one to be immediately freed from all matériái
contamination just by seeing Y o u .
na hi bhagavann aghatitam idath tvad-darsandn nrndm akhila-pdpa-ksayah
44b. H o l y name: By hearing Your holy name only once, even candalas are freed from
all matériái contamination.
yan-ndma sakrc chravandt pukkaso 'pi vitnucyate sathsdrdt
45. Nárada's mercy: Therefore, simply by Your glancé [tvad avaloká), my heart has
been completely purified. H o w can what Nárada told me not come true?
46. Y o u know everything {viditam ananta samastam) about the actions of living
entities, for Y o u are the Supersoul {jagad-dtmano) and the supreme guru (parama
guru). There is nothing for me to inform Y o u , just as a glow-worm's light is nothing
in the presence of the sun.
47. Materialists can't recognize Y o u : I offer respects to Y o u , the master of creation,
maintenance and destruction, the supreme swan (paramahathsdya), whose position is
difficult to understand for materialistic persons with a separatist mentality (bhidd).
48. Obeisances to the thousand hooded L o r d - (?) after whose actions (svasantam)
the creators of the universe can use their action senses (svasanti), (??) after whose
perception (cekitdnam), the knowledge senses can gather knowledge (uccakanti), and
(???) on whose head all the universes rest like mustard seeds!
49. Anantadeva, being very pleased with the prayers offered by Citraketu, the King
of the Vidyádharas, replied to him as follows:
50. Y o u have become perfect: By the instructions of Nárada and Angirá, you have
gained transcendental knowledge and are able to see Me.
51a. T h e supreme source: I am the source and soul of all beings who are M y
expansions.
51b. Sabda-brahma and páram brahma: Scripture or the transcendental vibrations
and the impersonal Brahman or the Deity are M y eternal forms.
52. T h e jiva and the world: The jiva thinks he is the enjoyer of this world and the
world is expanded as the source of enjoyment for the jiva. Both are produced by Me,
pervaded by Me and thus rest on Me.
53-54. Jágarti, svapna, susupti: The wakefulness, dreaming and deep sleep states of
the jiva are but energies of the Supreme Lord. One should therefore remember the
originál creator of these states, the Supreme Lord who is unaffected by them.
55. Supersoul: Know me to be the all-pervading Supersoul through whom a sleeping
person can understand his dreaming condition and his happiness beyond the activities
of the matériái senses. I am the cause of activities of the living entity.
56. Knowers of both states: The living entity is Brahman in quality and is a part of
the Lord, the Supreme Brahman. Thus both the living entity and the Lord, are knowers
of the activities of dreams and wakeful states.

58. Humán birth especially in the land of India facilitates Vedic knowledge and its
practical realization (jndna-vijndna-sambhavdm). I n such a position, if one does not
attain self-realization. it is not possihle in any other position.
59. Freedom through bhakti
smrtvehdyám pariklesam Rememhering the great misery in fruitive activities,
tatah phala-viparyayam which brings results opposite to those desires, and
abhayam capy anihayarh knowing a fearless condition arising from action free from
sankalpdd viramet kavih desires, a wise man should cease from matériái desires.
60. N o way out: A husband and wife work to get happiness and mitigate distress, hut
it never happens, because they are full of desires.
61-62. One should become a devotee hy:
i.   Understanding that karma produces opposite results
ii.   Knowing that self-realization is beyond the wakeful, dreaming and deep
sleep states
iii.   Freeing oneself from sense ohjects seen and heard hy one's discrimination
iv.    Being satisfied with Vedic knowledge and practical application.
63. T h e ultimate goal of life: Humans, hy their intelligence fixed in the process of
bhakti-yoga must understand will all their efforts the Lord and the jiva, who are one
in quality (para-dtma-eka-darsanam).
64. Y o u w i l l attain M e : O King, hy accepting M y instruction and gaining M y
transcendental knowledge and realization, you will achieve Me at the end of life.
65. T h e L o r d disappeared: After thus instructing Citraketu, the Supreme Lord,
Sahkarsana, disappeared from that place as Citraketu Iooked on.

1 - 1 0 : CITRAKETU L A U G H S AT L O R D SIVA
1-3. C i t r a k e t u travelled in outer space as the head of the Vidyádharas. With strong
body and senses, and while chanting the glories of Lord H a r i , he enjoyed life with the
women of Vidyádhara-loka, in the valleys of Mt. Sumeru, for millions of years.
4-5. C i t r a k e t u sees L o r d Siva: Once, while travelling in an airplane given by the Lord,
Citraketu saw Lord Siva in an assembly of sages, embracing Parvati on his lap. He
laughed loudly and spoke within the hearing of Parvati.
6-8. Citraketu's comments on Lord Siva:           3KKKIKHfK/KIKHK^^
6a    loka-guruh - Spiritual master of people who follow Vedic instructions
dharmam vaktd - Speaker of religion
sartrinám áste mukhyah - Chief of all embodied living entities
6b    Yet how wonderful it is that he is embracing Parvati, in midst of an assembly of
great saints!
7a   jata-dharas tivra-tapá - Hair matted, undergone great austerities
brahmavádi-sabhá-patih     - President in the assembly of Vedic followers
7b    Yet he is seated with his wife on his lap as if he were a shameless ordinary humán.
8a    maha-vrata-dharo - Master of austerities
8b    Ordinary people generally embrace and enjoy their wives' company in solitary
places. How wonderful it is that he is embracing his wife openly!
Note: Citraketu was not an offender, he merely expressed his wonder. Although
Citraketu criticized Siva, he did not offend Siva like Daksa. Daksa considered Lord Siva
insignificant, but Citraketu expressed his wonder at Siva's being situated in that way.
Citraketu appreciated how Siva was unaffected even in that situation.
9. L o r d Siva smiled and remained silent after hearing Citraketu's words. The
assembly members followed his example and did not speak.
Note: Citraketu's intention - "Siva is Lord. There is no harm if he performs
apparently wrong acts. But ignorant people will misunderstand and criticize him, and
thus will be destroyed for their offence (like Daksa). So, I should establish that he has
proper conduct. He should not have infamy." Lord Siva and the assembly there
understood this intention of Citraketu and kept silent. If Citraketu's words were
blasphemy of Lord Siva, the assembly would have blocked their ears and left.
10. Citraketu's tinge of pride: Not knowing the powers (atad-virya) of Lord Siva and
Mother Parvati, Citraketu criticized them and thought himself to be a better controller
of the senses than Siva (nirjitdtmábhimánine). Parvati angrily spoke.
Note: Somewhat proud - Although Citraketu never meant to insult Siva, he should
not have criticized the Lord, despite his transgressing of social customs. Citraketu was
somewhat proud at having achieved Lord Sankarsana's favour and therefore thought,
fearlessly, that he could criticize even Lord Siva.
1 1 - 1 5 : M O T H E R PÁRVATÍ CHASTISES A N DCURSES C I T R A K E T U
11. Has this upstart been appointed as ruler, punisher, master and restrainer for
shameless persons like us?
12. Others do not know dharma! I suppose Brahma, Bhrgu, Narada, the Kumaras,
Manu, and Kapila have all forgottén dharma as they did not prevent Lord Siva's
improper behaviour.
13. H e must be punished. Impudently he has surpassed the demigods by insulting the
exalted Siva, the guru of the universe and most auspicious personality, upon whose
lotus feet the demigods meditate.
14. He doesn't deserve the shelter of Lord Visnu's lotus feet, which are worshipped by
saints. He is püffed up, thinking himself a great devotee.
15. Become a démon: O impudent one, my dear son, take birth as a démon so that you
will not commit such an offence again towards exalted persons.
Note: A mother's punishment to a misbehaving son is actually out of natural affection.
For Citraketu, Párvati's curse was a boon, because after taking birth as the démon
Vrtrasura, he was promoted directly to Vaikuntha. Being born as Vrtrasura increased
his prema, since the devotees at the levél of prema do not consider any difference
between obtaining a body of a devotee or démon.
1 6 - 2 5 : CITRAKETU SPEAKS T O M O T H E R PÁRVATÍ
16. Citraketu descended from his airplane, bowed down before Parvati with great
humility, and pleased her completely. He said:
17.1 accept the curse with földed hands, O mother. Happiness and distress are given
by the demigods as result of one's past deeds.
Note: Lord Sankarsana wanted Citraketu to return to Vaikuntha as soon as possible.
So, out of fatherly affection He inspired Parvati to curse Citraketu to:
• Announce the great strength of a devotee in seeing curses and blessings, heaven,
hell and liberation equally.
• Remove the improper life of Citraketu as the leader of the Vidyádharas.
• Increase Citraketu's hunger for prema by the fire of separation.
• Bestow upon him the great sweetness of direct service in Vaikuntha.
fi.17 Mother Parvati Curses Citraketu
L i n k : "This is the nature of the wheel of samsara. It is not surprising. Therefore, my
dear mother, neither you nor I am to be blamed for this incident."
18. Transmígration in matériái existence
!        sarhsdra-cakra etasmin            In this wheel of matériái existence,
jantur ajhdna-mohitah             the living entity being deluded by ignorance.
bhramyan sukham ca duhkham ca          wanders, undergoing happiness and distress
bhunkte sarvatra sarvadd           as a result of past deeds, everywhere, always.
19. No one causes happiness and distress
naivdtmd na paras cdpi            Neither the living entity himself nor others
kartd sydtsukha-duhkhayoh           are the cause of matériái happiness and distress.
kartdrarh manyate 'trdjfia         Due to ignorance, he considers himself or the
dtmdnam páram eva ca              others as the cause.
20. Dualities have no eternaí effect
guna-pravdha etasmin             In this flowing river of matériái modes:
kah sdpah ko nv anugrahah          What is a curse and what is a blessing?
kah svargo narakah ko vd           What is heaven and what is hell?
kim sukham duhkham eva vd            What is happiness and what is distress?
Note: The matériái world is like a salt mine, in which one tastes only salt wherever he
goes. Similarly, the so called happiness in this world is also misery.
L i n k : "Who throws a person into the river ofgunas, and who delivers him?"
21. T h e L o r d , unaffected by maya, creates the jivas, their bondage [bandham),
liberation (moksam), happiness (sukham) and distress (duhkham) by His own energy.
L i n k : "Does the Lord favour somé jivas and hate others by binding up somé                 and
liberating others?" No!
22. The Lord's supreme equanimíty
na tasya kascid dayitah pratipo        No one is dear to Him, nor is one His enemy,
no one is His friend or relative nor He
na jhati-bandhur na paro na ca svah
considers anyone His own or separate.
samasya sarvatra nirahjanasya          He is equal everywhere and is unaffected.
He has no attachment for happiness. Then
sukhe na ragah kuta eva rosah
where is the question of anger?
'irt.
L i n k : "If the Lord is equal, why does He make one person happy and cause grief to
another?"
23. For the continuation of materialistic life, the Lord creates duality (happiness/
distress, profit/loss, bondage/liberation and birth/death) although He is neutral.
24. Please forgive me {ksamyatam) for my improper speech (asddhüktam).                I do not
please you just to get free from the curse, which is due to my past deeds.
25. Citraketu left in his airplane after thus satisfying Lord Siva and Párvatí, who
looked on in astonishment.
2 6 - 3 8 : L O R D SIVA'S TALKS T G PÁRVATÍ
26. T h e n Siva spoke to Parvati as Narada, the demons, Siddhas and others listened:
27. Greatness of devotees: Dear heautiful Párvatí! Have you seen the greatness of the
servants of Lord Visnu, who performs astonishing acts (adbhuta karmának)} Devotees
(mahdtmdnám) are not interested in any kind of matériái happiness (nihsprhdndm).
Note A : Párvati's beauty - Lord Siva indicated that Párvati is heautiful, but the beauty
of the devotees is greater.
Note B: Visnu's astonishing act - He caused Párvati to be offended and utter a curse.
He showed the greatness of His devotee Citraketu (/) whose prema did not decrease at
all and («) who remained indifferent even when cursed.
Note C : No effect of the curse - If Párvati's curse cannot give the slightest
unhappiness to Citraketu, then that curse is actually powerless.
28. Devotees never fear any condition of life. For them heaven, hell or liberation are
all the same. They are interested only in the service of Lord Náráyana.
ndrdyana-pardh sarve        na kutascana bibhyati
svargdpavarga-narakesv        api tulydrtha-darsinah
29-30. Conditioned state and dualities: The living entities are conditioned in the
matériái body through the Lord's mdyd. The dualities of happiness and distress, birth
and death and curses and blessings are natural by-products. One identifies with them
only due to lack of mature consideration (aviveka-krtah).
31. B h a k t i automatically produces knowledge and detachment. Therefore devotees
are never interested in the so-called happiness and distress of this world.
vdsudeve bhagavati bhaktim udvahatdth nrndm
jndna-vairdgya-virydndm  na hi kascid vyapdsrayah

32. Independent mentality: Those who consider themselves independent, separate
controllers (prthag-isa-mdninah) cannot understand the identity of the Supreme Lord.
Lven Brahma, I (Siva), Narada and the great sages cannot.
33. The Lord is affectionate to all                                                   ^^^^^H
na hy asydsti priyah kascin    No one is dear to Him, not is anyone inimical.
ndpriyah svah paro 'pi vd      No one is His relative and no one is alien to Him.

sarva-bhüta-priyo   harih     Thus He is the auspicious and dear friend of all.
34-35. Both C i t r a k e t u and I are very dear to the Lord {acyuta priyah). So, one should
not be astonished by seeing the activities of the Lord's devotees (mahdpurusa-bhaktesu)
who are peaceful (sdntesu) and equal to all (sama-darsisu).
36. Párvati was pacified hearing her husband's speech. Thus she gave up her
astonishment at Citraketu's behaviour and became steady in her intelligence.
37. Standard behaviour of a Vaisnava (sádhu-laksanam): King Citraketu, although
capable (alantamah), did not countercurse Párvati, but humbly accepted her curse and
bowed before Siva and Párvati. Then he left.
38. Citraketu becomes V r t r a s u r a : That same Citraketu later appeared in the sacrifice
of Tvastá as the démon Vrtrásura, endowed with transcendentai knowledge and
practical realization.
39. T h u s I have explained how Vrtrásura was born as démon, as you had inquired.
40. Freedom from bondage: One who hears about the pure devotee Citraketu from a
devotee will be freed from the conditional life of matériái existence.
41. Back to Godhead: One who recites Citraketu's history in the morning, controlling
his words and mind and remembering the Supreme Lord, will certainly go back to
Godhead.

2 0 - 3 1 : PARÍKSIT'SQUESTION ABOUT
THE MARUTS A N D DITI'S SERVICE T O KASYAPA
20-21. Pariksit inquired: W h y did Indra convert persons born with a demoniac
mentality into demigods? Did they perform any rituals or pious activities? I and all the
sages present here are eager to know this. Kindly explain.
22. T h e question is appreciated: After hearing Pariksit speak respectfully (adrtam),
precisely (alpam) and with words having many meanings (artha-vat), omniscient
Sukadeva Gosvami, praised his endeavour with great pleasure and replied.
Note: Srila Visvanatha Cakravarti Thákura points out three meanings for this story.
/.   By performing tamasic bhakti with the goal of harming others, one becomes
purified in heart. Along with developing bhakti without tamás, one gains
liberation and gives up the tendency for violence to others. E.g. Diti.
ii. Those with crooked hearts, who see faults in others, though they are
intelligent, do not see those same faults in themselves. E.g. Diti.
Hi. A learned person can be cheated by the attraction of a woman. E.g. Kasyapa.
23. Diti's anger: Diti was overwhelmed with lamentation and anger, since her two
sons, Hiranyáksa and Hiranyakasipu, had been killed by Visnu to help Indra.
2 4 - 2 6 : Diti's contemplation
24. When w i l l I rest w i t h a pacified mind after killing Indra, who is: a killer of his
brothers (bhrdtr-hantdram),        a sense enjoyer (indriydrdmam),      cruei (ulbanam),
hardhearted (aklinna-hrdayam) and sinful (pdpam).
25. T h e envious do not know the true interest of life: When dead, the bodies of all
the rulers (isa-abhihitasya) will be transformed into worms (if left to rot), stool (if eaten
by dogs) or ashes (if hurut). One who enviously kills others for protecting such a body,
does not know his own benefit, since he will go to hell as a result.
krmi-vid-bhasma-sathjndsid yasyesdbhihitasya ca
bhüta-dhruk tat-krte svdrtham kim veda nirayo yatah
26. I wish to have a son who can remove Indra's pride, who is considering his body
eternal (dhruvam), and thus has become unrestrained (unnaddha-cetasah).
27-28. D i t i acted to satisfy Kasyapa: Thinking thus, Diti began constantly acting to
satisfy Kasyapa. With her service, humility, self-control, sweet and charming words,
smiles and glances, she gained control over his mind.
Í..IH Diti Vows to Kill imii.i
29. Though learned, Kasyapa was captivated (jadibhüto) by Diti's artificial behaviour
and thus assured to fulfil her desires. Such a promise by a husband is not astonishing
behaviour towards a wife (na tac citrarh hi yositi).
30. Brahma created women to captivate man's mind: I n the beginning of creation.
Lord Brahma, wanting to increase population and seeing that all living entities were
unattached, created woman from the better half of man's body, by whom the men's
minds are carried away.
vilokyaikdnta-bhütdni bhütdny ddau prajdpatih
striyam cakre sva-dehdrdham yayd pumsdm matir hrtd
31. Powerful Kasyapa, extremely pleased (parama-pritah) by the mild behaviour of
his wife Diti, smiled and spoke to her as follows:
32-43:   D I T I ' S DESIRE T O KILL INDRA A N D KAS'YAPA'S REFLECTIONS
32- 3 6 : Kasyapa M u n i to Diti
32. A s k for a benediction since I am very much pleased by your behaviour. If a
husband is pleased, what desires are difficult for his wife to obtain?
33- 34. A husband is the supreme demigod (daivatam paramam), representative of
the Lord and object of worship for a woman, just as Lord Vásudeva is worshipped
through the various names and forms of the demigods by fruitive workers.
35. T h e duty of a devoted wife: A wife devoted to her husband (pati-vratd ndryah),
desiring the highest benefit (sreyas-kdmdh), should very devoutly worship her husband
as a representative of Vásudeva.
3 6 . 1 shall fulfil your desires: Because you have worshipped me with devotion, I shall
reward you by fulfilling your desires, which are unobtainabie for an unchaste wife.
37. Diti's request: If you want to give me a benediction, I ask you for an immortal
(amrtyum) son who can kill Indra (indra-hanam), the killer my two sons.
Note: The word indra-hanam means "one who can kill Indra," but it also means "one
who follows Indra." The word amrtyum means "not to be killed by anyone" but it also
indicates "one who would be equal in status to the demigods."
38. Kasyapa laments: Upon hearing Diti's request, sorrowful Kasyapa lamented,
"Alas, the great danger of the impious act has come to me now."
'-rtx.5E.»^rtXt5*j'^v5^>.'rtxe3"j-'**Vc51.>^rtv5»j^3 9 - 4 3 : Kasyapa reflects on women's nature
39. I w i l l fali down to hell: Attached to matériái enjoyment (arthendriydrámo), my
mind has been captivated by mdyd in the form of my wife (yosin-mayyeha mdyayd).
Therefore, I am surely a wretched (krpanah) person who will glide down toward hell.
40. A l l condemnation upon me! What offence is there if a woman follows her nature
(svabhdvam iha yositah)} However, I am to be condemned since I am ignorant of my
own benefit (abudham svdrthe) and could not control my senses (ajitendriyah).
41. Who can understand the dealings of women, who have f aces like lotuses blooming
in autumn, words sweet to the ears, but sharp hearts like razor blades?
42. N o one is actually dear to women. They are supposed to be very saintly, but for
their own interests they can kill even their husbands, sons or brothers, or cause them
to be killed by others.
Note: The natural instincts of a woman or man are manifested only in the bodily
conception of life. We should see all women as spiritual units, whose only duty is to
satisfy Krsna. Then one is not influenced by the matériái modes. Everyone should he
interested in the activities of the spirit soul, not the body. The soul is sometimes
described as purusa due to the spirit of enjoying the matériái world whether in the body
of a man or a woman. Krsna consciousness, however, provides first-ciass training for a
man or a woman. A man should he trained as a first-ciass devotee of Krsna, and a
woman should he trained as a very chaste foiiower of her husband.
43. Solution: M y promise to give her a benediction cannot be violated, but also, Indra
should not be killed. I n this situation, I have a suitahle solution.
Note: Kasyapa thought of the following solution: "1 wiii teach Diti a Vaisnava
austerity, hy which her heart wiii he purified. Thus, she wiii not want to kiii Indra."
4 4 - 5 4 : KASYAPA INSTRUCTS DITI IN PUMSAVANA V O W
44. Kasyapa, thinking in this way and somewhat angry, condemning himself, spoke
to Diti as follows.
45. Kasyapa's equivocal statement, " I f you execute this vow properly for one year,
you will get a son who will kill Indra (indra-hd) and will be a friend of the demons
{adeva-bdndhavah)."
Note: The ahove was the meaning desired hy Diti. Kasyapa's meaning however was
that she would get a son who would foiiow Indra {indra-hd) and he a friend of the
demigods {deva-bándhavah).
6.18 Diti Vows to Kill Indra
46. D i t i : " 1 w i l l accept and follow the v o w . Please teli me what I have to do, what is
forbidden and what will not break the v o w . "
4 7 - 5 3 : Kasyapa Muni instructs the rules of the pumsavana vow
Do n o t . . .
Harm anyone; curse anyone; speak lies; cut your nails and hair; touch impure
things like skulls and bones.
Enter the water while bathing; become angry; speak or associate with sinful
people; wear unwashed clothes; put on a garland that has already been worn.
Eat food - which is leftover, offered to the goddess Kali, contaminated by flesh
49    or fish, brought by a südra, or seen by a woman in her menstrual period.
Drink water - by joining your palms.
Go out on the Street - without having washed your mouth, hands and feet; in
50    the evening; with your hair loose; without being properly decorated; unless you
are very grave and are sufficiently covered.
Lie down - without having washed both of your feet or without being purified;
51    with wet feet or with your head pointed west or north; naked, or with other
women, or during the sunrise or sunset.
Do's: The woman should...
Worship cows, brahmanas, Laksmí and the Lord (go-viprán sriyam acyutam) -
52   before breakfast, while wearing washed clothes, and being always pure and being
adorned with turmeric, sandalwood pulp and other auspicious items.
Worship women who have sons and whose husbands are living [vira-vatih) with
flower garlands, sandalwood pulp, ornaments and other paraphernalia.
Worship her husband and offer him prayers and meditate upon him, thinking
that he is situated in her womb (kostha-gatam).
54. Result: If you perform this ceremony called pumsavana without violations for at
least one year, you will give birth to a son who will kill Indra {sakra-hd - also mean a
foiiower of Indra).
5 5 - 6 4 : BIRTH OF THE MARUTS
55. D i t i agreed to undergo this vow with great jubilation. She became pregnant, having
taken semen from Kasyapa, and faithfully began discharging the vow.
56. Indra started serving her: Understanding Diti's purpose, Indra, who sees his self-
interest, engaged himself in the service of Diti, who was residing in an dér ama.
57. Indra daily brought flowers, fruits, roots, wood, kusa grass, leaves, sprouts, earth
and water at the proper time.
58. Deceitful service: Just as a hunter becomes like a deer to kill the deer (by covering
himself with a deerskin), so Indra served Diti with deceit, in order to find a fault in her
vow.
Note: Indra desired to find somé fault in Diti's vow was his misconception. Since
whatever faults exist are nullified by remembering and glorifying Visnu, her vow
should also become faultless, even if there was a fault.
59. Indra's worry: When Indra could find no faults, he became worried and thought,
" H o w will there be good fortune for me?"
60. Diti's mistake: Having grown weak because of the vow, Diti once unfortunately
neglected to wash her mouth, hands and feet after eating and went to sleep during the
evening twilight.
61. Indra entered Diti's womb: Finding this fault, Indra, who has all mystic powers
(yogesah), entered Diti's womb while she was unconscious and fast asleep.
62. Indra cuts the embryo: Indra, with his thunderbolt, cut her embryo into seven
pieces, which began to cry. Indra told them, " D o not cry, (md rodih)" and then he cut
each of them into seven pieces again.
Note: Three possible explanations are given for how the 49 jivas came into place:
i.    A single jiva can expand his body into 49 forms for a short time like a yogi or
like someone else enjoying a lot of both piety and sin.
ii.   Knowing the future event, 49 jivas entered into one body, and then entered
each of the 49 parts when cut by Indra.
iii. 48 more jivas entered each of the other parts the moment Indra divided the
one fetus, just as separate jivas enter each branch when a parson cuts branches
off a creeper or bush and plants them in the ground.
63. The Maruts pleaded: Being aggrieved, they pleaded to Indra with földed hands,
"Dear Indra, we are your brothers, the Maruts. Why are you trying to kill us?"
64. Indra comforted them: When Indra saw that they were his devoted followers, he
said, " I f you are all my brothers, you have nothing more to fear."
65. Just as you, Pariksit, were saved by the Lord in the womb, the 49 pieces of Diti's
embryo were all saved by tbe Lord's mercy {srinivdsa anukampayd).
66-67. The power of worshipping the Lord: If one worships the Lord even once, he
attains the same bodily features as Visnu (sdmyatám). Because Diti worshipped Lord
Visnu for almost one year, the 49 Maruts became equal to the demigods. Visnu
removed the fault of their mother, and made them drinkers of soma.
^^^B          6 8 - 7 8 : HONEST DISCUSSION BETWEEN DITI AND INDRA            W I I ^ ^ H
68. Diti, being purified, was pleased when she got up from bed and saw her forty-
nine sons, brilliant as fire, and in friendship with Indra.
69-70. Diti asked Indra to teli the truth: Dear son, I adhered to this vow just to get a
son to kill you twelve Ádityas. I prayed for only one son, but now there are 49. H o w
has this happened? If you know, please teli me the truth. Don't speak lies.
71-72. Indra spoke the truth: " M y dear mother! Blinded by selfish interests (artha-
buddhih), I lost sight of religion [na dharma-drk). Understanding your vow, I came to
find fault in you and cut the embryo. First I cut the child into 7 pieces, and then each
of the children into 7 pieces again. However, none of them died.
73. Secondary result: Seeing that great wonder, I decided that this was a secondary
result of your worship of Lord Visnu. Even liberation is a mere secondary result
(siddhih kdpy dnusanginí) of worshipping Visnu    (mahdpurusa-püjdydh).
74. Expert devotees: Those who, desiring only worship of the Lord without any
matériái desires including liberation, are considered expert in understanding their
benefit and the Lord fulfils all their desires.
drddhanarh bhagavata ihamdnd nirdsisah
ye tu necchanty api páram te svdrtha-kusaldh smrtdh
75. What intelligent man, who serves the most dear Lord, who gives Himself to His
devotees, would accept matériái happiness, which is available even in hell?
drddhydtma-pradam devath svdtmdnarh      jagad-ísvaram
ko vrnita guna-sparsarh budhah sydn narake 'pi yat
76. Forgive me: O best of all women (mahiyasi), I am a fool. Kindly excuse me for my
sinful acts. The dead embryo has become alive by good fortune of your devotion."
77. Diti was satisfied by Indra's good behaviour. Then, Indra offered respects to her,
and with her permission went to heaven with his brothers, the Maruts.
78. Sukadeva Gosvámi concludes: I have replied to the questions in regard to the
auspicious birth of the Maruts. What else should I explain?

his relatives, and then give his wife the remnants of the cooked rice (caroh) which will
ensure good progeny (suprajdstvam) and very good fortune (susaubhagam).

2 5 - 2 8 : PHALA-SRUTI
25-28. Phala-sruti: If this vow is observed according to the description of sástra:
•   A man, even in this life, will achieve all the benedictions he desires.
•   A wife will surely receive good fortune, opulence, sons, a long-living husband,
a good reputation and a good home.
•   A n unmarried girl will be able to get a very good husband.
•   A n avírá woman (who has no husband or son) can be promoted to the spiritual
world.
•   A woman whose children have died after birth can get a child with a long
duration of life and also become very fortunate in possessing wealth.
•   A n unfortunate woman will become fortunate.
•   A n ugly woman will become heautiful.
•   A diseased man can gain relief from his disease and have an able body.
•   If one recites this narration during the sraddha ceremony, the demigods and
Pitrs will be extremely pleased with him and fulfil all of his desires.
•   Lord Visnu and mother Laksmí will be very pleased with him.
O King Pariksit, now I have completely described how Diti performed this ceremony
and had good children (the Maruts) and a happy life.

'^^tV^^ií^'^^íV^&'éV'&W^^^m^^^                            The Power of the Holy Name
naivam-vidhah purusa-kára urukramasya
pumsám tad-anghri-rajasá jita-sad-gunánám
citram vidüra-vigatah sakrd ádadita
yan-námadheyam adhuná sa jaháti bandham
"My dear King, a devotee {pumsdm) who has taken shelter of the dust from the lotus feet
(tad-anghri-rajasá) of the Lord (urukramasya) can transcend the influence of the six
matériái waves - namely hunger, thirst, lamentation, illusion, old age and death - and he
can conquer the mind and five senses (jita-sad-gunánám). However, this is not very
wonderful (naivam citrarh) for a pure devotee of the Lord because even a person beyond
the jurisdiction of the four castes - in other words, an untouchahle (vidüra-vigatah) - is
immediately relieved of bondage to matériái existence (jaháti bandham) if he utters the
holy name of the Lord (yan-námadheyam adhuná) even once (sakrd)." [5.1.35]
Context; After hearing the powerful activities performed hy the Mahárája Priyavrata, like
following the orhit of the sun and creating the seven oceans and islands of Bhü-mandala,
Pariksit Mahárája might have had douhts ahout these wonderful, uncommon activities.
Therefore, Sukadeva Gosvámí reassured him that such powerful activities are not at all
astonishing for a devotee.
i. Urukrama: For a devotee of the Lord everything is possible, because he takes shelter of the
all-powerful Lord, who is known as Urukrama for His wonderful act of covering the three
worlds with His three footsteps. (5.1.35 P)
ii. Tad-anghri-rajasá: A devotee who has taken shelter at the Lord's lotus feet can do
wonderful things unimaginahle to a common man, hy the grace of their dust. (5.1.35 P)
iii. Vidüra-vigatah: What to speak of a sanctified devotee, even a candála or an untouchahle
outcaste, is immediately freed from matériái bondage if he utters the holy name of the Lord
even once. (5.1.35 P)
iv. Sa jaháti bandham: "He gives up his matériái bondage." The body is a symholic
representation of bondage according to one's karma. (5.1.35 P)
V. Transforms the subtle body: Chanting the holy name immediately transforms the suhtle
hody. Because of this, one is immediately freed from matériái bondage. (5.1.35 P)
vi. Alternate versions for bandham are tanvam and tattvam. Tanvam means the hody made
of three modes and tattvam means the matériái elements such as mahat-tattva (5.1.35 V).
Bh.igcívdt.i Subotlliini
. KNOWING G O D T H R O U G H SERVICE
rahüganaitat tapasá na yáti        na cejyayá nirvapanad grhad va
na cchandasá naiva jalágni-süryair         viná mahat-páda-rajo-'bhisekam
"My dear King Rahügana (rahügana), unless (viná) one has the opportunity to smear
his entire body with the dust of the lotus feet (rajo-'bhisekam) of great devotees (mahat-
páda), one cannot realize the Absolute Truth. One cannot realize the Absolute Truth
simply hy ohserving celihacy (cchandasá), strictly following the rules and regulations of
householder life (grhád vá), leaving home as a vánaprastha, accepting sannyása
(nirvapanád), or undergoing severe penances (tapasá) in winter hy keeping oneself
suhmerged in water or surrounding oneself in summer hy fire and the scorching beat of
the sun (naiva jalágni-süryair). There are many other processes to understand the
Absolute Truth, hut the Absolute Truth is only revealed to one who has attained the
mercy of a great devotee." [5.12.12]
Context: While answering the several questions posed hy Mahárája Rahügana, Jada Bharata
gradually hegan to teach him ahout the Absolute truth. The Absolute Truth is manifested in
three features as Brahman, Paramátmá and Bhagaván. Jada Bharata spoke this verse to explain
the qualification required to understand the Bhagaván aspect of the Absolute Truth.
1.    Only method to understand the Absolute Truth: One cannot attain the perfection of
spiritual life simply hy following the directions of the Vedas. One only has to approach a
pure devotee and sérve him to understand the Absolute Truth without fail. (5.12.12 P)
2.    Only a pure devotee can give Krsna to others: Lord Krsna is the property of His pure
unconditional devotees, and as such only they can deliver Krsna to another devotee. Krsna
is never ohtainable directly. 5.12.12 is quoted to support this statement. (2.3.23 P)
3.    Ultimate word in all education: There is no alternative for a living entity to become
liberated from the conditioned state other than exclusively following and serving the
devotees. This is the ultimate word in all education. 5.12.12 is quoted hy Srila
Bhaktisiddhánta Sarasvati Thákura in his commentary to CB Madhya 23.516.
4. Repeated verse: Lord Caitanya quotes this verse to Sanátana Cosvámi while explaining
how devotional service is ohtained. (CC Madhya 22.51).
5.    Similar verse: This instruction expresses the same message as the instructions of Prahláda
Mahárája to Hiranyakasipu in 7.5.32 (Srila Bhaktisiddhánta Sarasvati Thákura).
VW\c3*L»'#Vt5'fi«^rt^e5^**Vc?^»^rtXt53j^
3 . EVEN THE LEARNED BECOME BEWILDERED
vadanti visvaih kavayah sma nasvaram
pasyanti cádhyátmavido vipascitah
tathápi muhyanti tavája máyayá
suvismitam krtyam ajaih nato 'smi tam
" O unbotn one [ajarh), learned Vedic scholars who are advanced in spiritual
knowledge [kavayah] cettainly [sma] know that this matériái world [visvarh]
is perishable (nasvaram), as do other logicians and philosophers
(cádhyátmavido vipascitah). In trance they realize the factual position of this
world (pasyanti), and they preach the truth as well (vadanti). Yet even
(tathápi) they are sometimes bewildered (muhyanti) by Your illusory energy
(tavája máyayá). This is your own wonderful pastime (suvismitam krtyam).
Therefore, I can understand that your illusory energy is very wonderful, and
I offer my respectful obeisances (natah asmi) unto you (tam)." [5.18.4]
Context: This verse is spoken by Bhadrasravá, the son of Dharmarája and ruler of
Bhadrásva-varsa, in his prayers to Hayasírsa.
SrTla Prabhupáda's comments
•   Power of the illusory energy: The Lord's mdyd sometimes also acts on the most
advanced learned scholars, who factually know the constitutional position of this
matériái world through realization (liberated souls).
•   Danger of inattentiveness:
a. Captivates: Because of association with the modes of matériái nature for a very
long time, even liberated souls sometimes become captivated by the illusory energy
due to inattentiveness in the transcendentai position.
b. Creates havoc: Therefore no one should think himself a liberated person,
immuné to the influence of mdyd. Everyone should very cautiously execute
devotional service by rigidly following regulative principles. Otherwise, a little
inattention will create havoc.
Bhagavata Suhodhini
•   T h e L o r d is patita-pávana not kapata-pávana: The Lord is always prepared to
excuse His devotee, but if a devotee takes advantage of the Lord's leniency and
purposefully commits mistakes again and again, the Lord will certainly punish him
by letting him fali down into the clutches of the illusory energy.
•    Insufficiency of theoretical knowledge: Theoretical knowledge acquired by
studying the Vedas is insufficient to protect one from the clutches of mdyd. One
must strongly adhere to the lotus feet of the Lord in devotional service. Then one's
position is secure.
Virarághava Ácárya's comments
•    Even vedántists may fali: Even those who have studied Vedanta and have
performed a certain amount of worship may fali into illusion and pine at the loss
of their children and so on. So to overcome mdyd one has to take refuge in the
Lord.
Vijayadhvaja T i r t h a ' s comments
•    Grace is needed: Even for the learned, the greatness of Hari will be confusing
without His grace.
K J ^ C 5 3 J K J ^ ^
kecit kevalayá bhaktya
vásudeva-paráyanáh
aghath dhunvanti kártsnyena
niháram iva bháskarah
"Only a rare person (kecit) who has adopted complete, unalloyed (kevalayá)
devotional service (bhaktya) to Krsna (vásudeva-paráyanáh)        can uproot
(dhunvanti) the wccds of sinful actions (agharh) with no possibility that they
will rcvivc (kártsnyena). He can do this simply by discharging devotional
servicc, just as (iva) the sun (bháskarah) can immediately dissipate fog
(niháram) by its rays." [6.1.15]
Context: Since Mahárája Pariksit was a great devotee, the answers of Sukadeva
Gosvámí, concerning karma-kánda and jhána-kánda, could not satisfy him. Therefore
Sukadeva Gosvámí, knowing very well the heart of his disciple, explained the
transcendentai bliss of devotional service.
1.     Jnána cannot uproot sinful desires: Previously Sukadeva Gosvámí gave the
example that the dried leaves beneath a bamboo tree may be completely burnt to
ashes by a fire, but may sprout again because the root is still in the ground.
Similarly, because the root of sinful desire is not destroyed in the heart of a person
who is cultivating knowledge without a taste for devotional service, there is a
possibility that his sinful desires will reappear. (6.1.15 P)
2. The test of K r s n a consciousness: If one is advanced in Krsna consciousness,
matériái activities, both sinful and pious, automatically become distasteful.
(6.1.15 P)
3.   Slight bhakti is enough to remove sins: Merely by the manifestation of the first
rays of the sun, fog immediately disappears. Similarly, if one has even a slight
relationship with devotional service, all the fog of his sinful life is immediately
vanquished. (6.1.15 P)
4.   Bhakti provides the results of other processes automatically: Those who are
vdsudeva-pardyana,    automatically achieve the results of mauna (silence), vrata
(vows), and other such methods mentioned in 6.1.13. In other words, these methods
are not so powerful. If one takes to devotional service, all of them are very easily
performed. (7.9.46 P)
5.   Destruction of nescience: Srila Bhaktisiddhánta Sarasvati Thákura states in his
commentary on C B Madhya 11.48-49 that for the destruction of nescience by the
Lord's holy name one should discuss Srimad-Bhagavatam             (3.9.15, 6.2.7, 11-12,
6.1.15, 6.3.24, and 6.16.44), the first verse of Siksdstaka, and       Bhakti-rasdmrta-
sindhu (Daksina-vibhága    1.51).
6.   R a r i t y of bhakti: The word kecit (somé) indicate that a person performing bhakti
as a main item is rare. Those who surrender to Vásudeva do not depend on austerity
or other practices (kevalayá).   They do not need special qualification. But because
others do not have faith they do not surrender. Because the devotees have faith they
are called vásudeva-paráyanáh       (Bhakti-sandarbha,   Anuccheda 126 and 6.1.15 V )
7.   B h a k t i destroys sin without effort: The sun by its own rays destroys unlimited
fog by its nature, without effort. Similarly, devotees by bhakti destroy unlimited
sins without effort. (Bhakti-sandarbha Anuccheda         126)
8.   Bhakti removes sins without the support of other processes: If a jnáni commits
sin, he destroys the sin by practice of jhána (yogena) alone. Similarly, if a bhakta
commits sin, he destroys the sin by practice of bhakti (yogena) alone, say by
chanting the name of the Lord. Verse 6.1.15 is quoted to verify this statement.
(11.20.25 V )

5 . T H E POWER O F SURRENDER
sakrn manah krsna-padáravindayor
nivesitam tad-guna-ragi yair iha
na te yamam pása-bhrtas ca tad-bhatán
svapne 'pi pasyanti hi círna-niskrtáh
"Although not having fully realized Krsria, persons who have even once
(sakrn) surrcndcrcd (nivesitam) completely unto His lotus feet (krsna-
padáravindayor)    and who have become attracted to His name, form,
qualities and pastimes (tad-guna-rági) arc completely freed of all sinful
reactions, for they have thus accepted the true method of atonement (cirna-
niskrtáh). Even in dreams (svapne api), such surrcndcrcd souls do not see
Yamarája (yamarh) or his order carriers (tad-bhatán), who are equipped with
ropes (pása-bhrtas) to bind the sinful." [6.1.19]
Context: Sukadeva Gosvámí explains bhakti yoga as the best process for protecting
one from all the sinful activities. The Ajámila episode is spoken immediately after this
verse to substantiate its import.
1.   Surrender: Bhagavad-gita        (18.66) describes the same principle as stated here.
(6.1.19 P)
2. Krsnas tu bhagaván svayam: It is significant that Sukadeva Gosvámí, having
several times repeated the words vásudeva-paráyana                (6.1.15) and    náráyana-
paráyana     (6.1.17), finally says krsna-padáravindayoh     in 6.1.19. Thus he indicates
that Krsna is the origin of both Náráyana and Vásudeva. (6.1.19 P)
3.   Even once: The word sakrt is significant because it indicates that if one sincerely
surrenders to Krsna once, he is saved even if by chance he falls down by committing
sinful activities. (6.1.19 P)
4.   Surrender is auspicious: The highest kalyana (auspicious) activity ( B G 6.40) is to
surrender to Krsna. (6.1.19 P)
5.   Surrender gives fearlessness: One should adhere to the lotus feet of Krsna very
tightly and sérve H i m under the direction of the spiritual master. Thus in all
conditions one will be akuto-bhaya, free from fear. (6.1.19 P)
6.   Escapes Yamarája: "Anyone who offers a little water or durva grass at the lotus
feet of the Lord is never subjected to the punishment of Yamarája." 6.1.19 is quoted
by Srila Bhaktisiddhánta Sarasvati Thákura in his commentary to C B Madhya
1.337.
7.   Brings detachment: The power of remembering the Lord is such that it destroys
all attraction to objects that may appear to the person (tad-guna-rági).       (Bhakti
Sandarbha Anuccheda 148)
8.   Yamadütas cannot capture: Just by náma-ábhása,       all anarthas can be removed -
but the holy name, being unhappy with offences committed against it, does not
manifest its complete power in the offender. Still, the servants of death have no
power to attack such a person. (Mádhurya    Kadambini, Chapter 3)
9.   What to speak of:
•    Even once remembering the Lord is sufficient, what to speak of many times.
•    Even thinking with the mind is sufficient, what to speak of hearing or uttering
the name of the Lord.
•    The mind which is materially attracted (tad-guna-rági)      is sufficient, what to
speak of a mind without matériái attractions.
•    Thinking even in dreams is sufficient what to speak of actual meditation.
(6.1.19 V )

1 . GLORIES O F BHAKTI-YOGA
.^^^^^^^^                                       ^
Can an insignificant jiva satisfy the Supreme Lord who has six opulences in full? The
pages of Srimad-Bhagavatam emphatically declare that it is absolutely possible. I n fact,
the Bhágavatam clarifies that satisfying the Supreme Lord in all one's endeavours is the
eternal function of the jiva. But how can a jiva accomplish that? That's where the king
of all spiritual pursuits, the path of bhakti, excels all other processes, since it can enable
everyone, regardless of matériái limitations, to satisfy the Supreme Lord through
offerings of love. As Sri Prahláda Mahárája states, even Gajendra, an elephant, satisfied
the Supreme Lord by his devotion. The following is a compilation of the glories of
devotional service collected from Srimad-Bhagavatam, Cantos 5 and 6.
BHAKTI A W A R D S LIBERATION
1. One can attain liberation from matériái bondage only by rendering service to highly
advanced spiritual personalities. [5.5.2 mahat-sevdm dváram áhur vimuktes...]
2. Bhakti awakens pure love for the Lord, which continues under all circumstances.
[5.24.19 no evaitat...]
3. Because Ajámila chanted the Lord's holy name in a helpless condition without offence,
he was freed from all sinful reactions and became eligible for liberation.
[6.2.7 ayam hi krta-nirveso..., 6.2.13 athainam mdpanayata...]
4. If one understands bhdgavata-dharma, or surrender unto the Supreme Lord, he is
immediately liberated and returns back to Godhead.
[6.3.20-21 svayambhür náradah sambhuh...]

life. [6.4.27-28 manisino 'ntar-hrdi sannivesitarh...]
6. If one faithfully and inoffensively chants the Lord's holy name, one surely returns to
the Lord's abode. [6.2.49 mriyamáno harer ndma...]

2 . GLORIES O F KRSNA-KATHÁ
For pure devotees it acts like nectar; for those who are aspiring to achieve perfection it
acts as medicine. Whether one is a siddha or sddhaka, hearing about Sri Krsna helps
everyone remember H i m . Krsna-kathd is that transcendentai food that simultaneously
acts like medicine as well as food for the soul by bringing one close to Krsna. As
Srimad-Bhdgavatam says, no one can achieve satisfaction without hearing krsna-kathd
sufficiently like one cannot feel satisfied without eating sufficiently. Here is a limited
collection about the unlimited glories of krsna-kathd described in Cantos 5 and 6 of
Srimad-Bhdgavatam.
NouRiSHES D E V O T I O N
1.   Glories of the Lord are very pleasing to great devotees and liberated persons.
[5.1.5 bddham uktarh bhagavata uttamaslokasya...]
2.   Anyone who attentively hears or speaks about the Lord's pastimes following in the
footsteps of the dcdryas certainly attains unalloyed devotional service.
[5.6.16 iti ha sma     sakala-veda-loka-deva-brdhmana-gavdm...]
3.   By constantly hearing the Lord's topics in the association of devotees, one gives up
desire of liberation and becomes attached to devotional service.
[5.12.13 yatrottamasloka-gundnuvddah...]
4.   Hearing and chanting the Lord's glories revives one's dormant Krsna consciousness.
[5.12.16 tasmdn naro 'sanga-susanga-jdta...]
5.   By hearing about the Lord, one may become a perfectly pure devotee.
[6.2.24-25 ajdmilo 'py athdkarnya...]
VANQUISHES UNWANTED THINGS,
6.   Constant hearing about the Lord's activities with eagerness cleanses one's heart of all
contamination. [5.18.11 yat-sanga-labdharh nija-virya-vaibhavam...,       6.3.32 srnvntdrh
grnatdm virydny...]
mímmímmmmímm-^mmíimm

eradicates the dirt from one's heart completely unlike Vedic ritualistic ceremonies.
[6.2.12 naikdntikam tad dhi krte 'pi niskrte...]
8.   Krsna-katha dispels all one's sinful reactions.
[5.3.4-5,12 and 6.3.24 etdvatdlam agha-nirharandya purhsdm...]
9.   Reading or hearing of the pastimes of the Lord or His devotees removes all sinful
reactions. [6.13.22-23 idarh mahdkhydnam asesa-pdpmandrh...]
EVEN G R E A T PERSONALITIES DESIRE T O HEAR

SUPREMACY OF HEARING KRSNA-KATHÁ

3 . ASSOCIATION O F DEVOTEES
In the modern world everything is sold on the hasis of how a product is advertised and
packaged attractively. In the mission of spreading devotional service, Krsna achieves both
advertising and packaging of bhakti attractively through His devotees. Devotees are the
animated advertising agencies for bhakti through their conduct and compassion. By their
unflinching devotion to the Lord and compassion to the living entities, devotees endeavour
to awaken devotional service in others who have forgottén it. Srtmad-Bhágavatam is itself
a mercy manifestation of the Supreme Lord through the agency of Vaisnavas like
Vyásadeva and Sukadeva Gosvami. Here is a collection from the pages of this Amala
Purdna about the glories of the association such Vaisnavas.
Glories of the association of devotees
1.   Opens the door to liberation: Rendering service to highly advanced spiritual
personalities is the door to liberation. [5.5.2 mahat-sevdm dvdram dhur vimuktes...]
1.   Gives protection: Priyavrata while enjoying once began to speak in a spirit of
renunciation remembering his past surrender to Narada. [5.1.36 sa evam aparimita...]
3.   Gives knowledge: By taking shelter of Narada, Priyavrata achieved the highest
perfection in transcendentai knowledge, then he engaged in spiritual activities with full
attention. [5.1.6 yarhi vdva ha rdjan sa rdja-putrah...]
4.   Destroys illusion: Attachment to devotees destroys illusion through knowledge and
allows one to attain remembrance of the Lord by speaking and hearing about him.
[5.12.16 tasmdn naro 'sanga-susanga-jdta-...]
5.   Gives krsna-katha: Talks concerning the qualities of the Lord are constantly sung in
the association of devotees. [5.12.13 yatrottamasloka-gundnuvddah...]
6.   Makes one fortunate: Humán birth is better than a demigod's birth as there is the
association of devotees available. [5.13.21 aho   nr-janmdkhila-janma...]
7.   Purifies completely: By serving hona fide devotees a sinner becomes completely puri-
fied, more than by any other process of purification. [6.1.16 na tathd hy aghavdn...]
8.   Conquers the illusory energy: Unless one worships the lotus feet of great devotees,
one will be conquered by the illusory energy, and his intelligence will be bewildered.
[5.3.14 ko vd iha te 'pardjito 'pardjitayd...]
9.   Purifies faster than holy place: Hearing about the Lord in the association of devotees
cleanses the heart morc quickly than visiting holy placcs or bathing in the Gangcs.
[5.18.11 yat-sanga-labdharh     nija-virya-vaibhavam...]

Even a large number multiplied by zero becomes a "zero" and renders the large number
valueless. Similarly, our devotional practices may be numerous, deep, long standing
and inspiring many others, however if we commit vaisnava-aparádha         then all that
becomes valueless, at least for the time being, until we are freed from that vaisnava-
aparádha by begging forgiveness from that Vaisnava and transforming our heart by
genuine repentance. The following are somé gems collected from the Fifth and Sixth
Cantos of the Bhágavatam, to enlighten us further on this topic.
_              DANGERS OF OFFENCE
• Surety of punishment: A n envious person who commits an offence before a great
personality is always punished (like Káli punished the dacoits who tried to kiii Jada
Bharata). (5.9.19)
• Spoils all one's spiritual assets: Vaisnava-aparádha     is háti mátá, a mad elephant.
When a mad elephant enters a garden, it spoils aii the fruits and flowers. Similarly,
if one offends a Vaisnava, he spoils aii his spiritual assets. (5.10.17 P)
• No protection from anyone: Fven if a person is as strong as Lord Siva, if he
biasphemes a Vaisnava he is sure to be destroyed. This is the verdict of aii sastras.
If one does not care for this and dares biaspheme a Vaisnava, he suffers life after
life. [CB Madhya-khanda 22.129, 22.55-56 quoted in 5.10.25 P)
• T h e Lord w i l l not excuse, although devotee might not take offence: Mahárája
Ambarisa was offended by Durvásá and Lord Visnu would not grant him pardon.
Finally he had to fali down at the lotus feet of Mahárája Ambarisa. One should be
very carefui not to offend the lotus feet of Vaisnavas and brdhmanas. (5.13.23 P)
• Chastisement from seniors: Lord Brahmá chastised the demigods for not properly
respecting the Brhaspati, who was their guru. (6.7.21P)
• Failure at every step: The demigods were defeated by their weak opponents. Thus,
by disrespecting a respectabie superior, one ioses iongevity and the results of pious
activities, and in this way one is degraded. (6.7.22P)
4. Vaisnava-Aparadha              299
• Even good qualities become useless: Indra's good qualities such as tolerance and
opulence, could not help him in his grief.
• M a y attract curses: He (Citraketu) is impudent, püffed of his achievements, and
thinking himself greatly important. He does not deserve to approach the shelter of
Lord Visnu's lotus feet. (6.17.14)
• Death punishment: Lven demigods cannot tolerate offences against devotees.
Being intolerant of the offences committed by the dacoits against Jada Bharata, the
infuriated goddess Káli displayed her fearsome features and immediately
decapitated them. (5.9.17-18)

• Attachment for position: Formeriy Indra had killed Visvarüpa (a brdhmana) out

5 . EAGERNESS FOR RENUNCIATION
Focus implies one pointedness. T o have focus on the pleasure of the Supreme Lord is
real renunciation. Lor that one has to remove his focus from selfish and extended
enjoyments. Srimad-Bhdgavatam guides its readers about the proper mood and
principles of renunciation through various personalities and their behaviour. I n Cantos
5 and 6 we find many kings who exhibited eagerness for renunciation. There is even
competition for renunciation between father and son. There are also instructions on
how householders can live renounced lives by surrendering to the Lord. After all one
can be entangled even in the forest if the six enemies of the senses are not controlled.
The following is a one such collection for our enlightenment.
PRIYAVRATA MAHÁRÁJA
Situation: Priyavrata renounced the world at an early age and was living as a
renunciant under the order of his guru, Narada. Then, Svayambhuva Manu, wanting
himself to rétire, asked Priyavrata to take to the responsibility of ruling the kingdom.
Competition for renunciation: Both father Svayambhuva Manu and son Priyavrata
wanted to renounce the world although they had such great sovereignty over the entire
world. I n contrast, today's rulers hardly like to give up their posts and positions even
advanced ages.
T h e secret of renunciation in household life: Although household life is entangling
one can still remain in the renounced spirit by being sense controlled and self-satisfied.
This was told by Brahmá. The way to do it is to seek shelter of the Lord's lotus feet. A
household devotee who is attached to the Lord's lotus feet, is as good as one who has
renounced everything.
Reinstated in renunciation: Priyavrata ruled for eleven arbuda years. Although he
performed great activities which are difficult to even imagine, Priyavrata felt he was
entangled in household life. He once contemplated in a spirit of renunciation that he
, V » « ! , V . J I ? . V ,
">. r.iíírnicsK I oi Kcniiiii i.ilioii
were entangled like a dancing monkey in a woman's hand. Thus he reawakened his
senses, regained discrimination, and gave his kingdom to his sons and resumed his
spiritual life.
NÁBHI
Situation: Nábhi, seeing the proper successor in the form of Rsabbadeva, who was
popular among citizens, renounced the kingdom. T o protect people in terms of the
Vedic religious system, Nábhi entrusted Rsabbadeva in the hands of brdhmanas,
renounced his kingdom, and went to Badarikásrama along with wife. Thus he achieved
the success of going back to Godhead.
LORD RSABHADEVA
Renunciation: Rsabhadeva enthroned His eldest son on the royal seat and left
Brahmá vatta to tour the whole world like a naked madman with dishevelled hair.
Indifferent to all insults: Accepting the dress of an avadhüta. He teamed like a blind,
deaf, dumb, or mad man. While wandering alone. He was always threatened, beaten,
urinated upon and spat upon. Sometimes people threw stones, stool and dust at H i m ,
and passed foul air before H i m . Being situated in His spiritual glory. He did not care
for these matériái insults.
Ájagara-vrtti: H e accepted the behaviour of a python. He remained in one place;
sleeping, eating, drinking, urinating, and passing stool. He rolled in His own stool and
urine so that opposing elements would not disturb H i m . His stool and urine filled
eighty miles of the countryside with a pleasant fragrance. Though the asta-siddhis
approached H i m , He did not exercise these powers.
Disappearance: He concluded His pastimes on the earth. He wandered like a madman
in the places of south India and finally His body was burnt in a forest fire.
KING BHARATA
Renunciation: H e gave up his heautiful wife, nice children, great friends and an
enormous empire, for he lenged to sérve the Lord. Although these things were very
difficult to give up, Mahárája Bharata was so exalted that he gave them up just as one
gives up stool after evacuating. (5.14.43)
After giving these up, he went to Pulaha-ásrama and practised devotional service by
giving up all matériái desires.
I     Bhagavata S u b o c ^
^               jÉMMHBI^rtB    rt2*^_^;;;rtY--"Y.fi^
Interrupted renunciation: Because of misplaced compassion totvard a deer, he
neglected his practices and remembered the deer at the time of his death. Thus he
attained the body of a deer in his next birth.
Renunciation continued: I n the body of the deer, he remembered his previous life. By
constant repentance he became completely detached from all matériái things. He left
his mother deer and again went to Pulaha-ásrama, and gave up his body there.
Perfected renunciation: Then he was born in a brdhmana family. Remembering his
past two lives, he behaved like a madman. He did not take any instruction from his
father and feared again falling into bad association. He neglected his body and
wandered like a madman, not caring for the insults by others. Although dacoits try to
kill him, he did not say anything. When the opportunity came, he gave instructions to
King Rahügana.
THE HARYASVAS
Situation: The Haryasvas, 10,000 sons of Daksa, went to Náráyana-saras to perform
austerities to fulfil their father's desire of increasing the population. Seeing them,
Nárada instructed them an allegory which teaches renunciation. Being intelligent, they
understood its meaning and decided to renounce the path of fruitive activities.
T H E SAVALÁSVAS
4»Situation: The Savalásvas, another 1000 sons of Daksa, also went to Náráyana-saras
to perform austerities. Again Nárada approached them and told them to follow the
path of their elder brothers. Thus they also took to the path of renunciation.
6. Leadershi|)
As Lord Rsabhadeva says, " A real leader is one who can bring his followers close to
the Supreme L o r d . " A leader is identified by his exemplary personal character,
sacrificing spirit, obedience to his autborities, caring for the pbysical and spiritual
necessities of his dependants, his spirit of renunciation and various other similar
qualities. Such a leader helps in the advancement of his dependants' spiritual
consciousness. There are many leaders whose stories appear in Cantos 5 and 6. While
it would not be practical to mention all their qualities here, somé principles and qual-
ities of leadership that we may learn from these personalities are presented below.
MAHÁRÁJA PRIYAVRATA
Sacrificed his personal ambition: Priyavrata Mahárája                accepted   leadership,
sacrificing his own ambition of remaining as a renunciant.
LoUowed autborities: Lor a greater cause he accepted the words of autborities.
Kept K r s n a i n the centre: While ruling, he always thought of the Lord's lotus feet.
Led i n a renounced spirit: Despite all his wonderful activities, in the end he renounced
all of them.
MAHÁRÁJA NÁBHI
Chose the right successor: Nabhi chose Rsabhadeva as his successor after seeing his
popularity among the citizens and ministers.
Ensuring that his legacy continue: Although Rsabhadeva was qualificd, Nábhi
entrusted him to brdhmanas for guidance to rule as per Vedic instructions.
LORD RSABHADEVA
Led by example: Rsabhadeva led an ideál brahmacari life by first going to gurukula
and giving guru-daksind. Then He led an ideál grhastha life and carried out the
ritualistic activities ordained by the sruti and smrti sastras. He regulated his citizens in
household life by enabling them to attain religion, wealth, fame, offspring, matériái
pleasure and finally eternal life.
Qualities H e exhibited as a great leader:
• Not materially attached
• Always equipoised
• Seeing everyone on the same levél
• Unhappy to see others unhappy
• Well-wisher of all living entities
• He satisfied everyone's needs
• He performed sacrifices to satisfy Lord Visnu
Lollowed the brahmanas: He ruled the people according to the system of varndsrama-
dharma, which enjoins that the brdhmanas instruct the ksatriyas and that the ksatriyas
administer the state through the vaisyas and südras.
Maintained the legacy: Rsabhadeva instructed His sons at Brahmávarta.
Responsible: He taught that, "One should not become a leader or an authority unless
one takes up the responsibility of delivering one's dependants."
Not carried away by H i s powers: Although H e developed mystic powers, Rsabhadeva
never used them.
MAHÁRÁJA BHARATA          J
Learned and experienced
Lngaged in respective duties himself
Affectionate to the citizens like a father
Lngaged his citizens in their occupational duties
Performed many sacrifices
MAHÁRÁJA G A Y A
Gaya's ideál rule consisted of the following principles:
• Giving full protection and security to the citizens (prajd-pdlana)
• Arranging sufficient food to feed all the citizens (posana)
• Distributing gifts to the citizens to satisfy them (prinana)
• Conducting meetings and satisfying the citizens with sweet words (upaldlana)
• Giving them good instructions to become first-class citizens (anusdsana)
• Performing sacrifices as recommended in the Vedas (ijya)
Gaya's unparalleled activities as a king:
• Used to perform all kinds of Vedic rituals
• Respected by all
• Learned in all scriptures
• Protector of dharma
• Possessor of all kinds of opulence
• Leader among gentlemen
• Servant of the devotees
Understood his subordinates: When the Yamadutas were agitated and challenged his
position, Yamarája called them "sons" (putrakd), understanding their emotional state.
Not claiming falsé superiority: H e explained to the Yamadütas that he is just the
servant of the Lord and that the Lord is the originál master. He did not take advantage
of their ignorance.
H u m i l i t y : While mentioning the names of the twelve mahdjanas, he mentions his name
last.

i
Gave corrections to the demigods: A leader should correct his subordinates for their
mistakes but also give solutions.
Led i n difficulty: When all the demons were fleeing from the battlefield, Vrtrasura
continued to fight with the demigods, encouraging the demons to fight.
KASYAPA
Channelling energy of subordinates: When Kasyapa understood that Diti wanted to
take revenge, he intelligently engaged her in devotional service and transformed her.

n
Srimad-Bhdgavatam is about the devotees of the Supreme Lord whose personal lives
show how to become a real devotee of the Supreme Lord. The very purpose of studying
Srimad-Bhdgavatam is to associate with these great devotees, by internalizing their
instructions taught through their personal lives. I n contrast, to teach one how not to
act, the Bhdgavatam describes the activities of personalities who commit mistakes or
act inimically towards the devotees and the Lord. Below are the brief descriptions of
various characters from Cantos 5 and 6 walking our way through the pages of Canto
5 and 6 of this paramahamsa-sarhhitd.
PRIYAVRATA MAHÁRÁJA              ^ H H ^ ^ ^ ^ ^ ^ ^ ^ ^
Life
• Brahmacarya: He became a great devotee having sought the lotus feet of Nárada, his guru
and thus achieved the highest perfection in transcendentai knowledge. (5.1.6)
• Marriage: Married Barhismati, the daughter of the Prajápati named Visvakarmá. In her he
begot ten sons equal to him and one daughter named Ürjasvati. (5.1.24)
• Lineage: Son of Sváyambhuva Manu. (4.31.26)
• Distinguished descendants: From his other wife he got 3 sons namely Uttama, Támasa and
Raivata. All of them later took charge of manvantara millenniums. (5.1.28)
Character
•       Obedient: Although Priyavrata was completely freed from all matériái contamination, he
ruled the matériái world just to honour the orders of his superiors. (5.1.23)
•      Devotee: Though engaged in worldly affairs, he always thought of the Lord. (5.1.23)
•       Detachment: Considered the opulences he had achieved hellish. (5.1.41)
•       Powerful King: No one but the Supreme Lord could do what he has done. He dissipated
the darkness of night, and with the rims of his great chariot, he excavated seven oceans
(5.1.39) dividing Bhü-mandala into seven islands. (5.1.31)
•       Introspective: Criticized himself for being fallen in matériái enjoyment. (5.1.37)
•       Renunciation: He gave up everything, including his wife and opuient kingdom. (5.1.38)

Íf
^ BHARATA MAHÁRÁJA
Character
•   Exalted devotee: Lord Rsabha asked his other sons to sérve Him. (5.5.20)
•   Influential: Bhárata-varsa is named after him. (5.7.3)
•    Perfect king: Learned, experienced and affectionate to the citizens. (5.7.4)
•    Sacrifices: He performed them as offerings to the different limbs of the Lord. (5.7.6)
•    Renunciation: Retired for Pulahásrama at the right time. (5.7.8 P)
•    Bháva stage: Hairs of his body stood on end; tears flowed from his eyes; constantly
meditated on the Lord. (5.7.12)
•    Compassionate: Took compassion upon the motherless calf. (5.8.7)
•    Misplaced compassion: Thought that for his welfare he should protect the deer. (5.8.9)
•    Fall-down: Always thinking of the deer, he neglected his duties. (5.8.15)
•    Missed opportunity: At the time of death, thought of deer and received a deer's body. (5.8.27)
•    Protection of bhakti: In the body of a deer, still remembered his past life. (5.8.28)
•    Introspective: In the body of a deer, he lamented about his past acts. (5.8.29)
•    Seeking devotee association: Leaving his mother, he again went to Pulahásrama. (5.8.30)
•    Cautious: As he was born again as a brdhmana, he acted like a madman to save himself
from bad association. (5.9.3)
•    Transcendentai: Tolerated all insults; never ate anything for sense gratification; unaffected
by dualities. (5.9.9-10)
•    Compassionate: While carrying the palanquin, he did not want to step on ants (5.10.2). He
instructed Rahügana out of great mercy. (5.13.24)
Early life and Qualities
• Well-learned: Ajámila was born in brdhmana family and studied all the Vedic literatures.
• Reservoir of good qualities: Good character and conduct; very mild, gentle, truthful,
chanted Vedic mantras, very pure, respectful, benevolent, well behaved, controlled in
speech and free from envy.
Victim of lust
• Affected: Became affected after seeing the scene of the drunken südra and the prostitute
shamelessly embracing and kissing each other. (6.1.60 P)
• Unsuccessful: With the help of knowledge, he tried to control his lust, but failed. (6.1.62)
• Contemplated: He always thought of her and finally brought her to his home. (6.1.63)
• Sinful life: Earned his livelihood by sinful means. Passed 88 years in maintaining his family
in sinful ways. Although 88 years old, he had ten sons. (6.1.22-24)
Posanam (Saved by the name of the Lord)
• Ajnáta-sukrti: By repeatedly calling his son, he unknowingly chanted the name of
Náráyana (6.1.26 P). Being a brdhmana, he worshiped Náráyana in his youth (6.1.27 P).
• Regret: He regretted all his wrong deeds such as giving up his wife and parents, and
procreating through a prostitute. (6.2.24-28)
• Aware: He was aware of his sins and the consequent results he was about to enter. (6.2.29)
• Gratitude: He was grateful to the Visnudütas for saving him. (6.2.30-32)
• Appreciation: Appreciated the holy names and his fortune to chant. (6.2.33-34)
• Determination: He was determined to control his senses, practice devotional service, not fali
down again, not identify with the body, firmly take shelter of Krsna's lotus feet. (6.2.35-38)
Success
• Detached himself from the matériái conception of life with determination. (6.2.39)
• Austerities: Performed austerities and executed bhakti-yoga. (6.2.40-41)
• Perfection: He again saw the Visnudütas, who took him back to Godhead. (6.2.42-44)
•   Position: Daksa's position is defined by Lord Rsabhadeva from an evolutionary point of
view, that he is superior to humán beings, ghosts, Gandharvas, Siddhas, Kinnaras, asuras
and demigods, but inferior to Siva, Brahmá, the Supreme Lord and brdhmanas (5.5.21)
Life
• Birth: First born during the reign of Sváyambhuva Manu, but due to his offence to Lord
Siva he was punished and received a goat-head. He gave up that body and in Cáksusa
manvantara, took birth again to Márisá and the Pracetás (6.4.17 P).
• Marriage: The Lord being satisfied by his austerities and prayers, awarded him the potency
to enjoy the illusory energy and the daughter of Pancajana named Asikní (6.4).
• Prajápati: He first created all kinds of demigods, demons, humán beings, birds, beasts,
aquatics and so on by his mind. Then after marrying Asikni he begot 10,000 sons known as
the Haryasvas and 1,000 sons called the Savalásvas. Later he begot 60 daughters.
Qualities
• Expert: Daksa received his name because he was very expert in sex life. (6.4)
• Austere: Daksa engaged in great austerities to please the Lord. (6.4.21)
• Merciful: Prajápati Daksa tried to benefit the conditioned souls by begetting them to give
them a life with a chance for liberation. (6.4.44 P)
• Dutiful: He did not hesitate to allow his sons to receive cultural instructions concerning
the perfection of life; he depended upon them to choose whether to return home, back to
Godhead, or to rot in this matériái world in various species of life. (6.5.25 P)
Character
• Anger: When Daksa heard that the Savalásvas had also left this world to engage in
devotional service, Daksa was angry at Nárada. When Daksa met Nárada, Daksa's lips
began trembling in anger. (6.5.35)
• Offensive: Offended Nárada Muni by cursing him for making his 11,000 sons brahmacáris.
• Fault-finder: He improperly thought Nárada Muni to be asddhu, or non-saintly, because
Nárada had foiled his intentions. (6.5.36 P)
^^^^^^^^^^^^^INDRA
Position
• Esteemed position: Had various great personalities in his assembly from whom he was
receiving respects and services. (6.7.2-8)
• Not a pure devotee: He anxiously waited for Vrtrásura to be killed and he was pleasing
the Lord only for matériái profit. (6.9.48 P)
• Faith in authority: Approached Lord Brahmá for proper instruction (6.7.19); followed the
orders of Brahmá and Visnu (6.7.26, 6.10.3); helieved in the Lord's protection. (6.9.27)
• Under the Lord's protection: Just to help Indra, Lord Visnu killed the two brothers
Hiranyáksa and Hiranyakasipu and took the form of Vámanadeva. (6.18.23, 5.24.18)
Character
•   Yoga-isah: Has all the mystic powers and could enter Diti's womh. (6.18.61)
•   Mindful: Indra revived Airávata with his nectar-producing hand. (6.11.11-12)
•   Self-interested: To hreak Diti's promise, he engaged in her service. (6.18.56)
•   Sattvic: Situated in mode of goodness. (6.7.12)
Repentance, Responsibility and Action
• Developed consciousness: Immediately understood the mistake of disrespecting guru. (6.7.10)
• Regretted immediately: Condemned himself in the presence of the assembly. (6.7.10)
• Repentance along with action: Vigorously searched for Brhaspati. (6.7.15,17)
• Release from sinful life: Relieved of all sinful reactions hy strictly worshipping. (6.13.17)
Accepting the good
• Praised good instruction even from an enemy: Hearing the straightforward, instructive
words of Vrtrásura, Indra praised Vrtrásura. (6.12.18)
• Appreciated the enemy's devotion: Glorified Vrtrásura for his devotion. (6.12.19-20)
Like other conditioned souls - affected by circumstance
• Envious: Became envious of King Rsabhadeva and stopped ponting. (5.4.3)
• Proud: Being extremely proud of matériái opulence he transgressed the law of Vedic
etiquette of respecting his spiritual master, Brhaspati. (6.7.2-8)
