1. Character Portrayals
• Afraid and angry: Out of circumstantial anger, hecame afraid of heing defeated hy the
demons, and in great anger at Visvarüpa he cut off his three heads. (6.9.4)
• Deceitful: Became outwardly friendly with Diti and served her in a faithful way. (6.18.58)
• Insecure and Self-conscious: Cut Diti's emhryo into 49 pieces. (6.18.62)
Instrumental in presenting useful lessons
• Extrémé opulence leads to degradation: True hoth individually and collectively. (6.7.12 P)
• Familiarity breeds contempt: One should he carefui in one's dealings with the ácárya.
(6.7.15 P)
• Námno bálád yasya hi pápa-buddhih: Intentionaiiy committing sins while chanting the
holy name is a most ahominahle offence. (6.13.8-9P)
• Purification by devotional service: Both Diti and Indra finally hecame happy with each
other, as hoth were engaged in the service of the Lord. (6.18.77)
VRTRASUEAÉ
Birth: Appeared from Tvasta's sacrifice, intended to kill Indra.
As a G r e a t Ksatriya Leader
• Leading from front: Lead the asuras in the hattle against the demigods. (6.10.17-18)
• Courage in battle: Did not leave the battle, rather he encouraged others to fight. (6.10.30)
• Maintainer of faith in dependants: Encouraged the fleeing army by explaining to them the
futility of temporary life and the glorious destination after death on the battlefield. (6.10.32-33)
• Chastiser of crooked activity: When the demigods attacked the fleeing demons from
hehind, he chastised them for their cowardly behaviour of not following ksatriya codes.
(6.11.4-5)
• Heroism: Though without an army, he fought alone and was glorified hy hoth sides. (6.11.8)
• Foiiower of ksatriya codes: Did not strike Indra when he was fatigued and injured.
(6.11.12)
As a Great Devotee
• Detached and prayerful: He did not desire heavenly opulences, hut desired the fricndship
and association of pure devotees like Nárada after death. (6.11.18)
• Faith in the Lord: Reminded Indra ahout the infallihle power of the thunderboit
empowered hy Lord Visnu. (6.11.19-20)
• Great preacher: Preached to Indra ahour the temporary nature of matériái happiness and
the ultimate control of the Supreme Lord to encourage him to fight. Even Indra was
astonished seeing his devotional qualities. (6.12.6-19)
• Destination: In his originál consciousness, he returned home, hack to Godhead, to become
an associate of Lord Sankarsana. (6.12.35 P)
Bhagavata Subodhini
JTRAKETU    IUAHA R A J A
Character
• Gratitude: Grateful to forefathers and Vaisnavas who enlighten others. (6.14.26)
• Hospitality: Respectful to saints and was exemplary in receiving Angira Rsi. (6.14.15-16)
• Partially dutiful: Desired to have a son to accomplish his prescrihed duties (6.14.26) hut
neglected his duty towards his childless wives, which resulted in their envy. (6.14.38)
• Proper spirit: Presented himself as a fool before the guru and requested enlightenment.
(6.15.16)
• Submissive: Perfectly executed the instructions of Nárada and attained the Lord. (6.16.30)
• Prayers: Offered prayers to the Lord giorifying the supreme position and bhdgavata-
dharma spoken hy the Lord. (6.16.34-49)
• Detached: Fixed in giorification of the Lord even in the midst of women. (6.17.2-3 P)
• Coucerned: To protect ordinary people in committing the offence of misjudging Lord Siva,
he corrected Lord Siva in friendly jokes. (6.17.9 P)
• Humble: Respectfully accepted the curse of Párvati without any retaliation. (6.17.16 P)
• Wise: Aware of the futility of benediction and curse, hell and heaven. (6.17.20)
• Glorified eveu by a Mahájaua: Lord Siva glorified him as a great Vaisnava. (6.17.28)
^ ^ ^ ^ ^ ^ ^ ^ ^ ^ ^      JÁRADA M U N I
Qualities
• Perfect guru: Perfectly instructed Priyavrata ahout the mission of humán life. (5.1.1 P)
• Preacher: He is engaged in distributing Bhdgavatam, for the benefit of all. (6.5.30 P)
• Intelligent: Advised Haryasvas intelligently using an allegory. (6.5.12 and 20, P)
• Perseverant: Never gave up his mission despite condemnation. (6.5.23 P)
• Expert: Very expert in delivering all the conditioned souls hack to Godhead. (6.5.24 P)
• Deliverer: Delivered Manu's entire family and tried his hest to deliver Daksa. (6.5.35 P)
• Toleraut: Did not retaliate the curse of Daksa. (6.5.44)
• Enlighteuer: Enlightened King Citraketu who hecame hopeful with knowledge. (6.15.9)
• Mahájaua: One of the 12 mahdjanas who are knowers of bhdgavata-dharma. (6.3.20)
• Author: He described how to achieve devotion in the Ndrada Pahcardtra. (5.19.10)
• Chauter: Glorifies Anantadeva with his own verses, accompanied hy the tumburu. (5.25.8)
• Merciful: Everyone engaged in deity worship, should seek the mercy of Nárada. (6.18.17)

2. Krsna's Names
RSNA's NAMES
Everything about the Supreme Lord is so special that His every action, -words, limbs,
qualities aud pastimes earu H i m various names which are personal aud nourishiug
when remembered by His devotees. The unlimited Lord can be addressed with
unlimited names by His unlimited devotees. I n these pages of Srimad-Bhagavatam
Cantos 5 and 6, the Lord is addressed by various names. These have been presented
below in three categories.
Note: Although somé of these names appear repeatedly in the Bhágavatam, in different
contexts, only one instance has been indicated here. No douht there are many more
meanings to all of these names, but for the particular meaning intended, the
corresponding verse numbers sérve as a reference.

3 . ANALOGIES
Compassion to all living entities by bringing them closer to Lord Sri Krsna is the pur-
pose of Srimad-Bhdgavatam. As a desperate teacher, who wants to make sure somehow
or other the message of Godhead is understood by the audience, the Bhdgavatam is
filled with analogies to helps us understand, identify and internalize the process of
bhakti through employing various analogies as listed from Cantos 5 and 6 below.
Note: The like terms of each analogy have been colour-coded for easy understanding.
ABOUT THE LORD
The Lord's svarüpa
1.   Just as the sun evaporates urine from the earth but is never affected by contamination;
similarly, the Supreme Lord is so powerful that nothing we might consider abominable
affects Him. (5.1.11 P)
2.   Just as a fire, although existing in one place can expand its Hght and heat everywhere;
similarly, the omnipotent Lord although situated in His spiritual abode expands Himself
in both the matériái and spiritual worlds by His various energies. (6.8.32-33 P)
The Lord's energies
3.   Just as an iron rod becomes fire when placed in contact with fire; similarly, the matériái
energy when engaged in the service of the Supreme Spirit is also transformed into spiritual
energy. (5.12.8 P)
4.   Exactly as a piece of iron moves under the influence of a iodestone (permanent magnet),
inert mattét moves by the glancé of the Lord. (5.18.38)
5.   Just as the nipples on the neck of a goat cannot give any milk; similarly, the Prakrti alone
cannot manifest creation being the secondary cause and only acts under the Lord.
(5.18.38 P)
6.   Just as heat and light, the energies of the sun, act within the universe and make everything
work; similarly, the energy of the Lord acts throughout the creation. (5.20.23 P)
7.   Just as a fire although situated in one place spreads illumination for a long distance; •
similarly, the Supreme Personality of Godhead although situated in His abode expands
everywhere through his various energies. (6.5.17 P)
The Lord's mercy
8.   Just as sometimes a small cliild eats dirty things and his parents take away the dirty things
and offer somc sweetmeat; similarly, sometimes devotees aspire for matériái benedictions
but the Lord kindly takes away their matériái desires and gives them the highest
bcncüictioí!. (5.19.27 P)
9.   Just as a person not inclined to die must nonetheless give up his iongevity, opulence, fame
and everything else at the time of death, similarly, if one receives mercy of the Supreme
Lord, he can gain all these at the appointed time of victory. (6.12.13)
10. Just as King Pariksit was burned by the brahmastra of Asvatthamá, but was saved when
Lord Krsna entered the womb of his mother; similarly, although the one emhryo was cut
into forty-nine pieces by the thunderboit of Indra, they were all saved by the mercy of the
Supreme Personality of Godhead. (6.18.65)
The Lord as the controller
11. Just as the bird is bound by the rope; all demigods including Brahmá and I myself (Lord
Siva) are controlled by the Supreme Personality of Godhead. (5.17.23)
12. Just as a puppeteer controls his dancing dolls and a husband controls his wife, Your
Lordship controls all the living entities in the universe, such as the brdhmanas, ksatriyas,
vaisyas and südras. (5.18.26)
13. Just as the birds are caught in a net and cannot move independently; similarly, aii living
beings in all the planets of this universe, including the presiding deities of all the planets,
are fully under the control of the Lord. (6.12.8)
14. Just as a wooden doll or an animál made of grass and leaves cannot move or dance
independently but depends fully on the person who handlés it; similarly, all living beings
dance according to the desire of the supreme controller, the Personality of Godhead.
(6.12.10)
The Lord as the creator, maintainer and destroyer
15. Just as a sporting elephant plucks a lotus flower from the water; similarly, the Lord, the
originál boar, lifted the earth from the Garbhodaka Ocean. (5.18.39)
16. Just as a boy at the beach creates something in which he is not interested, similarly the
Lord, keeping everything under His control, causes creation, maintenance and annihilation
although He is certainly not interested in the temporary cosmic manifestation. (6.15.6)
17. Just as pots made completely of earth are situated on earth after being created and are
transformed into earth again when broken; similarly, the cosmic manifestation is caused
by the Supreme Brahman, situated in the Supreme Brahman, and annihilated in the same
Supreme Brahman. (6.16.22)

3. Analogies
In reiatíon to the jiva
18. Just as a blind man is led by a person who has eyes with which to see; similarly, the jiva
must be conducted by the Supreme Personality of Godhead. (5.1.15)
19. Just as the air or vitai force is within the bodies of all beings; similarly, the Lord is present
within the hearts of all living entities. (5.11.14 P)
20. Just as aquatics always desire to remain in the vast mass of water, all conditioned living
entities naturally desire to remain in the vast existence of the Supreme Lord. (5.18.13)
21. Just as the crocodile can exhibit its strength only in water, the jiva can manifest his
spiritual power only in contact with the all-pervading Vásudeva. (5.18.13 P)
22. Just as the driver of a bnllock cart ties ropes through the nostrils of his bnlls to control
them, the Supreme Personality of Godhead binds all men through the ropes of His words
in the Vedas. (6.3.13)
23. Just as the different limbs of the body cannot see the eyes, the living entities cannot see
the Supreme Lord, who is situated as the Supersoui in everyone's heart. (6.3.16)
24. Just as the sense objects [form, taste, touch, smell and sound] cannot understand how
the senses perceive them; similarly, the conditioned soul, although residing in his hody
along with the Supersoui, cannot understand how the Supreme Spiritual Person, the
master of the matériái creation, directs his senses. (6.4.24)
25. Just as the various servants in the different departments of big estahlishments cannot see
the supreme managing director under whom they are working; similarly, the conditioned
sonls cannot see the supreme friend sitting within their bodies. (6.4.24 P)
26. Just as the water of a river is of the same nature as the water of the sea; similarly, a spirit
soul is equal in quality to the supreme soul and thus one should meditate upon the Supreme
Lord (6.7.12 P)
27. Just as a rope causes fear for a bewildered person who considers it a snake, hut not for a
,'ervon with proper iruellieen who knows it to he only a rope; similarly, the Lord, as
the Supersoui in everyone's heart, inspires fear in a non-devotee who regards Him as the
snake-like source of all fear, hut not in a w,,; who does not see contradictions in Him.
(6.9.37 P)
28. Just as when a person comes under the shadow of a big tree, the disturhances caused hy
the heat of the scorching sun are immediately mitigated; similarly, the jiva needs only to
seek shelter of the shade of the Lord's lotus feet, then all the matériái tribulations that
disturb him will he suhdued. (6.9.43 P)
29. Just as the elements of the matériái body are eternal; similarly, the living entity who
appears through these matériái elements is also eternal. (6.15.7)
30. Just as a court judge is neutral when two opposing parties appear before him, but still
takes action as the case warrants; similarly, the Lord sits in the core of everyone's heart as •
the neutral witness, hut still He takes action for the good and bad activities of a jiva.
(6.16.11 P)
31. Just as iron cannot biirn unless beated by fire; similarly, the bodily senses cannot act
unless favoured hy (electrified hy) the Supreme Brahman. (6.16.24)
. 32. In the presence of the sun there is nothing to he revealed hy the light of a glow-worm;
similarly, in the presence of the Lord there is nothing to he revealed hy the insignificant
jiva. (6.16.46)
33. Just as the government is neutral and ultimate supreme authority, the justice is
administered hy the departments of the government, and the government is not
responsible for the individual judgments; similarly, the Supreme Lord is neutral to
everyone, and for the maintenance of law and order His supreme government has various
departments, which control the activities of the living entities. (6.17.23 P)
34. Just as lilies open or close because of the sunshine, and thus the bumblebees enjoy or
suffer, hut the sunshine and the sun glohe are not responsible for the happiness and distress
of the bumblebees; similarly, the Supreme Lord is neutral to everyone, hut for the
maintenance of law and order His supreme government has various departments, which
control the activities of the living entities (6.17.23 P)
In reiatíon to the devotee
35. Just as when a bumblebee enters the opening of a lotus flower and drinks its honey, it is
fully protected hy the petals of the lotus; similarly, a person seeking shelter at the lotus
feet of the Personality of Godhead is protected from all dangers. (5.1.19 P)
36. By manipulating a fire-generating stick, great sages can bring forth the fire lying dormant
within wood; similarly, those expert in understanding the Absolute Truth try to see the
Lord in everything—even in their own bodies. (5.18.36)
37. If a vulture curses a cow to die, the curse will not he effective. Similarly, accusations made
hy demoniac persons against devotees of Krsna cannot have any effect. (6.10.28 P)
In reiatíon to the demigods
38. Just as we massage a person's legs, we do not really sérve the legs hut the person who
possesses the legs; similarly, if we offer service to the demigods who are different parts of
the Lord, we actually sérve the Lord Himself. (5.7.6 P)
39. Just as a government officer is sometimes accepted as the entire government although he
is actually hut a departmentai manager, similarly, the demigods, having achieved power of
attorney from Visnu, act on His hehalf, although they are not as powerful as He.
(5.20.3-4 P)
40. Just as tax collectors collect revenue and bring it to the government treasury; similarly,
the demigods carry the sacrificial offerings to the Lord (5.20.17 P)
41. Just as one central emperor controls different departmentai rulers (kings), there must he
one supreme controller (Lord Visnu) to guide all the judges or rulers (Yamarájas) who
are engaged in giving justice to different karmís or workers. (6.3.6)
!. Analogies                          29
42. When insignificant persons use nugh words to cast falsé, angry accusations against
saintly persons, their fruitless words do not disturb the great personalities; similarly, all
the efforts of the demons against the demigods, who were favourahly situated under the
protection of Krsna, were futile. (6.10.28)
43. Just as a fog is vanquished hy the brilliant sunrise; similarly, the reactions to gravely
sinful acts of Indra were nullified hy worshipping the Supreme Personality of Godhead
in the horse sacrifice. (6.13.20)
The Lord's weapons
44. As a blazing fire hurns dry grass to ashes with the assistance of the hreeze, may that
Sudarsana cakra hurn our enemies to ashes. (6.8.23)
•                         A B O U T THE PROCESS OF BHAKTI-YOGA
The process of bhakti
45. Just as if a venomous serpent is hereft of its poison fangs, it is no longer fearful; similarly,
if the senses are engaged in the service of the Lord, there is no need to fear their activities.
(5.1.17 P)
46. Just as an ordinary man always engages his mind in matériái activities; similarly, a
devotee always engages his mind in spiritual activities. (5.1.6 P)
The glories of the holy names
47. Just as when a lion roars, all the small animals flee in fear; similarly, when the holy name of
the Lord is chanted in any condition all the reactions of one's sinful life depart. (6.2.7 P)
48. just as a fire hurns dry grass to ashes; similarly, the holy name of the Lord, whether
chanted knowingly or unknowingly, harns to ashes all the reactions of one's sinful
activities. (6.2.18)
49. Just as, if a person unaware of the potency of a medicine takes that medicine or is forced
to take it, it will act even without his knowledge; similarly, if a person unaware of the
value of chanting the holy name of the Lord chants knowingly or unknowingly, the
chanting will he equally effective. (6.2.19)
Offences in bhakti
50. Just as when a mad elephant enters a heautiful garden, it destroys everything, leaving a
barren field; similarly, the vaisnava-aparádha is so dangerous that even an advanced
devotee becomes almost devoid of his spiritual assets if he commits it. (5.1.5 P)
51. Just as a sane person should not find faults with the sun for evaporating urine from the .
Street; similarly, an ordinary man cannot criticize the most powerful even for seemingly
wrong behaviour. (6.17.10 P)
Q           Bhagavataluboa^
The qualities and nature of bhakti
52. Just as the sun can immediately dissipate fog by its rays; similarly, only pure bhakti can
uproot the weeds of sinful actions with no possibility of any revival. (6.1.15)
53. O lotus-eyed Lord, as baby birds that have not yet developed their wings always look for
their mother to return and feed them, as small calves tied with ropes await anxiously the
time of milking, when they will he allowed to drink the milk of their mothers, or as a
morose wife whose husband is away from home always longs for husband to return and
satisfy her in all respects, I (Vrtrásura) always yearn for the opportunity to rendet direct
service unto You (the Lord). (6.11.26)
54. Just as seeds when sterilized or fried do not produce plants; similarly, when materially
obsessed persons worship the Lord, they are not subject to matériái rebirth. (6.16.39)
Importance of mind control
55. After capturing animals, a cunning hunter does not put faith in them, for they might run
away; similarly, those who are advanced in spiritual life do not put faith in the mind.
(5.6.2)
56. Just as an unchaste woman is very easily carried away by paramours, and it sometimes
happens that her husband is violently killed hy her paramours; similarly, if the yogi gives
his mind a chance and does not restrain it, his mind will give facility to enemies like lust,
anger and greed, and they will douhtlessly kill the yogi. (5.6.4)
Instructions on the process of bhakti
57. Just as, if a person leads a blind man to a dark well, the latter will surely fali in; similarly,
if a spiritual master, father or king puts his disciple, son or citizen into the bondage of
karmic activity, then the latter won't he henefitted spiritually. (5.5.15)
58. Just as a person may dream that he has become a king with a good family, wife and
children, hut immediately at the end of that dream he comes to the conclusion that it is
falsé; similarly, sometimes a sádhaka may desire for all kinds of matériái happiness which
are undesirahle, hut then realizes his goal is spiritual salvation. (5.11.3 P)
59. Just as the lamp with a charred wick displays a smoky flame and the lamp with a fresh
wick displays a bright flame; similarly, the mind attached to the gunas is the cause of the
jiva's suffering and the mind detached from the gunas is the cause of i n a s l i b e r i U n v i .
(5.11.8)
60. Just as medicine saves one from a snakebite; similarly, the Vedic instructions save the
conditioned soul from the scorching heat of suffering. (5.11.2 P)
Nn.iioeic
A B O U T SAINTLY PERSONALITIES
Nature and characteristics
61. Just as a father sacrifices many things out of affection for his son; similarly, great saintly
persons sacrifice all kinds of bodily comforts for the benefit of humán society. (5.1.26 P)
62. Just as the splendour of a valuable gem is covered hy dirt; similarly, the spiritual efful-
gence and knowledge of Jada Bharata was covered hy the dirt on his hody. (5.9.9-10)
63. Just as great learned brahmanas can extract the fire dormant within wood hy chanting
the fifteen Samidheni mantras; similarly, Krsna conscious persons can find the Supersoui,
who hy His own spiritual potency is situated within the heart. (6.4.27-28)
64. Just as a millionaire may very easily possess hundreds and thousands of dollars; similarly,
Vrtrásura was a perfect Vaisnava, and therefore he was also a brahmana. (6.13.3 P)
ABOUT T ^ J Ü ' A
Bound by k a r m a
65. Just as a sleeping person acts according to the body manifested in his dreams and accepts
it to be himself; similarly, the jiva identifies with his present body and is unable to know
his past or future lives. (6.1.49)
66. Just as a person struggling in the ocean must swim through it alone; similarly, the jiva has
a body with which he has to struggle alone in this ocean of matériái world. (6.1.50 P)
67. Just as a silkworm uses its own saliva to create a cocoon and then becomes trapped in
it, with no possibility of getting out; similarly, the foolish jiva, unable to control his senses
and mind, is forced to act according to the influence of the modes of matériái nature
and then becomes trapped against his desires. (6.1.52)
68. Just as small particles of sand sometimes come together and are sometimes separated due
to the force of the waves; similarly, jivas sometimes come together and are sometimes
separated by the force of time. (6.15.3)
69. Just as gold and other commodities are continually transferred from one place to another
in due course of purchase and sale; similarly, the jiva as a result of his fruitive activities,
wanders throughout the entire universe, being injected into various bodies in different
species of life by one kind o£ father after another. (6.16.6)
Family life
70. Just as a king in his fortress conquers his powerful enemies; similarly, the householder
systematically conquers his mind and five sense organs. (5.1.18)
71. Just as tigers, jackals and other ferocious animals in a forest are ready to take away a •
lamb from the custody of its protector; similarly, the wife and children enter the heart of
the merchant and plunder him in so many ways. (5.13.2)
m          Bhagavata   SubodhinTp^|3J^|^j|||3^
72. Just as a person seeking honey trom a beehive is              by the bees; similarly, the
conditioned soul, seeking a little insignificant sex enjoyment searches after debauchee
women and is litsuiícil auci duistn: by the women's kinsmen. (5.13.10)
73. Just as a herdsman tries to protect his sheep to the best of his ability, but the tigers and
foxes take them away by force; similarly, although a miserly man wants to guard his
money very carefully, his family members take away all his assets forcibly, even though
he is very vigilant. (5.14.3)
74. Although the plowman plows his fields every year, seeds of weeds remain and again come
up with the plants sown in the field; also, although camphor may be removed from a
pot, the pot nonetheless retains the aroma of camphor; similarly, as long as the seeds of
desire to enjoy family life are not completely burned out, fruitive activities are not
destroyed. (5.14.4)
75. Just as a person aggrieved by hunger and thirst is not pleased by the externai
gratification of flower garlands or sandalwood pulp; similarly, because I (King
Citraketu) have no son, I am not pleased with my empire, opulence or possessions, which
are desirable even by great demigods. (6.14.25)
76. Just as a person who has no mother at home and whose wife does not speak sweetly
should go to the forest; similarly, for a woman who has no son, who is not cared for by
her husband and whose co-wives neglect her, going to the forest is better than to remain
at home. (6.14.40 P)
77. Just as when seeds are sown in the ground, they sometimes grow into plants and
sometimes do not; similarly, sometimes a prospective father, being impelled by the
potency of the Supreme Lord, can béget a child, but sometimes conception does not take
place. (6.15.4)
78. Just as an elephant becomes free from a muddy reservoir of water; similarly, King
Citraketu came out of the dark well of family life. (6.16.15)
Without Krsna consciousness
79. Just as a man suffering from cold in the forest runs after a phosphorescent light in a
marshy land, considering it to be fire; similarly, those whose minds are overcome by the
mode of passión are attracted by the color of gold, considering it to be of real value
(5.14.7)
80. Just as liberated forest animals and birds that are careless are again bound by a hunter;
similarly, if one obtains a humán body in the land of Bhárata-varsa but still does not
take to devotional service he is certainly again entangled in samsara. (5.19.25)
81. Just as a pot containing liquor cannot be purified even if washed in the waters of many
rivers; similarly, nondevotees cannot be purified by processes of atonement even if they
perform them very perfectly. (6.1.18)

3. Analogk"
82. Just as prostitute changes dresses to attract one's attention; similarly, the unsteady
intelligence of every living entity when mixed with mode of passión distracts the soul's
attention from the Lord. (6.5.14)
83. Just as, if a person becomes the husband of a prostitute, he loses all independence; similarly,
if a living entity has polluted intelligence, he prolongs his materialistic life. (6.5.15)
Bodily conditioning
84. Just as a dream that we see at night is certainly falsé, but a horrible dream certainly affects
the person seeing it; similarly, the soul's fatigue is not factual, but as long as one is immersed
in the illusory bodily conception, one is affected by such falsé dreams. (5.10.21 P)
85. Just as one may put a pot of milk and rice within             , and the milk and rice are
automatically heated one after the other, similarly, due to bodily p a i n s a n d p l e a s u r e s , the
senses, mind and soul are affected. (5.10.22)
86. Just as a person sitting in his car is certainly different from his car, but if there is damage
to the car, being overly attached to the car, he                  !s p a i " , similarly, the soul is different
from the body and the senses, but due to ignorance, he identifies himself with the body,
and he ieels p l e a s u r e a n d p a i n d i i c to bodily pleasure a n d pain. (5.10.22 P)
87. Just as a person needs different dresses during the summer and winter; similarly,
conditioned souls change bodies according to their desires. (6.16.19 P)
Attachments
88. Just as, if a poor man loses somé money or gold, he at once becomes very agitated;
similarly, the mind of Mahárája Bharata would become agitated when he did not see the
deer. (5.8.15 P)
89. Just as a deer chases a mirage in the desert; similarly, a conditioned soul being overly
attached chases after the objects of the senses. (5.14.6)
90. Just as when a poor man gets somé money after great difficulty, his affection for the
money increases daily; similarly, when King Citraketu, after great difficulty, received a
son, his affection for the son increased day after day, (6.14.36)
A B O U T THE MATERIAL W O R L D
Nature
91. Just as straws floating on the surface of the ocean are gathered and separated by the
waves of ocean; similarly, all living entities are gathered and separated in the material
world by the waves of the ocean of nescience. (5.10.12 P)
92. Just as, if one falls into a mine of salt, he tastes only salt wherever he goes; similarly, this .
material world is full of miseries, thus the so-called temporary happiness is also misery
only. (6.17.20 P)
93. Just as a person mistakenly considers a flower garland to be a snake; similarly, in the
material world, by a lack of carefui consideration, the conditioned soul differentiates
between happiness and distress, considering one good and the other bad. (6.17.30)
In reiatíon to the demigods
94. Just as when a king is no longer in power the nobility in the society vanishes; similarly,
with the destruction of the entire universe, including the demigods, the benedictions
received from the demigods also vanish. (6.16.38)
Karma-kánda
95. Just as a person attempting to climb a hill endures the pain of piercing thorns and
pebbles; similarly, a person desiring to cross over ritualistic ceremonies certainly feels
pain only and thus suffers unlimitedly. (5.14.18)
96. Just as an elephant cleanses itself by taking a full bath, but then throws dust over its head
and body as soon as it returns to the land; similarly, a person may atone for the past sinful
acts very carefully, but may again commit sinful acts. (6.1.10)
97. Just as an experienced physician never encourages a patient to eat food injurious to his
health, even if the patient desires it; similarly, a pure devotee fully accomplished in the
science of devotional service will never instruct a foolish person to engage in fruitive
activities for material enjoyment, not to speak of helping him in such activities. (6.9.50)
Jnána-kánda
98. Just as, if a diseased person eats the pure, uncontaminated food prescrihed by a physician,
he is gradually cured, and the infection of disease can no longer touch him; similarly, if a
conditioned soul follows the regulative principles of knowledge, he gradually progresses
toward liberation from material contamination. (6.1.12)
99. Although the dried leaves of creepers beneath a bamboo tree are burned by fire, they
grow up again as their roots are not fully destroyed; similarly, the sins destroyed by
following the limbs of jnána are not fully destroyed with its entirety. (6.1.13-14)
VARNÁSRAMA-DHARMA
IIliiii-iiimiiiiniiii       .iÉtiniiniiiiiifiin I I ' J ^ I I I M — , H , n —Mag—irttiJi• i IÉ
100. Just as bulls are obliged to move according to the direction of a driver puliing on ropes
knotted to their noses; similarly, all living entities are bound by the Vedic injunctions
to the divisions of varnásrama according to our qualities and work. (5.1.14 P)
101. Just as a cow delivers milk in the presence of her calf; similarly, the earth fulfilled the
desires of Mahárája Gaya, who was able to utilize all the resources of the earth to benefit
his citizens. (5.15.10 P)
102. Just as the father and mother are the friends and maintainers of their children, as the
eyelid is the protector of the eye, as the husband is the maintainer and protector of a
woman, as the householder is the maintainer and protector of beggars, and as the learned
is the friend of the ignorant; similarly, the king is the protector and giver of life to all his
subjects. (6.4.12)
103. Just as the living entity is happy when the seven properties of material nature [the totál
material energy, the ego and the five objects of sense gratification] are in proper order;
similarly, a king is always protected by seven elements - his guru, his ministers, his
kingdom, his fort, his treasury, his royal order and his friends. (6.14.17)
fttHH                                          WARFARE
104. As the stars in the sky cannot be seen when covered by dense clouds; similarly, the
demigods, being completely covered by networks of arrows falling upon them one after
another, could not be seen. (6.10.24)
105. Just as a mad elephant tramples hoUow bamboos in the forest; similarly, Vrtrásura
trampled the demigods beneath his feet on the battlefield (6.11.8)
106. Just as a mountain is struck by a thunderboit; the elephant Airávata, felt great pain and
spat blood when struck by the club of Vrtrásura, (6.11.11)
107. Just as a request for money from a miser is completely useless, your (demigods) attack
upon me (Vrtrásura) with your club was certainly useless. (6.11.19)
108. Just as Kaitabha had forcefuily attacked the Supreme Personality of Godhead when the
universe was inundated; similarly, Vrtrásura vigorously took up his trident and with great
force attacked Lord Indra, the King of heaven. (6.12.1)

110. Just as a big python might swallow an elephant; Vrtrásura came before Indra and
swallowed him and Airávata, his carrier. (6.12.29)
VEDIC COSMOLOGY                                                   j
When a potter's wheel is moving and small ants located on that big wheel are moving with
it, one can see that their motion is different from that of the wheel because they appear
sometimes on one part of the wheel and sometimes on another. Similarly, the signs and
constellations, with Sumeru and Dhruvaloka on their right, move with the wheel of time,
and the antlike sun and other planets move with them. The sun and planets, however, are
seen in different signs and constellations at different times. (5.22.2)
When bulls are yoked together and tied to a central post to thresh rice, they tread around
that pivot without deviating from their proper positions—one hull being closest to the
post, another in the middle, and a third on the outside. Similarly, all the planets and all
Bbagavala Subodhini

the hundreds and thousands of stars revolve around the polestar, the planet of Mahárája
Dhruva, in their respective orbits, somé higher and somé lower. (5.23.3)
GENERAL W I S D O M
113. Just as within a garden a flowering tree attains a good reputation for the garden because
of its fragrant flowers; similarly, within a family a famous man attains a good reputation
and recognition for the family because of his good qualities. (5.15.16 P)
114. Just as, if a grain of wheat is divided into two parts, one can estimate the size of the upper
part by knowing that of the lower part; similarly, expert geographers instruct, if a
universe is divided into two parts, one can understand the measurements of the upper part
of the universe by knowing those of the lower part of the universe. (5.21.2)
115. As an expert physician diagnoses and treats a disease according to its gravity, one should
undergo atonement according to the severity of one's sins. (6.1.8)
116. Just as springtime in the present indicates the nature of springtime's in the past and
future, so this life of happiness, distress or a mixture of both gives evidence concerning
- the religious and irreligious activities of one's past and future lives. (6.1.47)
117. Just as an ignorant animál sleeps in peace with its head on the lap of its master, faithfully
believing in the master's protection; similarly, the innocent, unenlightened citizen fully
surrenders in good faith and fricndship to its leader. (6.2.5-6)
118. Just as a razor is meant to shave the hair from one's face, but if not carefully handled,
can cause disaster; similarly, one should be extremely carefui to utilize the span of his life
for spiritual realization, or Krsna consciousness and not to create a disaster by misusing
his lifetime. (6.5.19 P)
119. Just as a stone boát would be unable to float and would sink in the water with its
passengers; similarly, those who mislead people go to hell, and their followers go with
them. (6.7.14)
120. Just as a beggar should not ask charity from a person who is in difficulty; similarly, one
who is able to give charity should not deny a beggar. (6.10.6 P)
121. Just as a blind eye simply gives pain but cannot help one see; similarly, a son who is
neither glorious nor devoted to the Lord gives pain and is useless. (6.11.4 P)
122. Just as a cloud indiscriminately pours water on the earth; similarly, the beneficent King
Citraketu to increase the reputation, opulence and Iongevity of his son, distributed all
desirable things to his citizens. (6.14.35)
123. As the hunter of a deer becomes like a deer by coveríng his body with deerskin and
serving the deer, so Indra, although at heart t h e enemy of the sons of Diti, became
outwardly friendly and served Diti in a faithful way. (6.18.58)
